- `#` title. For core chapter files this is the chapter/part heading itself (e.g. `# PREAMBLE / FOUNDATIONAL REQUIREMENTS`, `# CHAPTER SIX: FOUNDATIONAL RIGHTS`). Chapter Five band/aim files keep their descriptive `#` band title. The cross-chapter vignette file `core_10.005_application_vignettes.md` keeps a descriptive `#` title.
- Optional `<a id="chapter-…"></a>` anchors immediately above the `#` title carry the chapter/part anchors; an optional `*Non-operative subtitle:*` line may sit directly beneath the title.
- On implementation `*_00_registry_and_reading_rules.md` files only: one-line `*In plain terms:*` registry-annex gloss (not a second front door; see **NAV-IMPL-LANDING-01**).
- In this order: the placement widget; any file- or part-level reader-guidance widget; the chapter-opening **Trace** widget, if there is one; the **binding constitutional-owner opening line**, if the file is an owner-layer chapter (see **OWNER-OPENING-01**); then `<br>`. Any introduction, the `*In plain terms:*` gloss, and the binding text follow. No `---` rule sits between the title and the widgets.

**Inside the placement widget (non-operative):**

- `core_*` — binding-together notice, which chapter/part/band the file holds, README reading-order pointer, and file-sequence navigation (**Next**, **Upstream**, **Previous**) when present. A `**Constitutional owner:**` bullet may appear here or in the reader-guidance widget to help navigation; it does not bind. The **binding** owner claim still appears in the main text, after the opening widgets and any chapter-opening Trace (**OWNER-OPENING-01**).
- `*_00_registry` — edition and effective date, core vs implementation status, four-layer map (**CJS** / **CS** / **CI** / **CF**), navigation wrapper / reader-landing link, **CJS-1.3** pointer, and routing-anchor indexes previously split across multiple reader-guidance widgets.

**Do not keep visible at file top:** **Application baseline**, upstream inheritance boilerplate, or pipeline routing that duplicates the Corpus placement widget, chapter reader-guidance widgets, or [README.md](README.md). Chapter-specific scope boundaries and anti-substitution notes belong in the Corpus placement widget (file-level) or in chapter reader-guidance widgets.

### Implementation-corpus landing pages (NAV-IMPL-LANDING-01)

The four root wrappers are the **only** human landings for their layers:

| Wrapper | Layer | Default next |
|---|---|---|
| [corpus_joint_structure.md](corpus_joint_structure.md) | **CJS** | `cjs_reader_guide.md` (non-operative how-to-read), then `cjs_01_*` |
| [corpus_systems.md](corpus_systems.md) | **CS** | `cs_01_*` |
| [corpus_institutions.md](corpus_institutions.md) | **CI** | `ci_01_*` |
| [corpus_forum.md](corpus_forum.md) | **CF** | `cf_01_*` |

Each `*_00_registry_and_reading_rules.md` is a **registry annex** (identifier rules, reserved-family notes, optional routing detail) — not a second front door and **not** a second table of contents. The human family index is generated onto the four wrappers from [tools/architecture/family_map.json](tools/architecture/family_map.json) (`make family-map-indexes` / `make family-map-audit`). Default sequential reading is **wrapper → `*-1` scope file**. **CJS** inserts a non-operative [joint-structure reader guide](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_reader_guide.md) between the wrapper and `cjs_01_*`; that file is how-to-read only and is not a second landing. Footer audit treats `*_00` as side-path annexes (`tools/footer_audit.py`). **CI-2** and **CF-2** are reserved family IDs (routing lives in the annex); do not renumber later families to close those holes.

**Visible stack (in this order):**

1. `#` plain-language title (not editor jargon alone).
2. One-line `*In plain terms:*` gloss.
3. **Scope:** conceptual lifecycle bullets (what this layer is for) — not a reprint of the section-family index. End with one meta bullet pointing to the collapsed index for file-level detail.
4. **Does not:** boundary bullets (what this layer refuses to own or redefine).
5. **Implements from the core files:** short linked list of core chapters/articles this layer operationalizes (prefer ascending chapter order).
6. **Siblings:** Core [README](README.md) first, then peer domain wrappers, then joint structure (or, on the CJS landing, the three domain wrappers plus the topic-router reader index).
7. **Binding:** one plain statement — when incorporated under [Chapter Eighteen](core_18.000_incorporation.md), these rules bind as implementation detail; they must satisfy the Sentient Constitution and must not override or narrow it.
8. **Already know your topic?** pointer to the collapsed index and/or registry annex.

**Collapsed (non-operative) widgets, after the visible stack:**

- **Compatibility and authority** — edition alignment, compatibility entrypoint, authority note.
- **Layer index** — grouped stable family → authoritative subfile table generated from `family_map.json` (the machine-oriented TOC). Groups are reader maps (taxonomy vs protocols; machinery vs domain interfaces), not new corpora.

**Closing (immediately before the navigation footer):**

9. **What to do now:** continue to the next file for this layer’s boundary (what it owns here vs what remains in the Constitution or other implementation folders). **CJS** may add that most readers need later sections only when cited — not the folder front to back.
10. `---` then **`Next file:`** to the layer’s `*-1` scope file. **CJS** points **Next file:** at [cjs_reader_guide.md](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_reader_guide.md); that guide then points at `cjs_01_*`.

**Do not** leave compatibility notes, authority boilerplate, edition stamps, or the full family index visible above the orientation stack. **Do not** treat `*_00` as the default next hop from the wrapper.

**Single sources:** global edition and reading order in [README.md](README.md); implementation shared contract in **CJS-1.3**; section-family registries remain in `*_00` annex files; human landings remain the four wrappers under this rule.

### Implementation scope/boundary pages (NAV-IMPL-SCOPE-01)

Each layer’s first substantive file (`cjs_01_*`, `cs_01_*`, `ci_01_*`, `cf_01_*`) is the **scope and boundary** page: what that layer owns, what it does not, and where to continue. Default sequential reading is **wrapper → `*-1` → later family files**. **CJS** default sequential reading is **wrapper → reader guide → `cjs_01_*` → later family files**.

**Visible stack (in this order):**

1. `## XX-1: Scope, purpose, and boundary interface` (or the layer’s established `*-1` title).
2. Collapsed **Trace**, then collapsed **Definitions · Assessment · Compliance**.
3. One-line `*In plain terms:*` — what this layer is for.
4. **What this layer owns** — bullets naming the layer’s operative homes.
5. **What this layer does not own** — bullets naming the correct home after an em dash (core chapters, sibling layers, or **CJS** as applicable).
6. **Read next** — short pointers to the next substantive file, the `*_00` registry annex if needed, and any CJS-owned shared contract / reading-order homes. Prefer a collapsed **Reader guidance (non-operative): Read next** widget when the list is orientation-only (see **NAV-READER-06**); keep it as visible bullets only when a short binding continue-here cue is still needed.

**Subsections under `*-1`:**

- Add `### XX-1.n` only for **layer-unique** elaboration that does not belong in the owns / does-not-own lists.
- **CJS-only** substance that other layers must cite — purpose of the joint layer / implementation-corpus gateway (**CJS-1.0**), identifier rules including **oDef** / **Def** and the constitutional-vs-joint-operational-definition distinction (**CJS-1.1**), shared contract (**CJS-1.3**), and parse mechanics for **oDef** (**CJS-1.13**–**CJS-1.14**) — lives in **CJS-1** (and its subsections). Parse mechanics live in [cjs_05.140_odef_parse_mechanics.md](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_05.140_odef_parse_mechanics.md); drafting contracts (**CJS-1.7**–**CJS-1.8.1**) live in [cjs_18.105_drafting_contracts.md](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_18.105_drafting_contracts.md). **CS** / **CI** / **CF** point to those homes; they do not restate them. Applicability, combined satisfaction, the default **CJS → CS → CI → CF** reading stack, and the [Fullest Protective Effect](core_05.045_band_integrative.md#fullest-protective-effect) rule among jointly applicable incorporated obligations live in **Chapter One §15.4.4** and **§8.4.3**; CJS cites those homes and does not restate them. Non-operative how-to-read for average humans lives in [cjs_reader_guide.md](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_reader_guide.md) only.
- Do **not** restate owns / does-not-own lists inside a `*-1.1` subsection when the file-level lists already state them.

**Placement rules:**

| Content | Home |
|---|---|
| What the layer owns / does not own | File-level lists on that layer’s `*-1` |
| Purpose of the joint layer / implementation-corpus gateway | **CJS-1.0** only |
| Default cross-layer reading stack | **Chapter One §15.4.4** only |
| Constitutional vs joint operational definitions | **CJS-1.1** only (other layers may keep one short pointer bullet) |
| Shared implementation-corpus contract | **CJS-1.3** only |
| Applicability, combined satisfaction, and the [Fullest Protective Effect](core_05.045_band_integrative.md#fullest-protective-effect) rule for jointly applicable incorporated obligations | **Chapter One §15.4.4** / **§8.4.3** (CJS-0.1 identifies which files the facts require) |
| Identifier / label rules | **CJS-1.1** + each layer’s `*_00` registry annex (**oDef.*n*** = **CJS-3.*n***; Chapter Five **Def.*** separate) |
| Parse mechanics (guidepost **What it is** / **How to measure and assess** / **What must hold**; **oDef** clusters) | **CJS-1.13**–**CJS-1.14** in [cjs_05.140_odef_parse_mechanics.md](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_05.140_odef_parse_mechanics.md) |
| Specialty classification examples | Owner taxonomy file (for example **CS-3**), not the `*-1` boundary page |

**Trace Upstream** for `*-1` boundary pages should cite the joint-application home (**Chapter One §15.4.4**), the identifier / **Def.*** / **oDef** home (**CJS-1.1**), or the relevant core chapter — not **CJS-1.3** unless the subsection is itself the shared-contract owner.

**Do not** keep a second **Quick orientation** that restates owns / does-not-own or reprints apply-CJS boilerplate already covered by **Read next** and **CJS-1.3**.

**Reference shapes:** [CS-1](corpus_systems/cs_00.310_scope_purpose_identifier_rules.md); [CJS-1](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_00.110_scope_purpose_boundary_interface.md); [CI-1](corpus_institutions/ci_00.210_scope_purpose_legitimacy_interface.md); [CF-1](corpus_forum/cf_00.410_scope_authority_boundary_rules.md).

### Adopted implementation filenames (NAV-IMPL-FILENAME-01)

Numbered implementation subfiles are chapter labels. A reader scanning `corpus_joint_structure/` must be able to tell which files are one chapter and which are not.

**Share a numeric prefix only as parts of the same chapter:**

| Shape | Role | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| `layer_NN_*.md` | Unique chapter home | `cjs_18.110_specific_joint_interlocks.md`, `cjs_05.105_cross_implementation_operational_terms.md` |
| `layer_NNx_*.md` | Extra same-chapter file (glued letter) | `cjs_05.110_oversight_operations.md`, `cjs_05.115_audit_process.md` |
| `layer_NN_a_*.md` | Extra same-chapter file (CS part letter) | `cs_06.305_information_types_and_handling.md`, `cs_06.310_data_classifications.md` |

**Do not** give two undifferenced files the same number (`cjs_02_foo.md` and `cjs_02_bar.md`). **Do not** reuse a chapter number for a different family (`CJS-1.9` is not `cjs_02_*`). Family `01` is the unique scope/boundary page for that layer — no `cjs_01_*` adopted implementation files. Stable section IDs (**CJS-2.1**, **CJS-1.9**) stay put when a file is renamed.

Gate: `make adopted-implementation-filename-audit` (in `make regression`).

### Pre-release fragment ids (NAV-PRE-RELEASE-FRAGMENT-01)

This corpus is **pre-release**. Keep one current fragment id per heading. When a section is dropped or renumbered, retarget every live citation and delete the old `<a id>` — do not leave redirect or fossil anchors. Dated `archive/` and `evaluation/results/` files remain historical record.

Gate: `make fossil-anchor-audit` (in `make regression`).

### Chart sync on renumbering (VIS-CHART-SYNC-03)

Mermaid chart labels are plain text, so link and anchor audits do not catch a chart that has drifted from the headings it summarizes. Whenever an Article or subarticle is renumbered, renamed, added, moved, split, or removed, check every reader-facing Mermaid chart that lists Articles and update it in the same change:

- **Where the charts live:** the Part overview charts at the top of each Chapter Six file (`core_06.000_rights_part_a.md` through `core_06.015_rights_part_d.md`), the chapter-wide Part map in `core_06.000_rights_part_a.md`, and their copies in `guides/CONCEPTUAL_OVERVIEW.md`. Search for other charts with `grep -rn "Article" --include=*.md` inside ` ```mermaid ` blocks.
- **What to match:** each node's Article number and title, its bullet list against the current subarticle headings, row labels such as `Articles VI–VII`, and Part ranges such as `Articles VI–XII`.
- **Layout:** when the number of Articles in a Part changes, rebuild the row grid (and any invisible-link chain that positions nodes) rather than just relabeling, so no Article is dropped from the chart.
- **Translations:** localized charts follow the translation re-sync, not the English change.

Gate: manual. Charts must also keep **VIS-CHART-READABILITY-01** and **VIS-CHART-THEME-02**.

### Chapter Nine process order (CH9-PROCESS-ORDER-01)

**Decision (2026-10-07, before the pre-release announcement).** Chapter Nine sections are numbered in the order the certification work is done, in six phases (five when first reordered; the Record phase was moved after Decide and an Operate phase added the same day; a third move on 2026-10-08 changed what sits in Frame and Operate, described below). The numbers were moved before the announcement, not after, because once outside readers cite sections, a renumbering costs every citation (NAV-PRE-RELEASE-FRAGMENT-01 forbids redirect anchors, so a later move could not be softened). Only order, labels, and links changed. No obligation was added, dropped, or reworded; a clause-level inventory (`tools/obligation_inventory_diff.py`) shows the same clauses before and after.

| Phase | Sections | File |
|-------|----------|------|
| I. Frame | §2 System Class Evaluation | Part A |
| II. Evaluate | **IIa, always:** §3 Whole-System Certification Evaluation (§3.1–§3.4); **IIb, when implicated:** §4 Evaluations Where Implicated (§4.1–§4.3); **IIc, when triggered:** §5 Rights-Floor and Domain Evaluations (§5.1–§5.6) | Part A |
| III. Review | §6 Forum Process | Part B |
| IV. Decide | §7 Certification Outcomes (includes the go-live gate) | Part B |
| V. Record | §8 System Certification Record | Part B |
| VI. Operate | **VIa, operate and recertify:** §9 Recertification and Reopening (§9.1 Challenging a Certification, with §9.1.1 Contestability paths; §9.2 Provisional and full certification; §9.3 Non-evasion); **VIb, standing bridge:** §10 Relationship to Standing | Part B |

§1 Purpose and Role precedes the phases. Numbering stays continuous across Part A and Part B; Part C keeps the Part A numbers it illustrates.

**Old to new (first move, from the original numbering).** Part A: 3 → 4, 3.1 → 4.1, 3.5 → 4.2, 3.6 → 4.3, 3.7 → 4.4, 3.2 → 4.5, 3.3 → 4.6, 3.4 → 4.7, 3.4.1 → 4.7.1, 3.8 → 4.8 (3.8.k → 4.8.k). Part B: 4 → 6, 4.1 → 6.1, 4.2 → 6.2, 5 and 5.1–5.2 unchanged, 5.3 → Part A §3 (retitled "Challenging a Certification"), 5.3.1 → Part A §3.1, 5.3.2 → 5.3, 5.3.3 → 5.4, 6 → 7 (6.1 → 7.1, 6.2 → 7.2, 6.2.1 → 7.2.1, 6.3 → 7.3), 7 → 8. Part C: 3.7.1 → 4.4.1, 3.8.k.1 → 4.8.k.1. Sections 1, 1.1, 1.2, 2, and 2.1 did not move.

**Second move (Record after Decide; Operate added).** Part B, from the five-phase numbering: 6 → 7 (6.1 → 7.1, 6.2 → 7.2), 7 and 7.1 → 6 (the old §7 introduction and §7.1 merge into one section, retitled "Certification Outcomes"), 7.2 → 8 (retitled "Recertification and Reopening"), 7.2.1 → 8.1, 7.3 → 8.2, 8 → 9. Part A and Part C numbers did not change. The reason: the record states the outcome, so the decision comes first; and a certification that is in force needs a phase of its own for keeping it current.

**Third move (go-live gate; challenge path to Operate; 2026-10-08).** Decision: a system does not go live, as a pilot or in full deployment, before it is certified. A pilot needs its own certification, scaled by class and by how substitutable the system is. Systems already operating when an adopter's instrument takes effect are excepted only for the transition period, under the "Existing instantiations" clock in Article XXVIII-A. Because nothing goes live before the outcome, the published record challenge path no longer opens in Frame: it opens when the outcome is published, and is already open for a system already operating. Section moves, from the six-phase numbering above: Part A §3 (Challenging a Certification) → Part B §7.1, with §3.1 → §7.1.1; Part A §4 and its subsections → §3 (4.k → 3.k, 4.4.1 → 3.4.1, 4.7.1 → 3.7.1, 4.8.k → 3.8.k); Part B 5 → 4 (5.k → 4.k), 6 → 5, 7 → 6 (7.1 → 6.1, 7.2 → 6.2), 8 → 7 (8.1 → 7.2, 8.2 → 7.3), 9 → 8. Part C: 4.4.1 → 3.4.1, 4.8.k.1 → 3.8.k.1. Part B shifted down by one so the chapter numbering stays continuous after Part A lost a section. This move changed obligations, not just order: the go-live gate and the systems-already-operating exception (Part B §5), the pilot rule (Part A §2.1), the challenge-path opening rule (Part B §7.1.1), and the matching "Challenge path first" paragraph (§4.2) and contestability-chain step 1 (§4.3). The conserved-obligations statement above does not hold for this move; the wording needs the steward's sign-off before release. Rewrite tool: `tools/ch8_go_live_gate_reorder.py`. Note: [evidence/2026-10-08/ch08_go_live_gate_reorder.md](evidence/2026-10-08/ch08_go_live_gate_reorder.md).

**Fourth move (status vocabulary; 2026-10-08).** Decision: the Chapter Nine outcome statuses are named **Full Certification**, **Provisional Certification** and **Not Certified**, and "recognition" no longer names a certification status. Outcome list in Part B §5: provisional certification, full certification, conditional certification (an overlay on either status), deferred certification, non-certification, withdrawal, expiration, release from conditions. A system that holds neither status is **Not Certified** (defined in Part B §5). The Part A §1.1 chart shows all three statuses off the Decide node, each flowing into Record, with Operate downstream. Terms renamed where they carry the certification sense: Chapter Five definitions Full Certification and Provisional Certification (anchors `#full-certification`, `#provisional-certification`); Part B §7.2 Provisional and full certification (`#72-provisional-and-full-certification`); CF-7.2 Constitutional alignment certification and review, with CF-7.2.3 Minimum Certification Record; CS-3 §1.4 Alignment-status certification and ambiguity default; CS-5 *Forum certification and lifecycle review*; and the matching wording in Chapters Three, Four, Five, Eleven and Thirteen, the CF and CS files that cite them, and the steward door card. Senses of "recognition" that are not certification status were left alone: standing recognition pathways and Chapter One §3.2, refuge and movement recognition, CF-10.11 cross-jurisdiction recognition, "recognizably identifiable" likeness, and the adopter guide's recognition of a shared standard process. Rewrite tool: `tools/ch8_certification_status_rename.py`. Note: [evidence/2026-10-08/ch08_certification_status_rename.md](evidence/2026-10-08/ch08_certification_status_rename.md).

**Fifth move (pilot section and Chapter Five definitions; 2026-10-10).** The pilot rule moved out of Part A §2.1 into its own §2.2 *Pilot certification* (anchor `#22-pilot-certification`), with the wording unchanged. §2.1 again runs from class scaling straight into participation and proportionality. Part B §5, the Part A §2 Trace, and the Chapter Nine overview table now point to §2.2; the 2026-10-08 entries above, which say "Part A §2.1" for the pilot rule, record what was true on that date. Chapter Five gained two Continuity-band definitions, **Go-Live** and **Pilot**, placed in alphabetical order between Full Certification and Provisional Certification. Each states the Chapter Five meaning only; the rules stay in Part B §5 and Part A §2.2. Two statements are new and need the steward's sign-off: a pilot that operates beyond its certified scope, population, or duration is a further go-live (a material expansion) needing its own certification, and a live release called a beta, trial, or preview is at least a pilot. CS-5 no longer calls a pilot "optional but recommended" or a non-production environment; it now points to the Pilot definition and to the Part A §2.2 expectation. CS-10 gained a pointer sentence on its "Pilots" bullet.

**Sixth move (Phase II split into IIa, IIb, and IIc; 2026-10-11).** Decision (custodian): Phase II (Evaluate) is split into three sub-phases, one section each, matching the three-tier table that §3 already carried. **IIa** (always) stays §3.1–§3.4. **IIb** (when implicated): Part A §3.5 → §4.1, §3.6 → §4.2, §3.7 → §4.3, §3.7.1 → §4.3.1, under a new §4 *Evaluations Where Implicated* that groups them and adds no obligation. **IIc** (when triggered): Part A §3.8 → §5, §3.8.k → §5.k (k = 1–6); Part C §3.8.k.1 → §5.k.1. Part B moves by two: §4 → §6 (4.1–4.4 → 6.1–6.4), §5 → §7, §6 → §8 (6.1 → 8.1, 6.2 → 8.2), §7 → §9 (7.1 → 9.1, 7.1.1 → 9.1.1, 7.2 → 9.2, 7.3 → 9.3), §8 → §10. Sections 1 through 3.4, Part C §3.4.8, and the six phases did not change. The §3 title stays *Whole-System Certification Evaluation*, and the §3 introduction now states that Phase II runs through §3, §4, and §5; citations of §3 as the whole-system duty were reviewed one by one (see the evidence record). The reason: §3.8 held about half of Part A and sat five levels deep; promoting the existing tiers to sections makes Part A's size and numbering follow the phase. The entries above use the numbers in force on their dates; read them through this map. Scripted by `tools/ch8_phase2_split_remap.py` (one simultaneous old-to-new table, so Part B's old §4 cannot collide with the new §4). Translations are paused; their link fragments are not retargeted in this move.

**Seventh move (Phase VI split into VIa and VIb; 2026-10-12).** Decision (custodian): Phase VI (Operate) is split into two sub-phases, one section each, on the model of the Phase II split. **VIa** (operate and recertify) is §9 Recertification and Reopening. **VIb** (standing bridge) is §10 Relationship to Standing, the bounded hand-off to Chapter Ten. Labels only: no section moved, renumbered, or reworded as an obligation, and the six phases did not change. The split follows the kind of work (operating a certified system versus handing verified facts to standing), where the Phase II split follows when an evaluation applies. Updated: the Part A §1.1 overview sentence, chart, and reader guide; the Chapter Nine overview reader guide; the Part B ownership list, chart, and a one-line sub-phase note at the head of §9 and §10 (as Part A §4 and §5 carry for IIb and IIc); and the table above. The chart's Phase VI box now reads "Phase VIa, Operate and recertify", and the dotted links to Chapter Ten read "VIb, §10". Translations stay paused.

**Eighth move (Part B §6 aligned with the phases; 2026-10-12).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): the §6 intro now names the phases instead of listing five acts; §7 and §8 gain the one-line phase notes that §9 and §10 already carry; §6.2 ends at a hand-off (step 6) instead of deciding and completing the record; the contestability chain uses one set of path names in §6.3 and §9.1.1; §9 defines *validation* once; and §6.1 is split into §6.1.1 (the four seats) and §6.1.2 through §6.1.7 (one subsection for each forum family, in the order §6.1 already used, with the bullet text unchanged). The §6 opening is reordered: lead sentence, requirement and phases, Tetrad, then the owner-layer boundary. No section moved, renumbered, or retitled; §6.1 keeps its number and anchor, and the six phases did not change. Three changes are operative and need the steward's sign-off: (1) the lead forum holds the verify-or-authorize seat and a separate record holder holds the record seat (this applies Chapter Eight §2 and §6 to §6.2; it adds no new prohibition); (2) validation is defined as a recertification or a reopened or fresh review of an operating system; (3) an explicit statement that stays and interim protection do not wait for the supervisory sequence (pointing to rules already in §7 and Chapter Thirteen). Updated: Part B §6 (intro, Trace, §6.1, §6.2, §6.3), §7 and §8 (phase notes), §9 (validation), §9.1.1 (pointer to §6.3). Note: [evidence/2026-10-12/ch08b_section6_phase_alignment.md](evidence/2026-10-12/ch08b_section6_phase_alignment.md). Translations stay paused.

**Ninth move (Charter Role Map; 2026-10-12).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): the published map of seats that Chapter Eight §5 requires is named the **Charter Role Map** and defined in Chapter Five (Continuity band, after the System Charter entry). A System Charter or Institutional Charter names it, or points to it. It has two layers: *eligibility* (which offices may hold a seat, substitute, or share a hosting) changes under the amendment authority of the System Charter or Institutional Charter; *current holders* change by dated version, and an act is judged against the version in force when it was taken. Part B §6.1.1 now says which seats the map places for a certification (initiating, record, contest) and that it does not place the verify-or-authorize seat, which belongs to the forums assigned under §6.2 and Chapter Thirteen; the long placement paragraph is gone, and the Record names the map version in force (§8.1). Chapter Eight §5 names the map and adds version and effective date; the Chapter Eight chart label matches. CI-3.6 gains field 12 (Charter Role Map) and a review bullet; CI-3.2 gains a fallback for certification seats; CI-4.5 gains the update rules; CI-4.6 uses the new name. Operative and needing the steward's sign-off: (1) the record and contest seats for a certification sit outside the operator's and proponent's control line, and the operator cannot alone change those entries (Chapter Ten §3.7 already requires this for standing records); (2) the verify-or-authorize seat is not placed by the System Charter or Institutional Charter; (3) the version-in-force rule. No section moved, renumbered, or retitled. Note: [evidence/2026-10-12/ch08b_section6_phase_alignment.md](evidence/2026-10-12/ch08b_section6_phase_alignment.md). Translations stay paused.

**Tenth move (Chapter Seven, System and Institutional Charters; 2026-10-12).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): a new Chapter Seven, `core_07.000_charters.md`, sits between the Rights Floor and the separation-of-duties chapter, and the old Chapters Seven through Seventeen are now Eight through Eighteen. Systems and institutions of Class L or above (Classes A, B, C and L; only Class P is excluded) are required to publish a System Charter or Institutional Charter (see the Eleventh move for the split). Chapter Seven carries the thin floor: what each must state, the Charter Role Map, changes and versions, and the timing default. The 12-field template (CI-3.6), the lane map (CI-3.2) and the seat catalog (CI-4.5, CI-4.6) stay in the Institutions corpus. The Chapter Five scope-instrument entries and the Charter Role Map entry stay the canonical definitions; Chapter Eight §5 keeps the seat requirements and holds the hook into Chapter Seven. Operative and needing the steward's sign-off: (1) the Class L threshold; (2) an institution takes the highest class of the systems it runs, or else is classified by its own impact; (3) a System Charter or Institutional Charter is required before go-live, or at the next classification review or certification for a body already running; (4) standing-record custody (CI-3.6 field 11) is carried by the Role Map. Renumbering: file names, section cites, chart labels and tool names moved by one from the old Chapter Seven onward. Chapter-numbered stable IDs moved with them: the regression scenario families `RS-CH7-*` and `RS-CH9-Q3-*` are now `RS-CH8-*` and `RS-CH10-Q3-*`, the pointer rule is `CH4-CH9-POINTER`, and the nested-list rules run `CH7-NEST-CANDIDATE` (System and Institutional Charters) through `CH18-NEST-CANDIDATE`. The sentience-status record key is now `chapter_thirteen_special_route`. The assessment schema's designation property, formerly the legacy name `chapter_eight`, is now `chapter_twelve` (with `$defs` `chapterTwelveTier`, `chapterTwelveSlot`, `chapterTwelveLabel`); stored records that still use `chapter_eight` must be renamed. Translations keep their old local numbering and file names; only links out to English files were updated. Older entries in this section and in `project/MEMLOG.md` were rewritten to current chapter numbers, except the pinned pilot notes in §5, which keep the old numbering on purpose.

**Eleventh move (System, Institutional, and Constitutional Charter; 2026-10-13).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): the bare Charter named three different documents, and Chapter Seven carried a paragraph to keep them apart. The word is replaced by three terms, all defined in Chapter Five. A **System Charter** is the scope instrument of one system. An **Institutional Charter** is the scope instrument of an institution that runs more than one system: it holds every one of its System Charters and states why it runs those particular systems. An institution that runs exactly one system publishes only that System Charter, which also serves as its Institutional Charter. A **Constitutional Charter** is the Chapter Fourteen founding instrument that authorizes a political community to govern (the "treaty, compact, or Constitutional Charter" family); it is not governed by Chapter Seven. The adoption instrument under Chapter Seventeen §10 is not a System Charter, Institutional Charter, or Constitutional Charter. The single Chapter Five entry for the bare Charter is replaced by three entries; **Charter Role Map** keeps its name. Chapter Seven keeps its file name (`core_07.000_charters.md`) and is retitled *System and Institutional Charters and Published Scope*; its §2, §3 and §5 are retitled and re-anchored, and the "Three documents that share a word" paragraph is deleted. CI-3.6 is retitled *System and Institutional Charter contents, review, and formation template* and gains an Institutional Charter additions paragraph. Operative and needing the steward's sign-off: (1) an institution with one system publishes one document; (2) an institution with several systems publishes an Institutional Charter that includes every System Charter and the portfolio rationale; (3) an institution that runs no covered system publishes an Institutional Charter with no System Charters to include; (4) adding, retiring, or merging a system is a material amendment of the Institutional Charter; (5) each System Charter keeps the Charter Role Map for its own system's acts and the Institutional Charter carries the map for the institution's own acts; (6) the multi-role sentence of the old paragraph (one document may serve more than one role only if it meets each chapter's requirements) is dropped. Anchors: `#charter` is now `#system-charter` and `#institutional-charter`; the Chapter Seven, Preamble §5, and CI-3.6 headings and the Institution Foundation Kit §5 and §8 headings carry the new names. The phrase "chartered scope" is kept as a plain adjective. Translations stay paused.

**Twelfth move (Institutional alignment certification; Chapter Nine Part D; 2026-10-13).** Decision (steward sign-off pending, drafted on branch `docs/ch09-institutional-alignment-certification`, uncommitted): Chapter Nine certified only a System, while Chapter Seven already sent an Institution to a certification that did not exist for it. A new file, `core_09.015_institutional_alignment_certification.md`, adds **Part D** (§11 to §15, continuing the numbering after Part B §10): institution class and institutional evaluation (Phases I and II, §12), forum roles, outcome and the **Institutional Certification Record** (Phases III to V, §13), and recertification, reopening, the cascade from System certifications, and the standing bridge (Phase VI, §14 and §15). Phases III to VI are reused from Part B by reference. Chapter Nine keeps its name and file names; no chapter is added and no existing term is renamed (CH9-PROCESS-ORDER-01 holds). Two Chapter Five entries are added beside the System Alignment Certification group, not in it: **Institutional Alignment Certification** and **Institutional Certification Record**. Operative and needing the steward's sign-off: (1) composition by link, not containment: an Institutional Certification Record cites each System Certification Record, never re-runs it, and one System record may be cited by several Institutions or none; (2) one system means one pass and one record, with an institutional findings block; (3) no go-live gate and no licence on forming or operating an Institution (Article XI-E), with a class-scaled window for a provisional first certification, modeled on the Article XXVIII-A clock; (4) the Integrity Forum leads and Institutional Forums hold the mandate and duty-compliance components; (5) cascade: a failed, withdrawn, or expired cited System certification is an indicator of impending misalignment of the Institution, and current misalignment only where the System's cause finding traces to the Institution's own governance; (6) forums, certifiers, and record offices are Institutions, led and reviewed from outside their own Material Control Line, and no two bodies certify each other in a circle within one cycle; (7) Class L takes all seven items in simplified form and omits none (six when first drafted; the seventh, office-holder standing and authenticity, was added in the Thirteenth move); (8) the institution class test sits in Part D §12.1 (portfolio floor; own impact; own impact above the portfolio governs institutional-only items). Edits elsewhere: Chapter Seven §2 (Timing, Class of an Institution), the opening chart and its reading bullets, §6, §7; Chapter Eight §2 (the fourth prohibited pairing now covers an institution's governing authority) and §9; Chapter Thirteen §2, §3, §4, §4.5 and the routing table (certification "for systems" now reads "for systems and institutions"); Part A §3.3 (institutional facts by link); Part B §8.1 and §9 (link and trigger); CI-3.2 and CI-3.6 (pointers). Not done: a Preamble owner-register entry and a Preamble process-map entry, a CS-3 pointer to the institution class test, translations, and the Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened-review step for the Chapter Thirteen edits. Part D is outside the six-phase table above because it repeats those phases for a different subject.

**Thirteenth move (formation check on office-holder standing and authenticity; remediation readiness, denial, and revocation; 2026-10-13).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; drafted on branch `docs/ch09-institutional-alignment-certification`, uncommitted): two extra checks were added where the process could otherwise be passed on paper alone. (1) **Chapter Seven §7 Formation Check: Office-Holder Standing and Authenticity.** Before a System or Institution of Class L or higher exercises authority over others, the holders of its covered offices (governing authority, Charter Role Map seats, and offices that bind others or hold records, keys, or funds) are checked for **standing** (no lock, competency bar met, conflicts cleared, from verified records only) and **authenticity** (holds the office in its own right and for the body's stated purpose, found from control, interests and rewards, conduct, and protected voice, not from holding a belief or belonging to a group by itself). Other staff are covered by a structural limb only. The result is a published statement, not a gate: a failed or late check is a defect to log, enters the next certification as a finding, and does not condition forming or operating (Article XI-E; the two narrow gates added in the Fourteenth move attach only to a named sentient's authority). The old §7 Relationship to Other Chapters is now §8. (2) **Chapter Nine Part B §7.1 Remediation Readiness: Willingness and Capacity to Correct** and **§7.2 Denial and Revocation.** Whenever a review finds misalignment, the forum asks whether the operator is willing and able to fix it, from conduct and a verified proof step and not from promises, and states one of ready, ready with support, willing not able, able not willing, neither, or not yet shown (one bounded deferral). Denial (the existing outcome non-certification) and revocation (the existing outcome withdrawal) are then required on stated grounds, and conditions may not be used to carry a plan the check did not find ready. Protection first: a Class A survival-essential system changes status at once and keeps operating only under separately authorized interim reliance. Not a permanent bar; not a licence. Part D gains item 7 in §12.2 (so seven items, not six), a §13.5 for Institutions (effects limited by §11.3: status, notice, adverse weight in its Systems' certifications, no seat in others' certifications while Not Certified, referral to CI-13, standing bridge), new reopening triggers in §14.2, and a nominee-in-a-covered-office evasion in §15. No new outcome word, seat, or Chapter Five term. CI-3.6 holds the statement template. Operative and needing the steward's sign-off: the covered-office scope; no individual screening of other staff; denial and revocation named as plain words for non-certification and withdrawal; the five revocation grounds; the rule that a Not Certified Institution may not hold a seat in another body's certification; the added weight against its Systems. Note: [evidence/2026-10-13/formation_check_and_remediation_readiness.md](evidence/2026-10-13/formation_check_and_remediation_readiness.md).

**Fourteenth move (two narrow gates in Article XI-E; Institutional Authority Standing Lock; 2026-10-13).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; drafted on branch `docs/ch09-institutional-alignment-certification`, uncommitted): Article XI-E barred every gate on forming or operating an institution, so a sentient with a verified wrongdoing record, or a sentient a forum had reason to think misaligned, could not be kept from controlling a body. Article XI-E now keeps the bar on any gate on forming or operating, and adds **two narrow gates on a named sentient's authority** (taking or exercising governing, controlling, or owning authority), never on the body or on anyone else: (1) an **outstanding lock** (an unresolved Institutional Authority Standing Lock, Anti-Constitutional Trust Lock, or other standing lock whose named pathway is authority over the same kind of institution), reaching only as far as the lock does; (2) an **interim hold on probable cause of misalignment**, issued only by a forum with jurisdiction under Chapter Thirteen §5 Interim protection, on specific verified facts disclosed to the sentient, more likely than not misaligned (judged on conduct, control, and rewards, never belief), a real risk of serious harm or of defeating the body's checks, and a narrower safeguard not enough; time-bound, contestable, not a finding, lock, standing record, or standing effect. The party imposing a gate bears the burden. Article XI-E also states a condition of **authentic formation** (added the same day at the custodian's request): a sentient may form or run a body only when authentically aligned with its stated purpose, and forming or running one with intent to mislead, or with probable cause to find that intent, is not allowed; judged on conduct, control, and rewards and never belief; a founder with no record is presumed authentic; no clearance up front; probable cause reaches the interim hold, and a body left without a lawful holder gets an interim steward and is not stopped. Both gates also satisfy Chapter One §7.1 Limitation Discipline. Because a lock attaches only to a verified finding, probable cause could not be a lock; it is an interim hold. A new **Institutional Authority Standing Lock** is added to Chapter Eleven §5.5 (trigger: a verified finding that the subject materially used an institution, enterprise, or office as the vehicle for a violation, including nominee and shell concealment, retaliation from within, and knowing retention of a misaligned reward; named pathway: governing, controlling, or owning authority in that body, its successors and nominee-held vehicles, and named comparable bodies; mandatory when the trigger is met; independent restoration record including a verified authenticity finding), with pointers in Chapter Eleven §4.2 and §10.6, the Chapter Five Standing Lock entry and creation-rights limits note, Chapter Seven §7 (limb one now checks both gates; the body refers probable-cause facts to a forum and may not hold a sentient out on its own view), Part D §11.3, §13.5 (referral), §14.2 and §15, and the CI-3.6 template. Chapter Thirteen interim-protection text was not edited. Operative and needing the steward's sign-off: the gates attach to the named sentient and reach the body only as vehicle, successor, controlled entity, or nominee-held form; the standard (more likely than not on specific verified facts); the lock is mandatory when its trigger is met; no lock from probable cause. Needs Chapter Seventeen §1 Heightened Scrutiny and Chapter Fifteen non-regression review, because it changes a Rights-Floor article. Note: [evidence/2026-10-13/formation_check_and_remediation_readiness.md](evidence/2026-10-13/formation_check_and_remediation_readiness.md).

**Fifteenth move (Interim Hold standard; 2026-10-13).** Decision (custodian, asked in session after a corpus sweep; drafted on branch `docs/ch09-institutional-alignment-certification`, uncommitted): the interim hold added to Article XI-E in the Fourteenth move was generalized into one standard. The sweep ([evidence/2026-10-13/interim_hold_sweep_evaluation.md](evidence/2026-10-13/interim_hold_sweep_evaluation.md)) found that Article XIX-B already names "temporary protective limits while a case is still open, before anything is verified" and Chapter Twelve Part A §4.1 invites interim steps, but nothing designed them. New: (1) **Chapter Five entry Interim Hold** (separate from Stay, which pauses an action and controls sequence); (2) **Chapter Thirteen §5, subsection Interim hold on a named sentient's pathway** (owner): ground (specific facts disclosed to the sentient and verified to a preliminary tier, more likely than not, serious harm or defeat of a check that cannot wait, narrower safeguard not enough, evidence tier rising with restrictiveness), issuer outside the subject's control line, lock-style record fields, a five-rung ladder (disclosure and monitoring; co-holder or second key; step-aside from one matter; escrow or segregation; restraint), duration reusing the Chapter Thirteen §6 outer bound with one extension on the §6.1 continuation burden (no second clock table), no finding and no standing effect, wrongful-hold redress, publication on the CF-11 schedule, a never-reached list (liberty and care separation, governance voting, speech and protected activity, Rights Floor and survival access, Stakeholder status, whole-body suspension, general property), and a closed list of applications; (3) **Chapter Eleven §5.6 Interim holds and locks** (different trigger, no standing effect, never converted, time under a hold counts toward a later lock's time bound but not its corrective conditions, no substitute for a lock, a hold needs no open remedy record under §4.4); (4) applications: Article XI-E (refactored to cite the standard), Chapter Seven §7 and CI-4.2 (covered office-holders at any time, not only at formation), CF-4.15 (new), CF-7.3.2, and Article XII-D (forum, certifier, record, and verify-or-authorize seats; first rung is step-aside from one matter; issued from outside the member's appointing authority and family; anti-pretext applies); (5) pointers in Article XIX-B, Part D, CI-3.6, and the Chapter Five Standing Lock entry. A Stay may bar an adverse action against a reporter or refuser (the protective direction); no new tool. Deferred: stakeholder-weight escrow, and a hold on holders of authority over Class A, B, or C systems on grave anti-constitutional allegations. Operative and needing the steward's sign-off: the ground and its evidence tier; the closed list of applications (a new use needs a naming provision); the never-reached list; time counting toward a later lock; reuse of the Chapter Thirteen §6 bound. Article XI-E's authentic-formation condition, the hold ground, the Limits clause, the Chapter Five creation-rights note, and Chapter Seven §7 also name **intent to harm** (harm this Constitution forbids; ordinary competition, lawful criticism, and lawful dissent are not harm in this sense), found from verified conduct, design and control, rewards, and what the sentient has verifiably said. A verified statement, call, or plan that others deserve harm or discriminatory treatment forbidden under Article VI-C counts against the sentient. **Belief rule (threaded, at the custodian's request):** the Constitution does not say a belief is never judged, because discrimination is often argued in religious or political terms. Holding a belief, or belonging to a faith, party, or group, does not count by itself, and a profession of good intent does not count in the sentient's favor; but a religious, political, or ideological label is no shield for a statement, call, plan, or act that harms others or treats them in ways Article VI-C forbids, and that is judged on what it states, calls for, or does. The rule rests on Article XI-A's own limit (manifesting belief consistently with the rights of others), Article XI-G (offense or disagreement alone is not harm), and Article XI-F; Chapter Seven §7 holds the safeguards (*Holding is not enough*, *Labels are no shield*, *Control is not membership*, and *What a holder has said*: specific, in context, not satire or criticism or dissent, no surveillance of private speech, answer and resolution, no penalty for the speech itself). Needs Chapter Seventeen §1 Heightened Scrutiny and Chapter Fifteen non-regression review, because it edits Chapter Thirteen and Rights-Floor articles, although it adds safeguards to a category Article XIX-B already recognized.

**Sixteenth move (Expression and the Harm Line; 2026-10-13).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; drafted on branch `docs/ch09-institutional-alignment-certification`, uncommitted): to stop restating the same rule wherever expression meets harm, Chapter One gains **§7.5 Expression and the Harm Line**, a Freedom principle that adds no new rule. It says: expression is protected as expression (criticism, offense, disagreement, and peaceful protest are not harm); expression is not a shield for conduct, so speech, publication, design, and policy that are the means of harm, harassment and bullying, cruelty, or discrimination are judged on what they state, call for, or do under the existing Chapter Five definitions (which keep their meaning: a material pattern for harassment and bullying, a real material-impact pathway for harm); the same test applies to a sentient and to an institution, and calling criticism of an institution bullying is a limit on expression that carries the burden under §7.1 and Chapter Four; religious, political, ideological, cultural, artistic, or commercial labels do not change the test, and a topic or point of view does not make protected expression harm; and limiting expression is different from using it as evidence (a limit must pass §7.1 and Article XI-B; evidence use follows the rules of the chapter that asks, and may not become a penalty for the speech). It carries one principle-level Scope sentence (it applies Limitation Discipline and the Chapter Five definitions to expression and creates no category of harm or ground for limiting freedom that Limitation Discipline does not allow); the earlier list of Articles it does not narrow was removed (and the same non-narrowing clauses were trimmed from §7.2 and §7.3, keeping §7.3's separable-conduct Scope sentence because §7.3 has no other statement of it) because principles rank above articles under the Internal Hierarchy, Chapter Six §1.2 Layer scope and Chapter Fifteen already bar narrowing a floor, and Article XI-B now carries the non-narrowing statement. Pointers: Article XI-B (new bullet *Expression is not a shield for conduct*, and a Trace upstream link), Article XI-G (Trace), and the Chapter One §7 Trace and diagram. Trimmed to point at it: Article XI-E (Authentic formation), Chapter Seven §7 (*Labels are no shield*, the evidence-only clause, *Not a penalty for speech*), Part D §12.2 item 7, the Chapter Five Interim Hold failure list, and the Chapter Eleven restoration condition. The Chapter Seven §7 evidence-use safeguards stay where they are (verified, in context, no surveillance, answer and resolution). Needs the steward's sign-off, plus Chapter Seventeen §1 Heightened Scrutiny and Chapter Fifteen non-regression review (Chapter One and a Rights-Floor article are edited); the review should confirm that §7.5 restates and adds no restriction, and that the limit-versus-evidence distinction is consistent with Article XI-B's strictest review for limits aimed at what is said.

**Seventeenth move (Chapter Seven section order and charts; 2026-10-14).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): Chapter Seven sections follow the path a body takes, which two reader charts now draw: §1 purpose, §2 when required, §3 what must be stated, **§4 Charter Role Map and standing-record custody** (was §6), **§5 formation check** (was §7), **§6 amendment, review, and scope misalignment** (was §4), **§7 what a System Charter or Institutional Charter cannot do** (was §5), §8 relationship to other chapters. Text moved with its headings and anchors; no rule was changed. Citations to the moved sections were retargeted in Chapters Zero, One, Five, Six, Eight, Nine Part D, Ten, Eleven, Thirteen, and CI-3.6, and the generated indexes were rebuilt. Dated records (TODO, MEMLOG, this log, `evidence/`) and the paused translations keep the old numbers. The two charts sit in §1; they name Chapter Seven §§2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 (VIS-CHART-SYNC-03).

**Eighteenth move (Chapter Seven new §2, No Licence, and Two Narrow Limits; 2026-10-14).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the right to form and the two narrow Article XI-E limits (an outstanding standing lock; a forum-ordered Interim Hold on probable cause) move out of §1 and the formation check into one early section, so the reader learns what can and cannot stop a body before the duty to publish a System Charter or Institutional Charter. The limits are stated once there, with the pointer to Article XI-E as owner; the formation check (now §6) points back to it and keeps its mechanics, and its "No gate on the body" bullet is shortened to a pointer. The decision-path chart moved to the end of the new §2. Chapter Seven is now §1 purpose; **§2 no licence and two narrow limits**; §3 when required (was §2); §4 what must be stated (was §3); §5 Charter Role Map (was §4); §6 formation check (was §5); §7 amendment, review, and scope misalignment (was §6); §8 what a System Charter or Institutional Charter cannot do (was §7); §9 relationship to other chapters (was §8). Citations in other chapters were retargeted and generated files rebuilt. Dated records and paused translations keep older numbers. Both Chapter Seven charts name Chapter Seven §§2 to 9 (VIS-CHART-SYNC-03).

**Nineteenth move (Chapter Seven §2 and §6 overlap removed; 2026-10-14).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the formation check is not folded into §2 (it is about 2,800 words, depends on §3 to §5, and about 22 links point at it). Instead the two §6 Result bullets that restated §2 ("No gate on the body" and "Limits of the right to form") become one pointer bullet. The one point §2 lacked, a body formed or run by a founder who is not authentically aligned with its stated purpose, with intent to mislead or to harm, now sits in §2's paragraph on the limits of the right to form. No renumbering, anchors, citations, or chart labels changed.

**Twentieth move (Chapter Seven two charts merged into one; 2026-10-14).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the seats-and-certification chart in §1 and the decision-path chart at the end of §2 overlapped (both ran the instrument, the formation check, certification, and keeping it true), so they became one chart at the end of §2 with the decision path as its spine. The Charter Role Map and Chapter Eight (segregation of duties) are now steps on that path, and Chapter Fourteen is folded into the "stays in force" box as one line. The §1 chart, its reader-map widget, and its bullets were removed, and §1 gains one pointer sentence to §2. The merged chart has one Reader guidance widget, one plain-terms line, and one set of bullets (the Chapter Eight seat bullet and the certification bullet carry the four-seat citations from the old §1 chart). No section renumbering. Chapter Seven now has one chart naming §§2 to 9 (VIS-CHART-SYNC-03).

**Twenty-first move (Chapter Seven formation check moved up to §4; 2026-10-15).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the formation check follows §3 directly, so the chapter reads required (§3), then who may hold the covered offices (§4), then what the instrument states (§5), then the Charter Role Map (§6). Old §4 (What System and Institutional Charters Must State) is now §5, old §5 (The Charter Role Map and Standing-Record Custody) is now §6, and old §6 (Formation Check: Office-Holder Standing and Authenticity) is now §4. §§1 to 3 and §§7 to 9 keep their numbers. Text of the sections is unchanged apart from section numbers, anchors, and link labels. About 40 links in 14 other files (Chapters Zero, One, Five, Six, Eight, Nine Part D, Ten, Eleven, Thirteen, Fourteen, CI-3, and the Institution Foundation Kit) and the 19 language pilots of Chapter One were retargeted. The chart's boxes and path are unchanged; its section labels now read §4 for the formation check, §5 for the minimum content, and §6 for the Role Map. Because §4 now cites the ten required items and the Role Map, it points forward to §5 and §6 (the earlier evaluation noted this dependency). In the same session the Class L, Class P, and recognition bullets in §1 were nested, and the recognition-of-contribution bullet moved to §9 under Chapter Ten. Derived files were regenerated (VIS-CHART-SYNC-03).

**Twenty-second move (Request for a System Charter or Institutional Charter; formation check failure at Class L; 2026-10-15).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the question was what happens when an Institution reaches Class L and fails the formation check. The existing rules already answer it (no gate on the body; the failure lands on the office; it enters the next certification as a finding; readiness, denial, and revocation follow Part B §7 and Part D §13.5). Three things were added. (1) Chapter Seven §3 gains **Request for a System Charter or Institutional Charter**: anyone who notices a new or newly Class L System or Institution with no published System Charter or Institutional Charter may ask its governing authority for one, with no standing, fee, or showing of harm. The body has seven days to respond (publish, give a date, point to an existing instrument, or give the written showing that it is below every Domain-Specific Materiality line) and thirty days from the request to publish, with the formation check statement. *Timing* now reads "next classification review or certification, or within thirty days after a request, whichever comes first". A missed response or due date is a defect to log and correct, can be taken to the forum on the challenge route, and is never a condition of forming or operating. (2) A body that says it is below the line gives the written showing within the week; the request then becomes a classification challenge and the thirty days do not run while it is pending. When a body is found Class L, on that challenge or any other, the date of the finding counts as a request, with the same week and thirty days (pointer added to the Challenge route in Domain-Specific Materiality). (3) §4 *Standing fails* now says that, if refilling the office would leave the body without a lawful holder of a governing office and others depend on it, the forum with jurisdiction may name an interim steward and does not stop the body, as Article XI-E already provides for the Interim Hold. Scenarios `RS-CH7-FC-001` (failed check at Class L) and `RS-CH7-FC-002` (request, silence, repeat request, contested class) added in a new Ch7-FC family. The chart's "already operating" box and bullet name the thirty days. Core now fixes a seven-day and a thirty-day figure here; the clock for anything that reaches a forum stays with Article XXVI-C. No renumbering (VIS-CHART-SYNC-03).

**Twenty-third move (Chapter Seven resolution clock for a failed formation check; 2026-10-15).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): a failed check on a covered office runs on the same clock as a request for a System Charter or Institutional Charter. A week to respond (which office failed and what the body will do, or a showing that the finding is wrong) and thirty days to resolve it, with a new dated version of the statement confirmed by the verifier. The clock starts when the failure is found by the body, its verifier, or a forum, or is reported by anyone and not shown to be wrong, or when a holder's contest is decided. A failure that still stands after the clock and a reasonable chance to resolve it in good faith is referred by the lead forum as realignment resistance, which opens structural review under CI-13 and CJS-3.15. That review can end in dissolution or disbandment only as a last step, only where lesser measures cannot protect the Constitution's obligations, decided by the forum alone with the continuity safeguards. The check stays no gate on forming, publishing, or operating, and the failure alone never reaches the body. A missed request for a System Charter or Institutional Charter alone stays a defect to log and correct. Part D §13.5 effect 5 names the unresolved check as a referral ground. Sections touched: Chapter Seven §3 (*Timing* pointer), §4 (*Resolution clock*, *No gate on the body*), the chart's failed-holder box and bullet, Part D §13.5, scenario `RS-CH7-FC-001`. Steward review wanted before merge, because the path now ends in a possible dissolution and sits next to Article XI-E (no gate on forming or operating): Chapter Fifteen non-regression and Chapter Seventeen §1 Heightened Scrutiny should confirm that the path cannot reach a body on the failure alone. No renumbering (VIS-CHART-SYNC-03).

**Twenty-fourth move (small businesses: their own decisions, and seats held in common; 2026-10-15).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): a business run by a single sentient or a few is not to be buried in red tape and is to be able to build a viable seat structure with others. (1) Domain-Specific Materiality gains a small-business paragraph under the act test. Its own decisions (what it makes or sells, ordinary-trade prices, hiring, hours, suppliers) are not materially binding acts where the business cannot shape the market's terms and those it deals with can walk away. Seats attach only to an act material in at least one of the nine areas. A recurring covered decision (refund, tenancy, or booking rule) is one materially binding act when the policy is published or changed, and applying it is a clerical step. An adopting instrument may publish limits for pre-authorized routine choices at or below what the nine questions imply, open to review, and unable to move the line. Core sets no number, because size is not materiality. (2) Chapter Eight §6 gains **Seats held in common**: a small business may place its verify-or-authorize, record, or contest seat with a local cooperative or association, or any disinterested relying body. This is not a merger. The holder must be outside the initiating business's Material Control Line and have no interest the act determines, the group publishes one Charter Role Map that members adopt by reference, members may serve one another on different acts but not as a closed pair and no rotation may route an act into its initiator's control line, the group's own acts take four seats and an independent route outside it, joining is not required and terms cannot shut out a member or keep its Act Records, and a high stake still brings the stronger presumption of separate offices. A flat ban on rotation was not adopted, because it would defeat the purpose of a pool; the test is the control line. Pointers added in Chapter Eight §5, Chapter Seven §6 (*A shared map*), CI-4.6 shared seat rule 8, and CI-22. Scenarios `RS-CH8-SMB-001` and `RS-CH8-SMB-002` added in a new Ch8-SMB family. No renumbering (VIS-CHART-SYNC-03).

**Twenty-fifth move (Chapter Seven sections follow the chart; failed check gets its own section; chart cut to eight boxes; 2026-10-15).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the chapter is ordered by the path the one chart draws, so the sections read in the order a body acts. New order: §1 Purpose, Scope, and Owner Boundary (now also holds the chart as a roadmap, moved from the end of §2); §2 No Licence, and Two Narrow Limits; §3 When System and Institutional Charters Are Required; §4 What System and Institutional Charters Must State (was §5); §5 The Charter Role Map and Standing-Record Custody (was §6); §6 Formation Check: Office-Holder Standing and Authenticity (was §4); §7 When a Holder Fails the Check (new; holds the old formation check *Result* bullets, the *Resolution clock*, and the holder-fairness bullets, with the no-gate statement as its lead paragraph); §8 Amendment, Review, and Scope Misalignment (was §7); §9 What System and Institutional Charters Cannot Do (was §8); §10 Relationship to Other Chapters (was §9). The chart had 14 boxes and about 360 words and now has 8 boxes and about 110: forming (§2), is it material (§3), publish the System Charter or Institutional Charter (§4, §5), publish the office-holder statement (§6), a holder fails the check (§7), keep it true (§8), and Chapter Nine as an outside box. The nine bullets under the old chart were removed because each restated a section; the two citations only they carried moved to the sections that own them (the Chapter Nine four-seat and supervisory-sequence cites to §5, the Chapter Nine reopening cites to §8). The wording of the formation check, the resolution clock, and the fairness rules is unchanged apart from the lead paragraph and one bullet split in §7; the *In plain terms* lines of §1, §3, and §6 were shortened. The formation check's "no gate on the body" statements were reduced to one home in §7 (with §2 and the §1 *Not a licence* bullet), and the repeated "late or failed check is a defect" clause was dropped from §3 *Timing* and the §6 introduction. Citations were retargeted by slug: about 45 links in 15 core and implementation files, the scenario catalog (scenarios RS-CH7-FC-001, RS-CH7-FC-002, RS-CH8-SMB-002), and the 19 Chapter One language pilots. The link that cites the *Resolution clock* in Chapter Nine Part D now points to §7. Dated records (earlier moves in this file, TODO, MEMLOG history, `evidence/`, `archive/`) keep the numbers they were written with. Chart sync (VIS-CHART-SYNC-03): the chart names §§2 to 8 and Chapter Nine. Derived files were regenerated.

**Twenty-sixth move (dissolution and forced-contraction procedure in CI-13; two chart boxes added; 2026-10-15).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, "draft and insert"; uncommitted): CI-13 had a trigger floor and cooperation duties but no ordered steps, clocks, or definitions for the measures that CJS-3.15 and Chapter Nine Part D §13.5 name (suspension, receivership, structural separation, dissolution), and nothing for Systems. CI-13 now has ten numbered sections, CI-13.1 to CI-13.10: when a consequence matter opens (verified findings only); the findings the forum must make before any order; notice, a 7-day response, and a 30-day resolution window with one 30-day extension; a six-rung ladder (directed correction, constrained authority, a contraction order, supervised operation, receivership or structural separation, dissolution) with emergency containment as a separate lighter measure; the contraction order, which requires a body to stop, hand off, reduce, or convert the activities that put it over a Domain-Specific Materiality line until an independent verifier accepts a below-the-line statement, binds successors and bodies under common control, has a 24-month default term with 6-month review, and permits re-expansion on a resolution finding without making it a licence; two-key concurrence for rungs 5 and 6; continuity-transfer-first, remedy-first, destructive-disposition, and acquisitive-enforcement rules for dissolution; a Systems counterpart that serves orders on the steward and bars destruction of state-bearing components where a credible sentience indicator is on the record (sealed custody instead); and review, viewpoint-neutrality, redress, and enforcement rules, including defiance as a new verified fact. Pointers only elsewhere: CJS-3.15 (contraction named as an equivalent consequence; a new primary-failure clause), CF-7.1.1 (lead-record contents), CF-11.4 (the forum dissolution threshold keeps its immediate suspension, and the CI-13.7 wind-down requirements apply), CS-10.8 (ordered retirement follows it). Chapter Seven §7 *If it is not resolved* now names the ladder, and the §1 chart gained two outside-this-chapter boxes (CI-13 consequence review, reached from a holder failing the check and not resolving in thirty days; CI-13 contract below Class L or dissolve, as the last step). Four scenario seeds were added (RS-CI13-CR-001 to RS-CI13-CR-004). Numbers (7, 30, 12-month watch, 24-month term, 6-month review) are defaults in implementation text, and an adopting instrument may publish others with reasons within the tier outer bounds. Open for the steward: whether defiance of a final order becomes a named Chapter Twelve predicate; residual-asset distribution after remedy; receiver funding home; whether two-key concurrence is wanted at rung 5; reach to Class P bodies. Derived files were regenerated.

**Twenty-seventh move (practice clearance added to Article XI-E; Class P four-seat exemption; Chapter Seven §1 chart reordered; 2026-10-15).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): professional certifications and licences are handled as a *practice clearance*, a Competency Clearance (Chapter Eleven §6.2) that an adopting instrument may require before a named high-risk act, on a technical-forum risk finding (Chapter Thirteen §4.2, CF-10). Article XI-E gains one top-level bullet, *Practice clearance for a named high-risk act*, with risk finding, nothing-lighter-protects-them burden, decided-in-the-open, open routes and no monopoly, care in use, safeguards, and class-of-the-body sub-bullets; the plain-terms line, the intro sentence, *No denial through the back door*, the implementation bullet, and the Trace gain matching pointers. The clearance attaches to performing the act and never to forming or running a body, so the two-narrow-gates language on authority in a body is unchanged. Chapter Five (accountability band) gains one sentence separating a practice clearance from Competency Clearance for a seat; no new defined term yet (lowercase, inline). Performing such acts is material under Safety in Domain-Specific Materiality, so a body doing so is at least Class L; a requirement does not itself classify, and Class L never requires clearance. Chapter Seven: §1 now opens with the plain-terms guidance in the collapsed widget, the definition paragraph, the merged *Required at Class L or higher* lead, the chart, and then the bullets in the order Not a licence, Class P, When Class P stops being voluntary, Class L or higher; §2 points to the practice clearance. Chapter Eight §1 and §5 now say the four-seat floor does not apply to a Class P body's acts and applies from the day it takes a Materially Binding Act. Lower-tier practice certification is a protected title with disclosure and a host or hirer check, with no bar on the work (the recommended option, assumed approved). Second decision (custodian, same session): Class L duties may be met through a *joint professional practice*, in which a sentient or small practice adopts the published instrument of a host (professional association, cooperative, employer, or public registry) that carries the verify, record, and contest seats; class stays with the activity. New **CI-4.7** (*Practice licence, practice clearance, and joint professional practice*) holds the clearance entry rules, the lower-tier certification, the adoption notice, the host duties, and the safeguards (no closed shop, a direct route, no fixing, not one operation, a ninety-day re-adoption window, sampling). Pointers added in Chapter Seven §3 and §5, Chapter Eight §6, Domain-Specific Materiality aggregation, Chapter Nine Part D §12.4, and Article XI-E. Open for the steward: Chapter Five definitions for practice clearance and joint professional practice (both defined inline for now); scenario seeds; translations untouched. Later in the same session (custodian): CI-4.7 gained a public *host of last resort* that the adopting instrument designates and funds and that must admit any holder of the clearance (refusal only on the two narrow gates), with the direct route kept open; and an explicit split of forum authority: technical forums set and maintain the standards a host applies, hear validity challenges, and answer referred technical questions, while disputes about a host's conduct go to the Institutional Forum family, and technical forums do not accredit hosts or act as licensors (CF-10.7 to CF-10.10, CF-10.13). Derived files were regenerated. Revision (custodian, later same session): the tiers were reversed to match the steward's intent. A *practice licence* is the higher tier (severe or irreversible harm; a body performing a licensed act is at least Class L); a *practice clearance* is the lower tier (real but lesser harm, such as cosmetology or food handling; required before the act; no class of its own). Practice certification is dropped. Hirers and hosts check holders. Joint professional practice and the host of last resort apply only to acts with a listed licence or clearance. The wording earlier in this entry that calls the higher tier a practice clearance and the lower tier a practice certification is superseded by this revision. XI-E now carries both tiers in one bullet. The "nothing lighter protects them" showing in XI-E is now named the Avoidable Burden test and applies to both tiers: the party keeping a licence or clearance requirement shows it is necessary and proportionate to the harm in the finding, and each CI-4.7 entry records the lighter measures considered.

**Twenty-eighth move (Chapter Seven §3 split into four subsections; challenge clock; 2026-10-15).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): §3 was one flat list of eleven bullets that repeated rules other places own. It is now four short subsections that answer one reader question each: §3.1 Who must publish (the systems rule, a three-row table for the Institution cases, businesses and joint professional practice), §3.2 Which classes are covered, §3.3 When it is due, §3.4 If a System Charter or Institutional Charter is missing (the request, the week to respond, the thirty days to publish). Section numbers 4 to 10 do not change, and the §3 anchor is kept. **Moved, not changed:** *Equivalent instrument* and *Scaling* went to §4. **Cut as duplicates:** the Class P test (owned by §1, Domain-Specific Materiality, and CS-3), the Institution own-impact test (owned by Chapter Nine Part D §12.1; the portfolio floor stays in §3.2), and the adoption notice and the only-for-listed-acts rule (owned by CI-4.7). §1 gained one clause ("however many members it has, an Operating Party below every line stays Class P") and is the home of the Class P threshold. **One operative change:** the thirty days to publish now keep running while a classification challenge is pending, and are extended to thirty days after the decision only if the forum with jurisdiction judges the challenge review-worthy (it names a specific crossing in a named domain and the written showing leaves a real question about it). Before, the thirty days did not run while the challenge was pending. The Operating Party does not judge review-worthiness for itself. The same change is made in Domain-Specific Materiality (*Challenge route*, *A challenge does not stop the Operating Party*) and in scenario `RS-CH7-FC-002`. Cites to the old *Timing* and *Request for a System Charter or Institutional Charter* bullets now point to §3.3 and §3.4 (Chapter Seven §1, §6, §7; Chapter Nine Part D §11.3 window). Draft and sign-off questions are in the project note `claude/ch07-section3-restructure-draft.md`; the custodian accepted the draft's choices for now.

**Twenty-ninth move (Chapter Seven opens with "when required"; sections 1 to 3 reordered; chart opens at the materiality question; 2026-10-16).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, "option 2"; uncommitted): the reader's first question is whether the chapter applies to them, so the old §3 now opens the chapter body. New order: §1 When System and Institutional Charters Are Required (was §3; its subsections are now §1.1 Who must publish, §1.2 Which classes are covered, §1.3 When it is due, §1.4 If a System Charter or Institutional Charter is missing); §2 Purpose, Scope, and Owner Boundary (was §1; still holds the chart as the roadmap and the home of the Class P threshold); §3 No Licence, and Two Narrow Limits (was §2). Sections 4 to 10 keep their numbers. The chart no longer starts at "A group, system, or business forms": its first box is "Material to others?" and carries "Class L or higher — §1" and "Nobody needs permission to form — §3". The preface reading order no longer says it follows the chart, because §3 is the one section the chart names only inside a box. **No operative change.** Links and cites were retargeted with a slug-keyed rewrite (Chapters Five, Nine Part D, CI-3, CI-4, and the scenario catalog; `RS-CH7-FC-001`, `RS-CH7-FC-002`, and `RS-CH8-SMB-002` read-with lines); dated records (earlier moves here, evidence, archive) keep the numbers they were written with. The reasoning and the draft are in the project note `claude/ch07-section3-restructure-draft.md`.

**Thirtieth move (Chapter Seven: Purpose, Scope, and Owner Boundary folded into §1; sections renumbered; 2026-10-16).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): with "when required" now first, the old §2 mostly repeated it, so it is folded into §1. §1 keeps its title, *When System and Institutional Charters Are Required*, and now holds, in order: a lead paragraph (what a System Charter or Institutional Charter is, required at Class L or higher and voluntary at Class P); §1.1 Who must publish; §1.2 Which classes are covered; §1.3 Class P: a choice (the Class P block from the old §2, including the nine-domain test and the four-seat floor, which stay here as the home of the Class P threshold); §1.4 When it is due (was §1.3); §1.5 If a System Charter or Institutional Charter is missing (was §1.4); §1.6 The process at a glance (the chart, its reader guidance, and the no-permission paragraph). **Dropped as duplicates:** the old §2 "Required at Class L or higher" paragraph and process list (the chart and §1.2 carry them), "Class L or higher (Classes A, B, C, and L)", and "Depth scales with class" (owned by *Scaling* in §3). **Renumbered:** old §3 to §10 are now §2 to §9 (No Licence §2; What They Must State §3; Charter Role Map §4; Formation Check §5; When a Holder Fails the Check §6; Amendment and Review §7; What They Cannot Do §8; Relationship to Other Chapters §9), and the chart labels follow. **No operative change.** Links and cites were retargeted with a slug-keyed single-pass rewrite in Chapters Zero, One, Five, Six Part B, Eight, Nine Part D, Ten, Eleven, Thirteen, Fourteen, CI-3, CI-4, CI-13, the Institution Foundation Kit, and the scenario catalog (including read-with lines in `RS-CH7-FC-001`, `RS-CH7-FC-002`, `RS-CH8-SMB-002`); dated records (earlier moves here, TODO and MEMLOG history, evidence, archive) keep the numbers they were written with. Translations are paused and still use the old numbering.

**Thirty-first move (Chapter Seven: class overview in §1; §2 retitled; 2026-10-16).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) §1 gains a five-bullet overview of the system impact classes (A, B, C, L, P, one line each, from the CS-3 Part B posture strip) after the lead paragraph and before §1.1, with a pointer to [CS-3.8](corpus_systems/cs_09.310_system_impact_classifications.md#cs-9310-8-system-impact-classifications) for the full definitions. (2) §2 is retitled from *No Licence, and Two Narrow Limits* to *The Right to Form, and Three Narrow Limits* (anchor `2-the-right-to-form-and-three-narrow-limits`), because "licence" now also means professional licensure and the section holds three limits, not two: the two Article XI-E gates on a named sentient's authority (an outstanding standing lock; a forum-ordered Interim Hold) and the practice licence or practice clearance for a named act. The section's lead, plain-terms line, and practice paragraph are reworded to say so; the practice requirement is stated to be no gate on an Operating Party and outside the formation check, and §5 and §6 now call the two it applies "gates", matching Article XI-E. **No operative change.** Live links, link text, the reading-order line, and the scenario catalog read-with line follow the new slug; dated records (earlier moves, TODO and MEMLOG history, evidence, archive) and the paused translations keep the old title. Same word renamed in the same pass: Chapter Nine Part D §11.3 *No licence and no gate* to *No permission and no gate* (anchor `113-no-permission-and-no-gate`; its bullet *No licensing back door* to *No back door*), the *No licence* bullet in Domain-Specific Materiality to *No gate*, and the Chapter Eight *No licence and no trap* bullet to *No permission and no trap*. Ordinary uses of "not a licence" meaning "not a permission" are left as they are. Article XI-E's own wording is unchanged.
