Also in this pass (Chapter Seven): the *Joint professional practice* bullet in §1.1 is split into sub-bullets (what is adopted, adoption notice, class), with the separate "must state the §3 minimum" sub-bullet folded into "which must cover those acts" as redundant; §1.2 and §1.3 are trimmed of repeats (the Class P pointer and burden sentence, the crossing-the-line bullets, the second Charter Role Map and Materially Binding Act mentions) with the Class P burden sentence dropped from §1.3 (it read as a duty on every Class P party) and replaced by an inverted frame: anyone inside a Class P Operating Party who notices it crossing a line has a duty to report the crossing, as Protected Reporting. The duty is new text in Domain-Specific Materiality (*What must hold*, bullet *Those inside report a crossing*): it is a duty to say what one sees, not to investigate or prove; the report names a specific crossing in a named domain and goes on the existing *Challenge route*; it does not require acting against one's own safety or the Rights Floor; it does not move the burden, which stays with the Operating Party once a crossing is named; a developing, dependent, or unable-to-leave sentient may report and is protected but is not held to the duty, and silence is judged against what the sentient knew, could safely do, and the sentient's power in the group, never as a ground to punish a sentient who was dependent or afraid; the duty reaches only the Class P to Class L lines in this entry, with moves between higher classes left to CS-3; and *Primary failure* gains "ignoring, burying, or punishing an insider's report of a crossing". Mandatory lock (custodian, same session): Chapter Eleven §5.5 already makes its special locks mandatory when the verified trigger is met, and the *Institutional Authority Standing Lock* trigger already covered retaliating against a protected reporter; it now also covers suppressing, burying, or refusing to receive a Protected Reporting report through the institution, including an insider's report of a crossing, and the *Primary failure* line points to it. Passive silence by an insider is not a trigger. Five additions to the same Domain-Specific Materiality bullet (custodian: "yes" to all), which is now split into sub-bullets (who and what; where it goes; protection; records kept; a crossing is not a wrong; suppression; contribution; limits; the burden stays): (1) a direct route to a forum or an adopter-designated receiver when the report is about the Operating Party's own conduct and going to it first would be unsafe or would let it control the first answer; (2) the Operating Party preserves the records a received report names and may not alter, hide, or delete them; (3) crossing a line is not itself a violation, and hiding it or leaving the duties undone once known is the failure; (4) a good-faith report may be recognized as contribution under the Constitutional Alignment descriptor, and a report that shows no crossing is not a mark against the reporter; (5) a report made to harass or known to be false is outside Good Faith and has no protection. Scope receiving office (custodian, same session: an administrative arm of the Institutional Forum family, and of local forums for local community matters, that officially receives and verifies System Charter and Institutional Charter documentation): new **CF-5.5** (*Institutional and local registrars*) in `corpus_forum/cf_13.415_routing_operations_transfer_certification_representative_treatment.md`, an administrative arm of the CF-5.2 intake triage body. It receives a System Charter or Institutional Charter with its Charter Role Map and formation check statement, checks completeness only against the Chapter Seven §3 minimum, gives a dated receipt, keeps a public register, and passes §1.5 requests and insiders' crossing reports to intake without judging them. It grants no permission, rules on no class or content, runs no formation check, charges no fee, and is no gate: silence is receipt after seven days, a wrong-office lodging is forwarded with its date, and an unavailable office is met by open publication with notice. Chapter Seven §1.4 gains a *Where it is lodged* bullet, and the *Where it goes* sub-bullet in Domain-Specific Materiality now routes an insider's direct report through the registrar, which replaces the undefined "receiver the adopting instrument designates". New duty to lodge is an operative change in Chapter Seven; the office itself is implementation. Integrity registrar, first drafted as the audit results receiving office (custodian, same session: "something similar for the integrity office to receive financial and resource audit results"): new **CF-5.6** (*Integrity registrar*) in the same CF-5 file, an administrative arm of the Integrity Forum family's intake triage body. It receives the result of a financial audit (funds held, income and spending, reserves, fees, compensation and reward arrangements) or a resource audit (allocation and stewardship of shared or scarce resources, with CS-9) that an adopting instrument, a certification, a forum order, a CI-7.2 trigger, or the audited party's own CI-7.1 controls call for. It adds no duty to audit. It checks completeness only (audited party, period and scope, method, date, who commissioned and paid, auditor ties, findings, the audited party's response), gives a dated receipt, tags access classes under CF-15.3 with public as the default, keeps a register, tells the Integrity Forums handling the audited party's certification, and passes possible crossings, diversion or concealment, undisclosed conflicts, or misaligned rewards to intake unjudged. It does not grade audits, order or scope them, or decide independence or sufficiency. Safeguards add no-shelving (entered in the register within seven days) and Protected Reporting for an auditor who reports refused access. CI-7.2 gains a pointer bullet. Implementation text only; no core change. Renamed (custodian: the name should be more concrete, and the Chapter Seven bullet repeated it): the offices are now *registrars*, named for their forum family, Institutional registrar, local registrar, and Integrity registrar, and the Chapter Seven bullet names the registrar once with the CF-5.5 cite. A stale "Chapter Seven §6" italic cite in that trigger (left by the renumbering) is corrected to §5. The *Ordinary community activity* bullet, the challenge route, and CS-3.8 are unchanged. **Operative change; custodian to confirm;** and §3 gains one paragraph, *Acts that need a practice licence or practice clearance*, stating that a System Charter or Institutional Charter may not claim, and the Chartered Party may not exercise, such an act unless each performer holds the licence or clearance or acts as a supervised learner, with a one-sentence pointer in §2. The *When Class P stops being voluntary* bullet moves from §1.2 to §1.3 as a short paragraph ("The choice ends in two cases"), since it is about Class P's choice and not about which classes are covered; "voluntarily" is dropped as redundant, matching CI-3 ("when the operator asserts constitutional compliance"). The new §3 paragraph makes explicit what Article XI-E and CI-4.7 already imply; it adds no new gate on forming or operating. Chapter Seven §1.5 *A repeat request* (custodian, same session: "please clarify", twice): the sentence "A repeat request while a response or a finding stands is answered by pointing to it" leaves "If it is missed" and becomes its own bullet. A second request about the same Chartered Party, made while its earlier response or a finding on it still stands, starts no new clock and is answered by pointing to that response or finding. It stands until the Chartered Party's activity changes materially or new evidence about a crossing is shown, as the Chapter Nine Part D §14.2 reopening triggers describe, which are now two sub-bullets; a request that shows either is a new request and starts the week to respond and the thirty days to publish again from its date. The rule carries over to a response the stand-until-something-changes test that Domain-Specific Materiality (*Challenge route*) already applies to a finding. Custodian to confirm that a response, and not only a finding, stands on that test. Follow-up edits to that bullet (custodian, same session): the closing sentence (a request that shows either trigger is a new request and restarts the clocks) is no longer a third sub-bullet and now stands as a paragraph under the two trigger sub-bullets, opening "Either of the above is not a repeat." The paragraph after the Chapter Seven §1.6 chart ("Whether a System Charter or Institutional Charter is required ... nobody needs permission to form or operate ... The only gates are two narrow limits") is removed as redundant: the chart node, the §1.6 plain-terms line, the §2 lead and plain-terms line, and the §1.5, §4 and §6 defect statements already say each part of it. **No operative change.**

**Thirty-second move (Chapter Seven §2 and §3 wording; Charter Pointer defined; 2026-10-16).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) §2's third limit is set out as bullets, with the practice licence and practice clearance each tied to the act that risks harm if done badly, so the text cannot be read as permission to cause harm. (2) §3's list of what a System Charter states drops the periodic-review cadence, because the class sets it (CS-3 gives the System Classification Record a recertification cadence scaled to class); the list is now nine items, ends with Amendment authority, and sets out the binding stakeholder-choice item as three sub-bullets. The Chapter Fourteen pointer rule now cites item 8. Scaling and Periodic review say the class sets the cadence. (3) New Chapter Five definition **Charter Pointer** (Continuity band, after Charter Role Map): the reference a System Charter or Institutional Charter uses to name another published instrument or provision instead of copying it, which must resolve to the current published version. Charter Pointer replaces bare "pointer" wherever it means that in Chapter Zero, Chapter Five (System Charter and Institutional Charter entries), Chapter Seven, Chapter Fourteen §4.3, the Def.P2 entry, CI-3.6, and CF-5.5. The corpus-wide use of pointer for drafting cross-references under the non-relocation rule is unchanged, and the definition says so. Directory, band list, measurement seed, and the lexical audit allowlist are updated. (4) The equivalent-instrument rule is stated once, in Chapter Eighteen §4 (*A scope instrument kept under another name*), because it is about an adopter's existing instrument counting as its System Charter or Institutional Charter; the Chapter Seven §3 paragraph moved there and Chapter Seven §3 only points to it in its Trace. The repeats are removed: the marketing-copy and mission-statement bullets and the "or an equivalent published scope instrument" test in the Chapter Five System Charter and Institutional Charter entries, the same parenthetical in Domain-Specific Materiality, Public Oversight Baseline Disclosure, Chapter Nine Parts A and B, CS-2, and CJS-3, and the one in CI-3.6. An equivalent instrument that Chapter Seven accepts is identified as the System Charter or Institutional Charter, so those places need only name the System Charter. (5) CI-3.6 is split into eight subsections, CI-3.6.1 to CI-3.6.8 (owner boundary; who must publish; minimum fields; Institutional Charter additions; formation check statement template; periodic review; anti-evasion; systems and businesses), with the standing-record custody field and the Charter Role Map field set out as sub-bullets; "this subsection" now reads "this section" where it meant the whole of CI-3.6. (6) Unified review cadence (decision, custodian, asked in session): the forum outer bounds in Chapter Thirteen §6 are retimed to A 1 week, B 2 weeks, C 4 weeks, L 8 weeks, P 12 weeks (they were 1 week, 3 weeks, 2 months, 4 months, 6 months). The same figures are the review unit for certified systems and Institutions of the matching class: a full certification check at least every 13 units (13, 26, 52, 104, 156 weeks) and a forum-enforced financial, resource, or special audit at the midpoint (6.5 units, completed no later than the midpoint in whole days: 45, 91, 182, 364, 546), so certification checks and audits alternate at even spacing. Provisional certification keeps its shorter interval with the audit at the midpoint of the interval in force; Class P is covered only where the body publishes a System Charter or Institutional Charter; adopted instruments may set shorter intervals and never longer ones; reopening for cause is unaffected, and the next dates run from the latest completed certification pass. The numbers live only in Chapter Thirteen §6 (*Review cadence*). Chapter Nine Parts A, B, and D, CS-3, CI-3.6.6, and Chapter Seven point to it, and CF-5.6 points to it for the duty to audit that it did not itself create. The CF-11.3.1 stage windows, the Tier B milestone tables in the application vignettes, the overview diagram, the steward owner-clock index, and the steward-entry worked example are rescaled to match; the rescaled stage windows are a proportional proposal for the steward to review. (7) The cadence is removed from the Chapter Five System Charter and Institutional Charter entries and from CI-3.6.3, which now has eleven fields; later fields move up one (standing-record custody is field 10, the Charter Role Map field 11), and the references in CI-3, CI-4, CI-8, Chapter Ten, the Chapter Five participation band, the steward entry doors, the forum foundation kit, and the operator notes are renumbered. (8) CF-11.3.1 stage windows become a target budget (decision, custodian, asked in session): the five stage targets run in order and add up to the tier's outer bound, in shares of 1 : 2 : 1 : 2 : 1 (Tier A 1, 2, 1, 2, 1 days; B, C, L, P the same shares times 2, 4, 8, 12), with evidence preservation inside the intake window. They are targets only: a forum that runs one stage past its target stays compliant if integrated resolution finishes within the outer bound, which is the compliance line. A complexity-extended matter keeps its own intake window, uses the next slower tier's later-stage targets, and has the next slower tier's outer bound. This replaces the earlier proportional rescale, whose stages did not add up to the bound, and the Chapter Thirteen §6 pipeline-milestones bullet, the Outer-bound rule, and the application vignettes are worded to match. (9) Audit shift (decision, custodian, asked in session): a body may move its audit earlier, never later, for convenience, by up to a quarter of the certification interval before the midpoint (22, 45, 91, 182, 273 days for Classes A, B, C, L, P), on published notice with reasons given to the forum in advance; only the audit moves, never later than the midpoint and never the certification dates, and it still happens in the cycle. A shift covers one cycle and may be used in at most one cycle in three, and is not available during a reopened or fresh review or with an unremedied earlier audit finding. The quarter-interval limit and the one-in-three rate are my proposed values for the steward to confirm. **Operative changes needing the steward's sign-off:** the cadence removal, the tighter outer bounds, and the new audit duty are changes to core text.

**Thirty-third move (Chapter Seven §3 and §4 plain-language rewrite; certification verifier rule; operator and stakeholder standing records; 2026-10-17).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): (1) Chapter Seven §3's practice licence and practice clearance paragraph and its Scaling paragraph are set out as bullets in plainer wording, and §4 (Charter Role Map and Standing-Record Custody) is rewritten as short bullets with sub-bullets. §4 now says what the System Charter or Institutional Charter does with the map and points to Chapter Eight §§2, 3, 5 and 6 for what makes a placement valid. Its Class P bullet is removed because §1.3 covers it (§1.3 now also says a Class P party may keep a map with a voluntary System Charter or Institutional Charter or publish it on its own). The *Two layers of change* bullet is now *Two kinds of change*, and §5's cite of it is updated. (2) New rule *Outside the operator* in Chapter Nine Part B §6.1.1, mirrored in Part D §13.1: everyone who holds a verify-or-authorize seat in a certification is outside the operator's and proponent's Material Control Line and has no interest the outcome decides; the operator cannot choose, appoint, fund through a channel it controls, or remove its verifier; no one who works for it, is owned by it, or answers to it may hold the seat; a sentient who cannot meet this steps aside (recusal under Chapter Thirteen). Chapter Seven no longer restates the rule. (3) Chapter Five's Standing Record entry gains two subtypes, **operator standing record** (subject is a Chartered Party, its chartered scope, or a system it stewards) and **stakeholder standing record** (subject is a sentient or institution that uses, relies on, or is affected by those systems), and a failure line. Chapter Ten §3.7 gains *Two kinds of standing record by subject*: for an operator standing record the exclusion of the Chartered Party's whole Material Control Line is standing; for a stakeholder standing record it is decided record by record, by the Chartered Party's interest as claimant, alleging party, or affected party. Chapter Seven §4's custody bullet uses the terms. Operative wording in (2) and (3) needs steward sign-off. Not done: the CI-3.6 standing-record custody field is not yet updated for the two kinds. Derived files regenerated (ai_corpus indexes, plain-terms edition, definition lookup, boundary chunks, print pack); `make regression` passes.

**Thirty-fourth move (time-window names, TIME-NAME-01; 2026-10-17).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the corpus adopts a naming rule for time windows (window, target, bound, term, interval, period, span), recorded in this file under *Time-window names (TIME-NAME-01)* and enforced by a new rule in `make lexical-vocabulary-audit` (`avoid-window-clock-names`). Named phrases were renamed in the owner files and nothing else changed: tier clocks, response clocks, restore-challenge clocks, and the pipeline clocks became **tier windows** and **restore-challenge windows**; the Chapter Thirteen §6 bullets became **tier outer bound**, **stage targets**, **certification interval**, **audit midpoint**, **audit advance allowance**, and **provisional interval** (the *Review cadence* bullet and its anchor are unchanged); *recertification clock* and *recertification cadence* became **certification interval** and **provisional interval**; the Article XXVIII-A *transition clock* became the **transition window** (anchor `xxviii-a-existing-instantiations-transition-window`); CF-11.3.1 *Target windows and timing floors* became *Stage targets and timing floors* (anchor `cf-1131-stage-targets-and-timing-floors`) and the intake window became the intake target; in Chapter Seven §1.6 and §6 the bullets are named **response window** (a week) and **resolution window** (thirty days), with a **challenge extension** (§1.6) and, in CI-13.3, a **good-faith extension**; and "the response window of the matching tier" (Interim Hold review, CI-13.10, CI-3.6.5) now reads as the matching tier's window. No number, duty, or deadline changed. Not done: Chapter Five "time window" (to become *record span*), the CJS list that gives every window a binding name, and the translations, which still carry the earlier wording. The proposed `Cure Window` is not used because the lexical audit bans that word; the name is *resolution window*.

**Thirty-fifth move (time-window names: record span, CJS-0.4 list, leftovers; 2026-10-17).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the follow-through on the thirty-fourth move. (1) Chapter Five's "time window" (a record's covered time, not a deadline) became **record span** in the Standing Record entry, the certification record definitions, Chapter Five continuity and accountability bands, the Preamble, Chapter Nine Parts B and D, Chapter Ten, CF-9.8, the overview guides, and the Chapter Six assessment schema text. The Standing Record entry now says in a sentence that a record span is the time a record covers, is not a deadline, and starts no window. `avoid-window-clock-names` now also rejects "time window". (2) New non-operative section **CJS-0.4 Time-window names** in the CJS registry annex (anchor `cjs-04-time-window-names`): the kind words, three usage rules, and a table of every named window with its kind, what it times, and the owner that sets it. It has no numbers, so it adds no second clock table (Chapter Thirteen §6.1). It lists only names the owner files use. To match, CI-4.7 now names the **host replacement window** and CI-13.5 the **order term**. The urgency scales are described, and no mapping from notice or correction urgency levels to tiers is added. (3) Leftovers: the CS-4 validator wording ("tier outer bound"), the stale family key `article_xxiv_c_tier_outer_bounds` is now `article_xxvi_c_tier_outer_bounds` in the validator, schema, fixtures, and steward owner clock index, one fixture sentence ("restore-challenge window"), and two scenario-catalog phrases ("Resolution window", "thirty-day resolution window"). Not done: the translations, which still carry the earlier wording; giving a length to the qualitative windows or stating why they stay open ("promptly", "reasonable"), which is an owner decision; dated history rows (PRE_PUBLICATION_SPEC, VISION, MEMLOG, evaluations), which keep the wording they used; and the stale tier-bounds row in the project's `dissolution-procedure-draft.md` §2 and §12 (it should read 1, 2, 4, 8, and 12 weeks), which lives outside the repository.

**Thirty-sixth move (time-window scale, review cadence, Emergency level, restricted information office; 2026-10-17).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) The tier outer bounds are written in days: 7, 14, 30, 60, and 90 (Tiers C, L, and P were 28, 56, and 84). Questions 1 and 3 in Tiers C, L, and P became 9, 18, and 27 days; intake, Question 2, and remedy are unchanged. (2) The review unit is retired: certification is every 3 months (Class A), 6 months (B), 1 year (C), 2 years (L), and 3 years (P), the audit midpoint is halfway, and a counting rule for months and years was added; the **Review cadence** anchor is kept and the CJS-0.4 row and the interval-kind entry no longer list *review unit*. (3) Time-scale levels were added to Chapter Thirteen §6 (`#time-scale-levels`) and CF-11.3.1: Level 2 (Coordinated) and Level 3 (Heavy) for Tiers A and B on a showing, and an **Emergency level** for Tier A with a 3-day protective-step bound and a 3-day restore-challenge window. (4) Restricted evidence does not slow the clock: CF-11.2.3 and CF-11.3.3 were changed and an Anti-delay floor bullet was added. (5) New **CF-5.8 Restricted information office**, with a carve-out in CF-9.5, the review-order record in CF-15, and a default for the emergency-substitution window in CI-3.6. Not done: lengths for "promptly" windows, translations, regenerated indexes (see the verification pass). Evaluation: the project document *Urgency × complexity matrix for time windows*.

**Thirty-seventh move (prompt review: "promptly" aligned with the time windows; 2026-10-17).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the follow-through on the thirty-sixth move for the word "promptly". (1) New **Prompt review** rule and **prompt-review window** in Chapter Thirteen §6 (anchor `#prompt-review`): where a measure, order, hold, stay, or interim decision must be reviewed promptly, an independent reviewer begins review no later than the intake target of the matching tier (24 hours for Tier A, and 2, 4, 8, and 12 calendar days for B, C, L, and P). It is a compliance line, not a stage target. A measure with no classified matter is counted at Tier A. At the Emergency level the window is Tier A's 24 hours, inside the 3-day protective-step bound, so the start of independent review is due sooner than the other protective steps. (2) Sites changed: Chapter Thirteen §5 interim relief, Interim Hold, §6.1 restrictions and Review and notice; CF-11.3.1; Chapter Five accountability band (Stay); Articles VII-C and XIV; Chapter Nine Part B (reliance limits, stay authority); CF-4, CF-6.2.2, CF-13, CF-14; CI-13 order review; the three CJS-3 emergency entries; CS-11.4. (3) Prompt intake and preservation now mean the intake target (Chapter Thirteen §1, the Chapter Five Evidence Preservation assessment, CF-8, the CI-13 open-or-join duty). Prompt notice now means notice with the measure, or inside the restore-challenge window where deferral is strictly necessary (Article XXVIII-D, CI-14). (4) CJS-0.4 gained the **Prompt-review window** row and a rewritten last paragraph; TIME-NAME-01 gained the window name and a rule. Not done: the remaining "promptly" uses (principles, plain-terms glosses, the drafting-error carve-out, record-update duties in CS-2 and CS-3, the CF-5 "prompt way to challenge" wording), "reasonable period" uses, an audit that flags bare "promptly", and the translations. Evaluation: the project document *"Promptly" and the new time windows*.

**Thirty-eighth move (remaining "promptly" uses that fit the windows; 2026-10-17).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the second pass on the thirty-seventh move. (1) The plain-terms glosses in CF-13, CF-14, and CJS-3 (the Disclosure and Participation entry) now match their operative lines ("inside the prompt-review window"). (2) CF-5's "prompt way to challenge" and "prompt contest path" and the Chapter Five Accountability measurement text on "prompt routing" now read "inside the intake target", matching Chapter Thirteen §1. (3) The record-update duties in CS-2 §8.5 and CS-3 §7.5 (and the CS-3 pointer) now run "no later than the intake target of the tier that matches the system's class", counted from when changed conditions are recognized, using the class-to-tier presumption in Chapter Nine Part B §9.2. (4) The CJS-0.4 last paragraph was updated to match. Left for the next pass: the drafting-error carve-out (Chapter Twelve Part A), the Diligence credit (Chapter Ten), the Review and Correction Duty (Chapter Five continuity band), the Article XVIII-C gloss, and "reasonable period" uses.

**Thirty-ninth move (CUL-1 is the Emergency level; 2026-10-17).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) In CJS-3.15 the first correction urgency level, **CUL-1**, is the **Emergency level** of Chapter Thirteen §6 *Time-scale levels*. An issue is CUL-1 on the Emergency level's objective trigger (imminent loss of life, serious injury, or irreversible rights loss, verified or credibly alleged, not self-created). Containment screening starts inside the 6-hour intake target and the protective steps finish inside the 3-day bound. "Where feasible" qualifies the safe-mode coupling and never the time. A containment that is a measure in force against someone follows the §6.1 notice, restore-challenge, and prompt-review rules. Where a CUL issue is also before a forum, the matter's tier window governs, and a CUL level never changes a dispute's tier. (2) CF-11.2.3 and CF-11.3.1 point to CUL-1, and the CJS-0.4 *Urgency scales* paragraph says CUL-1 is the Emergency level. (3) The Chapter Five Stewardship Defect exclusion now reads "corrected inside the tier-appropriate window under Review and Correction Duty" (that duty is not a CUL duty; it already runs on tier-appropriate windows). Not done: CUL-2 and lower levels (none are defined in the corpus), the NUL scale (NUL-1 still says "without unjustified delay"), and any mapping of other levels to tiers. Chapter Thirteen is unchanged, so this is a Lane C change.

**Fortieth move (CUL and NUL levels take the tier columns; 2026-10-17).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the follow-through on the thirty-ninth move for the rest of the correction and notice scales. (1) **CUL-1** to **CUL-4** (CJS-3.15) now take the scope of harm and the columns of Tiers A, B, C, and L (Tier P for an issue contained in a private unit). The **containment window** is the column's intake target and the **correction window** is its outer bound; both are compliance lines, and going past the correction window needs a documented extension under Continuation discipline. CUL-1 runs at the Emergency level where its conditions are met and otherwise in the Tier A column, which revises the thirty-ninth move, where CUL-1 was defined by the Emergency trigger alone. (2) **NUL-1** to **NUL-3** (CJS-3.6) take Tiers A, B, and C, the same columns as CUL-1 to CUL-3. The **notice window** is the column's intake target. For NUL-1 the **declaration window** is the outer bound, and Type S or protected-investigation constraints narrow what the declaration says and never when it is made. "Without unjustified delay" and "as soon as containment posture allows" are retired. **NUL-4** stays a routine attestation interval with no tier. (3) None of these is a tier classification, and the slower complexity levels do not apply. (4) New names in CJS-0.4 and TIME-NAME-01: containment window, correction window, notice window, declaration window. The CF-11.2.3 and CF-11.3.1 pointers and the CJS-0.4 *Urgency scales* paragraph match. Existing adopter-published NUL windows longer than the new defaults must be shortened. Chapter Thirteen is unchanged, so this is a Lane C change. These pin previously open-ended duties, so they only tighten; no lengthening needs a Test 1 note. Not done: translations.

**Forty-first move (data-types record on the certification interval; "reasonable" periods at the Tier B bound; 2026-10-18).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) CS-2 §8.5 drops the intake-target deadline added in the thirty-eighth move. The System Data Types Record is checked on the system's regular certification interval, with the re-evaluation cadence being that interval; retype triggers stay in §5.2. CS-3 §7.5 matches: the System Classification Record is also checked on the certification interval, and the intake-target bullet and its CS-3 pointer are gone. (2) Every "reasonable period" or "reasonable timeframe" in owner text (Article II-E data deletion; Article XVIII-C and CS-5 sandbox transition; CS-9 start of transition after missed reauthorization; the CS-4 recovery property for a Class C steward; the CS-3 Class L intervention timeframe) now reads **14 days**, the Tier B outer bound, whatever class or tier the operator or system holds, as a ceiling. No new window name. CJS-0.4 *Windows with no length* matches. Articles II-E and XVIII-C are core text, so this is Lane D. Test 1: the deletion right shortens from "reasonable"; the sandbox-transition and CS-9 figures are demanding (completing a transition in 14 days) and should be checked for feasibility. Not done: translations.

**Forty-second move (time-reference audit, TIME-REF-01; 2026-10-18).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): a new audit, `tools/time_reference_audit.py`, makes every time reference show its logic. (1) A time reference is a number with a unit or a vague stand-in ("reasonable period", "promptly", "as soon as feasible", "without undue delay"). (2) A **scaled** reference must name a bound kind and the tier or class that sets it in the same sentence (a table row is read with its header row and lead-in line). A **fixed** reference must use a registered window name that owns its length. (3) The vocabulary is `tools/time_reference_registry.json`: bound kinds, basis patterns, fixed periods with owners, and exempt contexts (day-equivalent calibration; quoted mentions of retired phrases). New fixed lengths are registered there first. (4) References that predate the rule are in `tools/time_reference_baseline.json`, keyed by file and sentence text. The list only shrinks: new or edited non-compliant references fail, and entries that become compliant are reported as stale. `make time-reference-backlog` lists them. (5) Wired into `make regression` with `tools/test_time_reference_audit.py`. No corpus text changed. Lane: tooling only.

**Forty-third move (CS-4 rewritten in plain language; header numbering fixed; 2026-10-18).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) `corpus_systems/cs_14.305_critical_system_stewardship.md` is rewritten for general readers: shorter sentences, bullets where items are parallel, and a new CS-4.1 *Purpose and scope*. (2) Headings run CS-4.1 to CS-4.20 in order; the old section 10 is split into CS-4.10 with sub-sections CS-4.10.1 to CS-4.10.8, and the former section 11 content is folded into CS-4.10, so risk governance is now CS-4.11. (3) Headings renamed to match their content: sections 6, 7, 10 and 20. (4) Links to the old anchors were retargeted in 20+ files (core, implementation, evaluation, CONTRIBUTING, VISION), now using the heading slug `#cs-4103-inspectable-attributable-action` because HTML-ANCHOR-LINK-01 rejects custom `<a id>` targets; the steward-door lockstep check follows. (5) `tools/section_glosses.json` keys renamed to match. Historical `evaluation/results/*` and translations are untouched (stale until the next translation pass). No obligation changed except the one 14-day Tier B bound at CSS-C, already recorded in the forty-first move.

**Forty-fourth move ("promptly" leftovers resolved; 2026-10-18).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) CI-13.8 emergency containment: independent review now begins inside the **prompt-review window**, not "the Tier A window" (24 hours at Tier A, not the whole 7 days); a measure in force is a kind A site that the word search of the thirty-seventh move missed. (2) The Article XVIII-C plain-terms line says "within 14 days, the Tier B outer bound", matching the operative line. (3) New TIME-NAME-01 rule and CJS-0.4 usage rule **Promptly is not a length**: text that says promptly and names no window states a duty of diligence, implies no tier, level, or window, and does not excuse delay. CJS-0.4 *Windows with no length* now says why three uses stay open (the Article V Timeliness aim, the Chapter Ten Diligence credit, the Chapter Twelve Part A good-faith drafting carve-out). (4) TIME-REF-01 gains `qualitative_on_purpose` in `tools/time_reference_registry.json` (file, sentence text, reason; vague wording only, never a number), covering the Article V aim and the two drafting-carve-out lines; the baseline drops from 97 to 93 entries. The bare adjective "prompt" is **not** added to the audit, because it is also a verb ("measurable impact prompt a …") and part of the registered name *prompt-review window*. (5) Two Chapter Thirteen index labels drop "prompt" ("contestable threshold access"). (6) Test 1 self-check for the time-window changes written at `evidence/2026-10-18/time-window-test-1-self-check.md`, with the Tier L and P sentence. (7) Not done, by decision: Chapter Five definitions for *complexity level* and *Emergency level* (the owners are Chapter Thirteen §6 and CF-11.3.1, and CJS-0.4 holds the names), and translations (updated after the announcement). Lane C and D, not enforced pre-announcement.

**Forty-fifth move (Document at Action; CF-11.3.2 "as soon as lawfully feasible" retired; 2026-10-18).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) New stewardship principle **Chapter One §16.4 Document at Action** (`core_01.010_stewardship_capacity_principles.md`, after §16.3 Openness Aspiration): the act makes its own record, by automated capture wherever feasible; information no lawful restriction holds back is available when the act is taken; restricted material still leaves a visible marker, a stated ground, and a release path; the burden of recording sits on the system, not the steward. It sets no deadline, does not open inner life (§17.3 still governs), and does not outrank Safety, Truth, privacy, security, or justified confidentiality. It adds no new defined term and no Chapter Five entry, as §17.3 does not; it leans on Auditability, Transparency, Attributable Action, Contestability, Materiality, Dependency, Avoidable Burden, and Surveillance Boundary. (2) **CF-11.3.2** applies it: reasoned records are published when the action is taken, except the restricted part; reasons are recorded as part of the decision; where full reasons cannot responsibly be ready (an emergency interim measure), the action is published at once with its stated ground and a marker, and the fuller record is due no later than the matter's tier outer bound; the four delayed-publication items exist from the moment of action; a lawful order that bars even the marker is recorded at the time and the item is treated as restricted evidence under CF-11.3.3. (3) **CS-4.10.3** gains a pointer bullet (**Made at action**), and the CJS-0.4 rule **Promptly is not a length** says record duties that ask for a record "at the time of the act" follow §16.4 and that it is a design default, not a length. (4) The TIME-REF-01 baseline drops from 93 to 92 entries (the CF-11.3.2 sentence is gone). (5) Not done, by decision: the "as lawfully feasible" in CF-12 continuity-mode communications (a feasibility qualifier for crisis conditions, not a time phrase the audit flags); Chapter Five definition; translations. Lane D for §16.4 (core text); Lane C for CF-11.3.2, CS-4.10.3, and CJS-0.4. Test 1: the changes tighten (earlier availability, no open-ended timing), recorded in `evidence/2026-10-18/time-window-test-1-self-check.md`.

**Forty-sixth move (Document at Action pointers; CF-12.6 updated; 2026-10-18).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) Pointers to Chapter One §16.4 Document at Action added on the accountability side: the Accountability row of the Chapter One aim table, the Accountability leg Trace and the Attributable Action Trace in Chapter Five (non-operative Trace links, so no definition changes), and §16.4's own Trace now names the accountability leg. (2) **CF-12.6** no longer says "as lawfully feasible": the activation record under CF-12.3 is made by the activation itself, the facts parties need are published or communicated from it when the mode takes effect except any part a lawful restriction holds back (which still leaves a marker, as in CF-11.3.2), and where digital systems are impaired the CF-12.5 fallback carries the record and the communication. No new channel duty is added. Lane D for the Chapter One and Chapter Five link edits; Lane C for CF-12.6. Test 1: tightens (an open feasibility qualifier becomes a stated default with an existing fallback).

**Forty-seventh move (Chapter Seven §1.3 split; response and resolution windows take the tier scale; 2026-10-18).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) Chapter Seven §1.3 *Class P: a choice* is two sections: **§1.3 What Class P is** (the line, the private unit, the duty to report, the four-seat floor) and **§1.4 Class P: optional charters, and when they become required** (the voluntary route and the two events that end it). The old §1.4 to §1.6 are now **§1.5 When a System Charter or Institutional Charter is due**, **§1.6 If a System Charter or Institutional Charter is missing**, and **§1.7 The process at a glance**, with every inbound link and generated file updated. (2) The **response window** and the **resolution window** are no longer fixed at 7 and 30 days. Both take the Tier that matches the Chartered Party's class (Class A, B, C, or L takes Tier A, B, C, or L; a class in question counts at Tier L; Class P takes Tier P where CI-13.3 applies). The response window is the matching tier's intake target (24 hours, 2, 4, and 8 calendar days for Tiers A, B, C, and L) and the resolution window is its outer bound (7, 14, 30, and 60 calendar days), both in CF-11.3.1. The good-faith extension in CI-13.3 is up to one more resolution window; the challenge extension in Chapter Seven §1.6 starts a new resolution window when the challenge is decided. Sites changed: Chapter Seven §1.5, §1.6, §1.7 (chart and plain-terms lines), §4, §6; CI-13.3; the Domain-Specific Materiality challenge route in `core_05.050_band_oversight.md`; CJS-0.4 (rule and table); TIME-NAME-01; two regression scenarios. (3) TIME-REF-01 gains `scaled_windows` in `tools/time_reference_registry.json`: the two names move out of `fixed_periods`, count as a bound kind, and no longer excuse a number, so "a 7-day response window" fails; the baseline drops as the Chapter Seven sentences that used bare lengths are rewritten. (4) Effect on lengths: Tiers A and B shorten (response 7 days to 24 hours or 2 days; resolution 30 days to 7 or 14), Tier C keeps 30 days for resolution and shortens response, and **Tier L lengthens** (response 7 to 8 days, resolution 30 to 60). Lane D (core text); Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened review applies; Test 1 self-check still to write in `evidence/2026-10-18/time-window-test-1-self-check.md`. Translations are updated after the announcement.

**Forty-eighth move (time-window lockstep audit, TIME-LOCK-01; 2026-10-18).** Decision (custodian, proposed in session; uncommitted): a new audit, `tools/time_window_lockstep_audit.py`, keeps the downstream copies of the tier windows in step with their owners. (1) Owners: Chapter Thirteen §6 *Tier outer bound* (7, 14, 30, 60, and 90 days) and the CF-11.3.1 stage table (intake targets and the outer-bound row). The audit fails, and never repairs, when the CF-11.3.1 header, its outer-bound row, and the Chapter Thirteen list disagree. (2) Copies, in `tools/time_window_lockstep_registry.json`: the Chapter Seven §1.6 summary table and the sentences in Chapter Thirteen §6 (*Prompt review*), CF-11.3.1, and `guides/CONCEPTUAL_OVERVIEW.md` that list the intake targets. (3) `make time-window-lockstep-audit` checks; `make time-window-lockstep-fix` rewrites drifted copies from the owners, after which the generated files are rebuilt. A registered table or sentence that cannot be found is an error. (4) Wired into `make regression` with `tools/test_time_window_lockstep_audit.py`. No corpus text changed. Lane: tooling only.

**Forty-ninth move (Chapter Seven chart wording; chart section numbers first, VIS-CHART-SECNUM-09; repeat request handled independently; 2026-10-18).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): (1) Chapter Seven §1.6 *A repeat request*: the closing paragraph now reads "A request that shows either of the above is not a repeat. It is a new request, handled separately and independently of the earlier one. It has its own response window and resolution window, counted from its own date, and the earlier response, finding, and windows neither satisfy it nor are changed by it." The old wording said only that the windows "start again", which left room to argue that a new request replaced or restarted an earlier, overdue one. **Operative clarification; custodian to confirm.** (2) The §1.6 chart's first box is reworded as a plain question about whether what the party does materially affects others, with the Class L line beneath it, and the line that nobody needs permission to start moves from that box to the Class P box, where it opens with its section number. **No operative change.** (3) New rule **VIS-CHART-SECNUM-09** (*Chart section-number standard*): a chart box that cites a section puts the number first (`§5 Publish the office-holder statement`), not after the title behind a dash. Enforced by `make chart-section-number-audit` (new `tools/chart_section_number_audit.py` and test, added to `make regression`). Existing charts rewritten to comply: the Chapter Seven §1.6 chart, the two Chapter Nine process charts (Parts A and B), and the Chapter One *Non-Negotiable* box with its copy in `guides/CONCEPTUAL_OVERVIEW.md`. Edge labels are left as they are. **No operative change.**

**Fiftieth move (locks can sit on institutions; a dissolved Chartered Party is barred for good from returning; 2026-10-18).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): Chapter Seven §2 said the two gates in Article XI-E attach to "one named sentient ... never to the Chartered Party itself". That was wrong. A lock can sit on an institution (the Anti-Constitutional Trust Lock already names an institution, successor vehicle, or controlled entity as a subject), and a Chartered Party that a forum has dissolved should stay dissolved. (1) New special lock, the **Dissolution Reformation Lock**, in Chapter Eleven §5.5, set by every final dissolution order under CI-13.7. It sits on the dissolved Chartered Party, its successors, controlled entities, nominee- or shell-held vehicles, and any Operating Party that is one operation with them, and it follows the activity and the control, not the name (§9.4 look-through). It is permanent only where the order records pervasive anti-constitutional conduct or unremedied Irreversible Harm (new **Permanent locks** paragraph in Chapter Eleven §8, with a pointer in Chapter Twelve §4.2); then time, a resolved cause, or a change of holders cannot lift it, and it ends only if the order is set aside on review or material new verified facts show its finding was wrong. Otherwise it is restorable on an independent finding that the cause is resolved, the remedy is delivered, safeguards are in place, and a return is safer than continued exclusion. It bars no sentient from forming a different Operating Party and does not reach the receiver or successor the order names. (2) Article XI-E: the outstanding-lock gate now also attaches to an Operating Party that is itself under a lock. The count stays at two gates and three limits. (3) Chapter Seven §2, §5, §7, the plain-terms lines, and the chart's plain-terms line corrected to match, with a new §5 bullet for a dissolved Chartered Party that returns. (4) CI-13.7 gains item 10 (permanent bar on return) and item 7 is reworded; CI-13.10 says a set-aside order lifts the lock; the CI-13 orientation and rung table say so. (5) Part D, Chapter Five *Standing Lock* and creation-rights limits, and Chapter Eleven §4.2, §8 and §10.6 point to the lock. (6) Article XIX-D and XIX-E said "three special locks" and omitted the Institutional Authority Standing Lock; they now say "the special locks" and list that lock and the new one. **Operative change; custodian to confirm.** Open for the steward: (a) the permanent form of the Dissolution Reformation Lock has no restoration by correction, which departs from the "real way back once the harm is repaired" wording of Article XIX-D and from the Chapter Five line that a lock "temporarily limits" a pathway; review is for error only. (b) Whether a Chartered Party dissolved on a CF-11.4 forum-performance threshold should carry the same permanent bar. (c) Whether a System retired under CI-13.9 should carry it as drafted. (d) Translations were not touched and are behind the English text.

**Fifty-first move (authority is the name for a body that verifies, authorizes, or decides; 2026-10-19).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the corpus mixed *verifier*, *office*, and *authority* for the same job, and the custodian chose *authority*. The rule is recorded in this file under *Names for bodies that verify, hold, and file (TERM-BODY-01)*. (1) **Verifier** is retired as a noun: about 35 uses in Chapters Five, Seven, Eight, Nine, Ten, and Eleven, CF-9, CI-3, CI-4, CI-13, CI-14, CI-22, CJS-0, CS-10, the steward owner-and-clock index, the conceptual overview, and the two copies of the regression scenarios now say *verifying authority* (Chapter Nine already did in two places). New audit rule `avoid-verifier` in `tools/lexical_vocabulary_audit.py` (the files `avoid-instrument` covers, except `evaluation/` and `project/`) and a `verifier` row in `tools/architecture/lexical_guardrails.json`. The CONTRIBUTING.md role *Register verifier* is a project role and keeps its name. (2) **CF-5.8 *Restricted information office* is now *Restricted information authority*** (anchor `#cf-58-restricted-information-authority`); the cites in CF-5, CF-9.5, CF-11, CF-15, the conceptual overview, and the TIME-REF-01 baseline follow. CF-5.5 and CF-5.7 call the Backup Record Authority "a separate authority", and Chapter Thirteen §2.3 says a contest never goes back to "that authority". (3) Chapter Ten §3.7 *Informal and small-scope records* and CI-22: the body that verifies is the *verifying authority*, the body that enters and holds is the *record custodian*, and the CI-22 *records-recognition office* is the *records-recognition authority*. "Any disinterested body with published authority to rely on the record" stays, and then acts as the verifying authority. (4) Left alone: *office* as a unit or post (office-holder, Material Control Line offices, the offices a Charter Role Map places, "standing-record offices"), *registrar*, *record custodian*, *intake triage body*, and the dated records and earlier moves in this log, which keep the names they used. No obligation changed. Not done: translations (paused until after the announcement).

**Fifty-second move (audit registry and category report; 2026-10-19).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the audits were a flat list of 196 `make` targets, with 101 in `make regression` and about 40 more audit-like targets outside it, and no way to see the result by kind. (1) New `tools/architecture/audit_registry.json`: 146 audit targets in eleven categories, each marked `run` (`auto` or `never`) and `writes`. (2) New `tools/audit_report.py` and `make audit-report` / `make audit-report-all`: the targets run quietly, four at a time for the read-only ones, and the result is one row per category with only the failing targets expanded. The gate run took about 26 seconds here, against a few minutes one at a time. `--category`, `--only-failing`, `--json`, `--slowest`, `--list`, and `--strict` are options. (3) `make audit-registry-check` and `make audit-report-test` (15 unit tests) joined `make regression`, which now has 103 targets, so a new audit cannot go unregistered. (4) AGENTS.md and CONTRIBUTING.md point to the report. (5) `make regression` is unchanged as the gate. Found on the way and fixed: `make definition-appropriateness-audit` crashed on a stale call (`audit_cjs3_op_clusters`, renamed `audit_cjs1_op_clusters` in a981323b); it now runs, is registered `run: never` because it is advisory and rewrites its tracked ledger, and its 33 findings were triaged the same day (fifty-fifth move). The audits that write dated files under `evidence/` still do so on every run, and the retention rule bounds what they leave (fifty-third move). Not done: a mode that runs only the categories touching changed files.

**Fifty-third move (audit evidence kept to two runs; 2026-10-19).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): audit evidence under `evidence/<date>/` kept growing (31 dated folders, 23 runs of the Chapter One alignment audit alone). Rule **AUDIT-EVIDENCE-RETENTION-01**: each audit family keeps its newest two runs and the third-to-last is deleted when a new run is written. `tools/prune_audit_evidence.py` does it, driven by the family list in `tools/architecture/audit_evidence_retention.json`; every evidence-writing `make` target calls it as its last step, and `make evidence-prune` runs it alone. Only listed audit output is touched; evaluations, resolutions and other hand-written records in the same folders are not. First run deleted 107 files across 7 families (all still in git history); two links to deleted files (TODO.md and `archive/TODO_RESOLVED_2026-10-18.md`) now point to the newest file by pattern. Ten unit tests in `tools/test_prune_audit_evidence.py` run under `make audit-report-test`.

**Fifty-fourth move (Cluster 1 sign-offs; certification-seat clock; CF-5.7 additions; 2026-10-19).** Decisions (steward, in session): decisions 2, 3 and 4 of Cluster 1 approved as drafted (Chapter Thirteen §2.3 bullet; Chapter Ten §3.7 "sentient acting alone" bullet with the forum fallback; *Segregation of duties* Request row), archived in `archive/TODO_RESOLVED_2026-10-19.md`. Decision 5, option C: a certification seat the Charter Role Map leaves unplaced stays a defect the CI-3.2 lane map covers, but on a clock, namely the CI-13.3 response and resolution windows from the logged notice; a seat still unplaced then makes the System Charter or Institutional Charter invalid on the same terms as a gap in CI-3.6 field 10. Written into Chapter Seven §4.7 (new bullet), Chapter Eight (pointer), CI-3.2 and CI-3.6 field 11. Decision 7: CF-5.7 gained an *Answer* and *Silence* rule, a *Funding and accountability* block, an *Oversight* safeguard and a no-instruction rule, drafted from CF-5.8 and approved the same day. Decision 1, option A with the tidy: the name and default route stay, and Chapter Ten §3.7 now says "any such named route." All Cluster 1 sign-offs are closed; Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened review stays open. Translations are not synced.

**Fifty-fifth move (definition appropriateness audit triaged to zero open findings; 2026-10-19).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): the 33 open findings of `make definition-appropriateness-audit` were cleared. **23 `CJS-TRACE`:** the Chapter One to CJS-3 alignment audit kept its own copy of each cluster's expected Chapter One sections, and the copy still used the numbering from before Chapter One was reorganized, so every cluster looked as if its Trace cited the wrong sections. The CJS-3.1 cluster-map table owns that mapping and the Trace lines already agree with it, so `tools/ch1_cjs3_alignment_audit.py` now reads the table (`load_live_cluster_map`) and keeps no copy; CJS-3.0 and CJS-3.1 have no row and are checked on their own Trace line. **1 `CORE-TRACE`:** Chapter One §2.1 states Safety and Truth as the non-negotiable constraints and sends the reader to §4 and §5, which own the definitions, so `tools/ch1_ch5_alignment_audit.py` now exempts it from the D/A/C widget expectation like §3 and §6 (NAV-DAC-12). **5 `IMPL-NON-REDEFINITION`:** accepted with notes (four are false positives on the words Sentient, Institution and Dependency in label and gloss sentences; one is CS-4 saying what stewarding a system means for Critical System Steward status). **4 `IMPL-RELOCATION`:** CI-4.5, CI-4.6, CI-4.7 and CI-27.5 accepted with notes; the CJS-0.1 router already names CI-4 (CJS-R23) and CI-27 (CJS-R20) as primary owners, and the relocation audit applies no move. Four unit tests in `tools/test_ch1_cjs3_live_map.py` run under `make audit-report-test`. The audit stays out of the gate because it rewrites its tracked ledger on every run.

**Fifty-sixth move (Cluster 2 sign-offs: restricted information authority; `exculpatory` retired; 2026-10-19).** Decisions (steward, in session): (1) **CF-13.4** gains a paragraph: the restricted information authority keeps answering during family-capture continuity, the displaced family does not appoint, staff, fund or instruct it and takes no part in settling its budget, and the other families carry the displaced family's share under the published formula until restoration (option B; the draft's "held by the independent activation authority" was dropped because that phrase names no body that can hold funds). (2) **CF-5.7** gains a *Family capture* bullet under *Time and fallback*, with the same rule for the Backup Record Authority; the body the continuity record names for activation oversight names the keeper until restoration. (4) **CS-2** Part A: a Type S access dispute goes first to the restricted information authority (CF-5.8), and any other challenge goes under CJS-3.13. (5) **CF-5.8** *What it does not do* gains two bullets: it does not decide audit access (Article XVI-B, CJS-3.4) and does not conduct release review when a secrecy limit ends (CJS-3.22). (6) Option B, pointer only: **CF-5.8** *What comes to it* names a dispute about a disclosure limit stated under Chapter Ten §3.1, and the Chapter Ten §3 Trace gains a *Read with* line; the operative §3.1 bullet is unchanged. (3) Option A, approved later the same day: **CF-5.8** *What it decides under* preserves material showing a sentient is not at fault or is less at fault (Article XIV-A), and a summary, redaction or closed review may not strip it. **Word retired:** the steward did not know `exculpatory`, so it and `inculpatory` are on the do-not-use list. New rule `avoid-exculpatory-family` in `tools/lexical_vocabulary_audit.py` (same files as `avoid-cure-family`), a row in `lexical_guardrails.json`, and five uses reworded to "material showing that a sentient is not at fault, or is less at fault" (and the matching "is at fault" and "casting doubt on a witness" forms): Chapter Five *Evidence Preservation*, **Article XIV-A** (Rights Floor, wording only, no obligation changed), CF-8, and two in CF-9. `mitigating` and `impeachment` were not banned; they no longer appear in those sentences. Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened review applies to the Article XIV-A and Chapter Five wording. Translations are not synced (paused). Project note: `claude/cluster-2-signoffs-implementation.md`.

**Fifty-seventh move (time windows, sitting 1 sign-offs; 2026-10-19).** Decisions (steward, in session): (1) **CS-2 §8.5 and CS-3 §7.5** restore the bullet that a changed record is updated no later than the intake target of the tier that matches the system's class, counted from when changed conditions are recognized, beside the certification-interval check (the 2026-10-18 move had dropped it; that made the catch-up time read as 3 months to 3 years instead of 24 hours to 12 days); the CS-3 §3.5 pointer names it again. (2) **CF-11.3.2** fallback: the fuller record is due no later than the end of the prompt-review window of the matter's tier, not the outer bound. (3, 4) CF-11.3.2 "reasons are recorded as part of the decision" and the CF-12.6 Document at Action wording confirmed. (5) Chapter Seven §1.6 windows for a missing System Charter or Institutional Charter keep the tier scale for every class (Class L lengthens: response 7 to 8 days, resolution 30 to 60). (6) **Disputed class:** a Chartered Party that disputes its class has the windows of the class it already holds on its System Classification Record (steward's own option). My drafting for the gap: a party holding no class on record is counted at Tier C; the old rule was Tier L for every disputed class. Sites: Chapter Seven §1.6, CJS-0.4 (rule), both copies of the regression scenarios. (7) The repeat-request clarification in Chapter Seven §1.6 confirmed as written. The Test 1 self-check (`evidence/2026-10-18/time-window-test-1-self-check.md`) now covers the windows for a missing System Charter or Institutional Charter, the disputed-class rule and the CF-11.3.2 fallback, and §3 is rewritten as resolved. Lane D for the Chapter Seven items; Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened review applies. Translations are not synced (paused). Follow-up the same day: the CJS-0.4 *Windows with no length* sentence on record updates still said "checked on the certification interval, not on a separate deadline"; it now says the update is due inside the intake target and the record is also checked on the interval. Project note: `claude/time-windows-sitting-1-implementation.md`.

**Fifty-eighth move (time windows, sitting 2 sign-offs: Chapter Thirteen §6, "reasonable" is 14 days, Document at Action; 2026-10-19).** Decisions (steward, in session; all recommendations confirmed): (1 to 5) confirmed as written: the 7 / 14 / 30 / 60 / 90-day outer bounds, Level 3 (Heavy), the prompt-review window at the intake-target figures with no cap for Tiers L and P, the Emergency level and calendar certification intervals, and the 14-day deletion period in Article II-E. (6) **Class-scaled transition deadlines.** Article XVIII-C, CS-5 and CS-9 no longer say 14 days whatever class: the transition must be completed (CS-9: initiated) within the outer bound of the Tier that matches the system's class (7, 14, 30, 60, 90 days for Classes A, B, C, L, P), or within 14 days where no class is held. The flat 14 days had given a Class A system longer than a Class A Chartered Party gets to publish its System Charter or Institutional Charter. The Article XVIII-C plain-terms line says "the outer bound of the tier that matches the system's class, from 7 days to 90". CS-4 and CS-3 keep 14 days because each names one class. CJS-0.4 *Windows with no length* lists the three 14-day uses and the three class-scaled ones. (7) Chapter One §16.4: "an Avoidable Burden defect where capture was feasible and was not built", so a manual record where capture could not yet be automatic is not a defect. The Test 1 self-check has a row for the class-scaled deadlines. Lane D for the Article XVIII-C and Chapter One edits; Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened review applies to everything signed off here. Translations are not synced (paused). Project note: `claude/time-windows-sitting-2-implementation.md`.

**Fifty-ninth move (time windows, sitting 3 sign-offs; 2026-10-19).** Decisions (steward, in session): (1) the Domain-Specific Materiality answer to a classification challenge in `core_05.050_band_oversight.md` is due inside the response window, counted from the challenge, at the Tier of the class the Operating Party already holds (Tier C where none); the Article XXVI-C "clock" cite is gone and CJS-0.4 lists the classification-challenge sense. (2) CJS-0.4 adds the Early-instantiation window (Article VIII-C) and the Exclusivity term (Article XVIII-D, CI-24); nine unused names are retired from `tools/time_reference_registry.json`. (3) New lexical rule `review-in-tier-window` in `tools/lexical_vocabulary_audit.py` (review of a measure in force names the prompt-review window; this move log is exempt). (4) No Chapter Five definitions added. Decision 2, which "declaration window" the name means, is open. Not done: translations (paused until after the announcement).

**Sixtieth move (CI-14.4 broken into subsections; 2026-10-19).** Decision (steward, in session): CI-14.4 *Rights-Floor attainment ledger* held twelve bold lead-ins under one heading, so a cite could not point below the section. Each is now a `###` subsection, **CI-14.4.1** to **CI-14.4.12** (*Ledger structure*, *Minimum measure families*, *Measurement rules*, *Enforcement-readiness declarations*, *Interim enforcement of restraint duties*, *Interim safeguards for gap-affected sentients*, *Verification (scaled to material stake)*, *Indicators that verification is unreliable*, *Status and consequences*, *Publication*, *Use in gates, reauthorization, and re-baselining*, *Implementation custody (local publication required)*), each with a Trace block and the CI-3.6 pattern; the parent gains a **Subsections** list. Presentation only: no duty changed, and the CI-14.4 anchor is unchanged, so every existing link still resolves. Nine one-sentence lead-ins were added because MD-SECTION-LEAD-01 does not allow a subsection to open with a list or table. Not done: translations (paused until after the announcement).

**Sixty-first move (enforceability declaration bounded by the Tier; 2026-10-19).** Decision (steward, in session): the phrase *declaration window* meant two things, the NUL-1 incident declaration and the Article XXVIII-A / CI-14.4 enforceability declaration. The steward chose to standardize the Article language on the Tier or Class window, not to rename NUL-1's window. (1) Article XXVIII-A *Declaration follows data* now says the declaration is due no later than the outer bound of the Tier that matches the scope's class (7 days for Class A, 14 for B, 30 for C, 60 for L, 90 for P) where the scope is a system class, and 14 days, the Tier B outer bound, for any other scope, counted from when the verified criteria are met. (2) CI-14.4.4 *Published window* is now *Declaration bound*: the plan states the bound for each scope and may shorten it, not lengthen it. (3) CJS-0.4 gains a usage rule that *declaration window* means the NUL-1 incident declaration only. Not done: translations (paused until after the announcement).

**Sixty-second move (Dissolution Reformation Lock signed off; Cluster 4, sitting 1; 2026-10-19).** Decision (steward, in session): all recommendations confirmed. The lock stays as built in the fiftieth move (permanent on a final Chapter Twelve designation with a pervasive-conduct finding, or on unremedied Irreversible Harm; otherwise restorable). Three wording changes: (1) Article XIX-D plain terms and the Chapter Five *Standing Lock* plain terms say the permanent form is the one exception and that no lock on a sentient is permanent; (2) CI-13.1 item 5 and the Chapter Eleven §5.5 trigger say a forum dissolved on a CF-11.4 threshold carries the lock, restorable unless the wind-down order records a permanence ground; (3) new CI-13.9 item 6 and the §5.5 trigger say a System retirement order sets the lock in the form the §5.5 *Systems* bullet gives. Wording only beyond what the fiftieth move already made operative. Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened review is open in TODO. Translations are not synced. Follow-on decision (steward, same session): a forum dissolved on a CF-11.4 threshold is suspended at once, and its dissolution or disbandment takes effect on the two keys in CI-13.7 (the lead Integrity Forum's decision and the confirmation of a second independent forum); CF-11.4 and CI-13.1 item 5 now say so, and no new clock is added. Project note: `claude/cluster-4-lock-signoffs-implementation.md`.

**Sixty-third move (CI-13 procedure decisions, Cluster 4 sitting 2; 2026-10-19).** Decision (steward, in session): (1) CI-13.10 item 6 adds that defiance making a remedy or process unusable is assessed under Chapter Twelve criteria 4 and 6, with no new criterion. (2) CI-13.7 item 4 becomes an order of payment (remedy to those harmed, wages owed to workers, wind-down costs, non-culpable creditors and dependents, owners not at fault; shares of owners or insiders at fault go to unpaid remedy and then a named remedy system or receiver). (3) CI-13.10 item 7 states who pays the steward, receiver, liquidator and verifying authority: the Chartered Party's assets, then responsible sentients under CI-11.4, then the primary-stakes forum family's published budget, never affected sentients or dependents. (4) CI-13.9 item 2 says preservation ends only on a final not-sentient finding plus a forum order meeting the Article XXVIII-D floor, and the steward never elects deletion. (5) The name "contraction order" stays. (6) Class P reach, steward's own option: every Class P Operating Party, chartered or not, is subject to the ladder and the Dissolution Reformation Lock. CI-13.1 reads "Chartered Party" to include a Class P Operating Party with no System Charter or Institutional Charter; CI-13.4 says rung 3 has nothing to remove from it; the Chapter Five *Chartered Party* out-of-scope bullet and Chapter Eleven §4.2 and §5.5 point to this. Wording of operative CI-13 text; Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened review is open in TODO. Still open: the operative sign-off of CI-13. Translations are not synced. Project note: `claude/cluster-4-procedure-signoffs-implementation.md`.

**Sixty-fourth move (CI-13 quick orientation split; 2026-10-19).** Decision (steward, in session; presentation only): the visible **Quick orientation** at the front of CI-13 mixed reader guidance, operative scope statements, and a repeat of the Trace *Read with* list. (1) Five orientation bullets (who keeps the official record, what the institution still must do, how the consequence ladder works, what dissolution does, what dissolution is not) moved into a collapsed **Reader guidance (non-operative): Orientation** widget between Corpus placement and Trace. (2) The *Read with* bullet was dropped because the Trace already lists every file it named. (3) The two mandatory-review bullets (dissolution review; alignment-supervision review) stay visible as operative prose under **When review is mandatory**, with shorter labels, because the second one is the only statement of which institutions the alignment-supervision review covers and a non-operative widget cannot hold it. Not applied elsewhere: the other institutions files (for example CI-10, CI-11, CI-12, CI-14, CI-27) still open with a visible Quick orientation under the domain local-duty pattern. (4) The file gloss lost its sentence pointing to CJS-3.15, CJS-3.6 and CF-7, which *Shared rules live elsewhere* below it already says in full. (5) The text from the second *In plain terms* gloss through the lead-record list was rewritten in plainer words and given blank lines between its labelled blocks (they had run together into one paragraph when rendered); the two *Shared rules* paragraphs became one, and the record-custody options became a list. `tools/obligation_inventory_diff.py` matched every obligation 1:1 before and after. No obligation changed.

**Sixty-fifth move (CI-13 split into CI-13 and CI-28; 2026-10-19).** Decision (steward, in session): CI-13 held two questions in one file of about 51 KB, so it is split into failure and dissolution, and accountability and sanctions. (1) **CI-13** is now *Institutional failure and dissolution* (`ci_11.210_institutional_failure_dissolution.md`, about 31 KB): the orientation, mandatory-review statements, record-custody boundary, local duties and lead-record list, then CI-13.1 *Opening a consequence matter*, CI-13.2 *Findings before any order*, CI-13.3 *Notice, response, and resolution*, and CI-13.4 *Dissolution*. (2) **CI-28** is new, *Institutional accountability and sanctions* (`ci_11.215_institutional_accountability_sanctions.md`, about 28 KB): CI-28.1 *Correction and constrained authority* (holds the six-rung table), CI-28.2 *Contraction order*, CI-28.3 *Supervised operation and receivership*, CI-28.4 *Emergency containment*, CI-28.5 *Systems under consequence orders*, and CI-28.6 *Review, fairness, and enforcement*. (3) **Number map** (old to new): 13.1, 13.2, 13.3 unchanged; 13.4 to 28.1; 13.5 to 28.2; 13.6 to 28.3; 13.7 to 13.4; 13.8 to 28.4; 13.9 to 28.5; 13.10 to 28.6. Earlier moves in this file, the `claude/` project notes and the evidence folders keep the old numbers. (4) The new chapter is CI-28, not a renumbering of CI-14 to CI-27, so no existing chapter number moves. The family map, the footer chain (`tools/footer_audit.py`) and the registry annex place it right after CI-13, so the reading chain runs CI-13, CI-28, CI-14. (5) The text moved verbatim. The only wording added is pointers and framing: the CI-13 and CI-28 orientation, plain-terms gloss and shared-rules paragraph; a *Systems* pointer at the end of CI-13.4; a pointer to CI-13.4 in the rung 6 row of the table; a pointer to CI-28.1 in the CI-13.4 *When available* paragraph; and the lead-record bullet, which now names CI-28.1 to CI-28.5 and CI-13.4. A line-by-line comparison against the old file found no other difference, so no obligation was added, dropped or changed. (6) Cites repointed in the core files (Chapters Five, Six, Seven, Nine Part D, Eleven), CJS-0.1 and CJS-3.15, CF-7, CF-11, CS-10, CS-7, CI-1, CI-5, CI-8, CI-12, CI-14, CI-27, the CI-26 and CI-0 registries, the adoption table in `MINIMUM_VIABLE_ADOPTER.md`, `tools/time_reference_registry.json` (order term now owned by CI-28.2) and TODO. Chapter Seven's chart boxes now read "CI-13 and CI-28 — Consequence review" and "CI-28 and CI-13 — Contract below Class L, or dissolve". Where a cite meant the whole procedure it now names both chapters; where it meant sanctions it names CI-28 (CI-5 and CJS-3.15 primary failure); where it meant failure or dissolution it names CI-13. (7) Scenario IDs `RS-CI13-CR-*` are kept as stable IDs; their cites are updated. Derived files regenerated (family index, scenario catalog, `ai_corpus/`, `doc_architecture/generated/`). Not done: translations (none cite CI-13). Verification: `make audit-report` 103 of 103. Project note: `claude/ci-13-split-implementation.md`.

**Sixty-sixth move (ownership statements swept across the corpus; 2026-10-20).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): CI-13's opening sentence, `This file is the institutional implementation home for **CI-13**`, is the model for a binding owner statement. A sweep of every `core_*` file and every CJS, CS, CI, and CF file for a visible owner statement found 45 files where it was missing, held only in a collapsed widget, off the standard wording, in the wrong place, or carried the wrong section name. No operative duty changed. (1) **Added a visible owner line:** the Preamble (new claim: the Model, the measurement categories, and the owner register; flagged for steward review); Chapter One Parts A, B, and C; the Chapter Five hub, its five band files (the three Tetrad bands already said "owns" in the body, now first and in the standard form), and its six apex files; Chapter Six Parts B to E; the four registry annexes (CF-0, CI-0, CS-0, CJS-0). (2) **Added an "owns no rule" line that names the owners:** the Chapter Nine reading index, Chapter Nine Part C, and the Chapters Ten to Thirteen vignettes. (3) **Standard wording and placement:** Chapter Ten (was "owns", now "is the constitutional owner of", with the Question 1 and Question 2 split kept); Chapters Ten and Eleven and CS-4 (owner line moved ahead of the gloss or the first section); CS-2 and CS-3 Parts A and B (one opener). (4) **Split families made explicit:** CS-5 and CS-5 Part A each say what the other holds; CJS-1 says which sections sit in `cjs_04`, `cjs_05`, and `cjs_06`; the CJS-3 hub says it holds CJS-3.0 and CJS-3.1 and is the hub for the rest; `cjs_03o` and `cjs_03u` point at each other for CJS-3.3. (5) **Wrong names fixed:** CJS-1.7 and CJS-1.9 were cited under another section's name in 11 places in nine files; they now carry the heading names. CI-19's owner line had its title twice; it has it once. (6) New rules are in OWNER-OPENING-01 and NAV-IMPL-SUBFILE-01 above. (7) **Not changed:** `cf_13.475_sentience_status_record.md` ("designated implementation text", the term Chapter Five uses) and `cjs_reader_guide.md` (says it is reader guidance only) are already clear. Translations were not touched and keep the earlier text (paused). **Open for the steward:** 46 citations call CS-5 "User-facing capability surfaces", but the family map names CS-5 *Design, testing, verification, and deployment* and gives "User-facing capability surfaces" to CS-5 Part A; the owner lines now say which file holds what, but the citations were left alone. The sweep is now backed by `make owner-line-audit` (`tools/owner_line_audit.py`, 23 unit tests, in `make regression`; see the **Gate** paragraphs under OWNER-OPENING-01 and NAV-IMPL-SUBFILE-01). It also caught the Constitutional Performance band, whose claim read "This file is the home for" and sat after the gloss; it now reads in the standard form ahead of the gloss. Steward decision (2026-10-20): the Preamble claim stands; CS-5's two-title problem stays open and the 46 citations are left as they are for now. Gate: `make audit-report` (103 of 103 after the index regeneration). Note: `claude/ownership-statements-sweep-implementation.md` in the project.

**Sixty-seventh move (CS-5 split into CS-5 System lifecycle and CS-13 System requirements; 2026-10-20).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): CS-5 carried two unrelated topics under one number and two titles, so the family was split. Retitle: **CS-5** is now *System lifecycle* (design, testing, verification, and deployment; §§1–10; file name unchanged). **CS-13** (*System requirements*) is the old CS-5 Part A, the catalog of user-reachable capability surfaces, in `corpus_systems/cs_06.340_system_requirements.md` (renamed from `cs_05_a_user_facing_capabilities.md`). Its sections moved with a new number: CS-5.11 to CS-13.1, CS-5.12 to CS-13.2, CS-5.13 to CS-13.3, CS-5.14 to CS-13.4. No rule text changed except one sentence, noted below. (1) **Citations were sorted by what they point at, not by the label they carried.** The old label "User-facing capability surfaces" sat on 74 citations, and nearly all of them point at lifecycle sections (§8 governance continuity and exercises, §9 self-healing, regression testing, forum certification and lifecycle review, safety profiles). Those now read **CS-5** (*System lifecycle*). Only the eight that mean the catalog of user-reachable surfaces moved to **CS-13** (*System requirements*): the CS-D04 and CS-D12 rows and their trace lines in CS-0, the CS-1 scope list, the CS-5 owner and trace lines, and the Chapter Four trace line. The 28 citations that used the old long title (*Design, testing, verification, and deployment*) now read *System lifecycle*. Chapter Nine and CJS-3 text, CF-12, CI-11, CI-14, CS-3, CS-4, CS-8, CS-10, CS-11, and CS-12 were relabelled, never retargeted. (2) **Ranges and counts.** "CS-1 through CS-12", "CS-2–CS-12" and "CS-5–CS-12" now end at CS-13 where they list the systems families (CS-0, CS-1, the landing page, CJS-1, and the CJS reader guide). (3) **The "No CS-13" rule is gone, and its point is kept.** The old CS-5 Part A closing paragraph said "No CS-13 … Do not mint a required software family." CS-13 now exists, so that paragraph reads "Not a software list": the family catalogs user-reachable in-scope surfaces and nothing more, and must not grow into a list of required software. This is the one sentence that changed. (4) **Reading order and registries.** CS-13 stays in the reading chain between CS-5 and CS-6, where Part A was (the same placement as CI-28 after CI-13), so the Previous and Next links only changed target name. `tools/footer_audit.py`, `tools/architecture/family_map.json` (family id `CS-5A` is now `CS-13`; section homes 13.1 to 13.4), the two gloss tables, and the grouped family index in `corpus_systems.md` were updated; indexes and the four derived files were regenerated. (5) **Left alone on purpose:** translations (paused), evidence folders, dated records, the earlier moves in this file, and the former protocol-letter alias table (Protocol A is still CS-5). The sixty-sixth move's "open for the steward" item (CS-5's two titles) is closed by this move. Gate: `make audit-report`. Note: `claude/cs-13-system-requirements-split-implementation.md` in the project.

**Sixty-eighth move (Chapter Seven §4 opening rewritten; the old seat-rules and independence subsections folded in and then split out as two new subsections; 2026-10-20).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; core text, signed off by the steward 2026-10-20; Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened review open): (1) The "How this section is laid out" list at the head of §4 became a collapsed **Reader guidance (non-operative): how this section is laid out** widget. (2) New **§4.1 What the seats are, and why they exist** reminds the reader of the four seats and why they exist, taken from Chapter Eight §2, with no new rule. (3) New **§4.2 What makes a placement valid** holds the content of the old §4.2 *Where the seat rules are* (the placement tests: Material Control Line, independence, vacancy and substitution, merged hosting; the old four-seat bullet is covered by §4.1).
