The rule of the old §4.3 *Independence from the party whose act is at issue* (the record, contest, and verify-or-authorize seats sit outside the Material Control Line of the party whose act is at issue, and that party can't change those entries on its own) is now carried by the **Material Control Line** and **Independence** bullets in §4.2; it is no longer a separate subsection, and the bullet was then reworded to say who the party is, what control means (from the Material Control Line entry in Chapter Five), and that the party can't change who holds those seats by its own decision alone; the duty itself is unchanged. (4) The old §4.1 is now **§4.3 What a System Charter or Institutional Charter owes the map** and says what is a defect in three cases: the map is missing, the Charter Pointer is dead (not a live, published document), or the map is out of date (does not show who holds each seat now; a change of holder is a new dated version, §4.5). (5) **Numbering:** §4.4 *Which System Charter or Institutional Charter holds which map*, §4.5 *Changing seat rules and seat holders* (was *Two kinds of change*), §4.6 *Standing-record custody* (now two parts, §4.6.1 *Operator standing records* and §4.6.2 *Stakeholder standing records*, with the lead-in kept under §4.6) and §4.7 *Gaps in the map for operator standing records* keep their numbers from before this move, so the cites to §4.7 in Chapter Eight §5, CI-3 (two places) and the live TODO and MEMLOG did not change in the end. Slugs and the layout widget follow. Dated records (earlier moves here, archive, evidence, TODO and MEMLOG history) and translations keep the numbers they were written with. The fourth item of §4.6.1 no longer says "for when the Chartered Party is itself the subject" (operator records are always about the Chartered Party): it now follows Chapter Five's Charter Role Map entry and Chapter Eight §5, "the independent route when no qualified substitute is available", with the examples from CI-3.6 field 10, and it names the Backup Record Authority (CF-5.7) as the default where the System Charter or Institutional Charter names no route. Steward sign-off (2026-10-20): §4.1 to §4.3 as written; §4.5 now says a holder change for the record, contest, or verify-or-authorize seat takes effect only after notice and a chance to contest on the amendment path the System Charter or Institutional Charter names, with an emergency substitution under §4.7 effective at once and open to contest; the §4.6.1 pointers moved below the four bullets. No other operative duty changed. Gate: `make audit-report`.

**Sixty-ninth move (Backup Record Custodian CF-5.9; eligible record custodians; Record-Opening Authority and Record Custodian in Chapter Five; 2026-10-20).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; core text, signed off by the steward 2026-10-20; Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened review open): operator standing records need three independent seats, but only the record-opening seat had a standing independent body (the Backup Record Authority, CF-5.7), and the fallback for custody was "the supervising forum", which made a forum the custodian of last resort. (1) **CF-5.9 Backup Record Custodian** (new, `cf_05`): a custody twin of CF-5.7, kept by the Institutional Forum family or the local forum, with separate staff and no shared supervisor on a record, the same funding, reporting, intake-target clock, silence rule to a forum, capacity-failure and family-capture rules. It enters and holds authorized records and nothing else. **Scope (steward-approved reading):** when a System Charter or Institutional Charter has a gap it holds the versions opened during the gap and any operator standing record then held by an office inside the Chartered Party's Material Control Line (those offices are already excluded from every operator record); records held by a custodian outside the line stay. For one conflicted record it holds that record only. For a custodian that ends or cannot continue it takes the records held for the scope, with their trail. (2) **Eligible record custodians** (CI-3.6.3 field 10, two new bullets): a non-closed list (the Backup Record Custodian, a public archive or registry, a separate Institution with no control relationship, a shared-map host for records not about its own members, a records service paid from a fund the Chartered Party does not control) and seven tests (outside the Material Control Line including appointment, funding, membership and discipline; separate from the authority for the same record; no reciprocal or circular hosting, ruled out outright; continuity; challenge path; no fee; named in advance). A professional association fails the first test for a record about its own member's practice. (3) **Chapter Ten §3.7** (*Record custodian*, *Conflicted custodian on a single record*, the gap in a System Charter or Institutional Charter, *No self-custody*) and **Chapter Seven §4.6.1, §4.7** name the Backup Record Custodian as the default custodian beside the Backup Record Authority; the supervising forum is the fallback if it cannot act. CF-5.7, the CF-5 trace and the CF-5 opening list point to it. (4) **Chapter Five** gains **Record-Opening Authority** (after Verify-or-Authorize Seat) and **Record Custodian** (after Record Seat) in the Governance architecture cluster, as the role entries the corpus already relied on. No new standing central body was created for the role; the Backup Record Authority and Backup Record Custodian fill it by default. Seeds, cluster-order audit, definitions home, hierarchy map, plain-terms edition and `ai_corpus/` indexes were updated. Drafts kept in `archive/` (`BACKUP_RECORD_CUSTODIAN_AND_ELIGIBLE_CUSTODIANS_DRAFT_APPLIED_2026-10-20.md`, `RECORD_OPENING_AUTHORITY_DEFINITION_DRAFT_APPLIED_2026-10-20.md`). Translations not touched (paused). Gate: `make audit-report`. Later the same day, at the steward's direction, the "independent route" wording in CI-3.6.3 field 10 and Chapter Ten §3.7 was aligned to "when no qualified substitute is available", and the "How this section is laid out" lists in Chapter Seven §2 and §3 became Reader guidance widgets. The steward also decided, same day: Chapter Nine Part B §9.2 gains one sentence holding the clean-recertification count at zero after a design-cause finding until the redesign is in place and verified; the "Adoption Document" name is confirmed; and the sole-operator record-host question and the question whether the System Charter and Institutional Charter definitions absorb standing-record field 10, both open since 2026-10-13, are closed. The steward also signed off the operative wording of CI-13 and CI-28 as a whole (decision 7) on 2026-10-20. Heightened review (Chapter Seventeen §1) was run the same day by an independent AI reviewer; at the steward's direction the fixes were applied to Chapter Seven §4.5 to §4.7 and §5, CI-3.6.3 fields 10 and 11, CF-5.7, CF-5.9, Chapter Ten §3.7, the Chapter Five glosses, and Chapter Nine Part B §9.2 (verifier, clock, request for review). Record: `evidence/2026-10-20/heightened_review_record_2026-10-20.md`. Follow-ups the same day: capacity and surge publication in CF-5.7 and CF-5.9, and a dependents sentence in the Chapter Nine Part B §9.2 *Class A reliance* paragraph. Heightened review of the older batches (Clusters A, B and C: the Dissolution Reformation Lock and ladder, time windows, authority naming, Interim Hold and speech, authenticity) was run the same day; the fixes touch Chapters Five, Six, Seven, Nine, Eleven and Thirteen and CF-5, CF-11, CF-13, CI-3, CI-13, CI-14, CI-28, CS-5, CS-9 and CJS-0 as wording and process additions, with no renumbering (record: [evidence/2026-10-20/heightened_review_record_older_batches_2026-10-20.md](evidence/2026-10-20/heightened_review_record_older_batches_2026-10-20.md)).

**Seventieth move (Chapter Seven formation check: "limb" replaced by plain names for the three checks; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed; steward sign-off needed on the operative headings): "limb" was an anatomical metaphor that an ordinary reader would not read as "one of the checks that make up the formation check", and "structural limb" was the least clear of the three. (1) Chapter Seven §5.2 "Limb one: standing" is now **Standing check**, §5.3 "Limb two: authenticity" is now **Authenticity check**, and §5.6 "Structural limb for other staff" is now **Staff safeguards check** (anchors `#52-standing-check`, `#53-authenticity-check`, `#56-staff-safeguards-check`). "Safeguards" names what the check shows: pay and promotion rules that do not reward working against the purpose, a protected reporting route, and the Article III-C labor floor. "Leg" was rejected (Chapter Five apex legs), "part" (Part A to E), "prong" (legal jargon). (2) Matching wording in Chapter Nine Part D §12.2 item 7 and CI-8.205 (the statement fields). (3) `avoid-bare-check` guidance in `tools/lexical_vocabulary_audit.py` and `tools/architecture/lexical_guardrails.json` now names the three checks as the allowed qualified compounds. (4) No other file linked the old anchors; derived files regenerated. Earlier entries and evidence notes keep the old wording as history.

**Seventy-first move (Chapter Seven: bare "re-check" added to the unqualified-word list; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording and audit only, no rule changed): in Chapter Seven the noun "re-check" did not say what was being checked again. It is now always the **formation re-check**. (1) New audit rule `avoid-bare-recheck` in `tools/lexical_vocabulary_audit.py`, scoped to `core_07.000_charters.md` like `avoid-bare-check`: the noun re-check, re-checks, recheck, rechecks must follow "formation"; the verb forms (re-checked, re-checking) are not matched. Self-tests added, including the scope test. (2) New avoid/prefer entry in `tools/architecture/lexical_guardrails.json`. (3) Chapter Seven §5.8 retitled "Timing and formation re-check" (anchor `#58-timing-and-formation-re-check`; only the §5 table of contents linked the old anchor), the §5 table-of-contents line, and the "Formation re-check" bullet label; CI-8.205 statement field "Next formation re-check". Other chapters' uses of re-check and recheck (certification cadence, emergency powers, archives) mean different things and are not in scope. Steward sign-off needed on the §5.8 heading.

**Seventy-second move (Chapter Seven §5.10 "What this section does not do" removed; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; no rule changed; steward sign-off needed on the removal): the four sentences in §5.10 repeated text that is already in the chapter. "Creates no lock, no hold, no bar, and no standing record" is the last line of §5.2 and is repeated in §8 (Chapter Eleven). The CI-14.205 §2 and Chapter Eight relationships are in §5.2, §6, the Read-with list and §8. "Never asks what a sentient believes" is §5.4 and "Individual staff are not screened" is §5.6. "No permission" for forming is §2 and Chapter Nine Part D §11.3. The subsection and its table-of-contents line are deleted; nothing else linked its anchor `#510-what-this-section-does-not-do`. §5.9 is now the last subsection of §5.

**Seventy-third move (Chapter Seven §6 When a Holder Fails the Formation Check split into nine subsections; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed, no sentence added or dropped apart from labels and one pointer): §6 was a long run of bulleted consequences with a four-level nested bullet for the windows. It now follows the §5 layout (reader guidance, Trace, plain-terms line, then numbered subsections): §6.1 What a failed formation check does; §6.2 When the standing check fails; §6.3 When there is probable cause; §6.4 When the authenticity check fails; §6.5 When a dissolved Chartered Party returns; §6.6 Response and resolution windows (when it starts, response window, resolution window); §6.7 If a failure is not resolved (lifted out of the window bullets, text unchanged); §6.8 What can reach the Chartered Party; §6.9 Fairness to the holder. A §6.8 "What can happen to the Chartered Party itself" was drafted and then removed the same day because each of its three points was already stated elsewhere (§6.7 and the plain-terms box; §6.1, §6.5 and the plain-terms exception; §2.1). Its one point that §6 did not carry, that a failed formation check is evidence on the limits of the right to form but does not settle them, moved into the last bullet of §6.1. Fairness to the holder is now §6.8 (anchor `#68-fairness-to-the-holder`; only the §6 reader guidance linked the old anchor). The only wording changes are the dropped bullet labels (first word capitalized) and the pointer in §6.8, which said "*Resolution window* above" and now links to §6.7. A second pass the same day turned the paragraphs in §6.1 to §6.8 into labelled bullets, one idea each, with the authenticity resolutions, the escalation ladder and the emergency-substitution window as nested lists; no sentence was dropped (a word comparison shows only splitting words lost). §6.1 was then trimmed the same day: the bullet re-describing the three gates was replaced by one pointer bullet saying that no gate comes from the failure itself (the gates are defined in §2.2 to §2.4). The "Nothing is blocked" bullet in §6.1 and its reader-guidance line were corrected the same day because they were wrong for an Operating Party under a lock: a failed formation check never blocks by itself, but a lock (the Dissolution Reformation Lock on a dissolved Chartered Party that comes back) does, and the bullet now says so and points to §6.5. The §6 plain-terms box now opens with the dissolved-Chartered-Party exception (the lock stops it, not the formation check) instead of closing with it. Each of §5.8 and §6.1 to §6.9 opens with a one-line lead-in, as MD-SECTION-LEAD-01 requires of a section that would otherwise open with a list. §6 keeps its anchor and number, so the inbound links (CI-11.210, §5.8, the chart) are unaffected. Steward sign-off needed on the new headings.

**Seventy-fourth move (a third gate: the lock on the Operating Party itself; Article XI-E and Chapter Seven §2 recounted; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; operative wording in a Rights Article, steward sign-off needed): Article XI-E counted two gates, with the lock on an Operating Party folded into the outstanding-lock gate as a sub-case. The custodian directed that a lock set on a particular Operating Party (the Dissolution Reformation Lock, permanent where the order finds pervasive anti-constitutional conduct or unremedied Irreversible Harm, and the Anti-Constitutional Trust Lock where a final designation names an institution, successor vehicle, or controlled entity) be counted as its own, third gate. (1) **Article XI-E:** "Two narrow gates" is now "Three narrow gates": outstanding lock (sentient), probable cause (sentient), and a new sibling bullet "Lock on the Operating Party itself" that takes over the two sub-bullets formerly nested under the outstanding lock; the Operating Party is the subject only of that gate; safeguards now read "on all three gates"; the plain-terms paragraph and the Article's opening line are recounted. No sentence of substance was dropped or added. (2) **Chapter Seven §2** is retitled "The Right to Form, and Four Narrow Limits" (anchor `#2-the-right-to-form-and-four-narrow-limits`; inbound links in Chapter Five Oversight, the regression-scenario file, and the scenario catalogue updated): §2.2 First limit: an outstanding lock on a sentient (anchor `#22-first-limit-an-outstanding-lock-on-a-sentient`); §2.3 Second limit: a short hold on probable cause (unchanged); **§2.4 Third limit: a lock on the Operating Party itself** (new, holds the text moved out of §2.2 and the closing "none of the three gates" paragraph moved out of §2.3); §2.5 Fourth limit: a practice licence or clearance (formerly §2.4, anchor `#25-fourth-limit-a-practice-licence-or-clearance`; "the first three are the only gates, the fourth is not a gate"). (3) **Chapter Seven §5 and §6:** the formation check applies the three gates (the two on a named sentient to covered offices, the third to a Chartered Party that is itself under a lock); §6.1 lists all three; §6.5 gains a "Deliberate evasion" bullet: re-forming, reviving, or running a dissolved Chartered Party under another name or through a successor, controlled entity, or nominee- or shell-held vehicle to get around the lock is a form of anti-constitutional misconduct that a forum may designate under Chapter Twelve Part A on verified facts (custodian's rule; Chapter Eleven §10.6 already names the situation but did not call it misconduct). (4) **Pointers recounted:** Chapter Five Participation (creation-rights limits), Chapter Nine Part D (two places), CI-9.210, CI-14.205 ("one of the narrow gates"), and Chapter Eleven §5.5 (the Dissolution Reformation Lock's gate effect now names the Operating Party lock gate). The Anti-Constitutional Trust Lock is not permanent (Chapter Twelve Part A); it is in the third gate because it can sit on an Operating Party, so the third gate is not only for permanent locks.

**Seventy-fifth move ("probable cause" retired as the label for the Interim Hold ground; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; operative wording in a Rights Article, steward sign-off needed): "probable cause" was used about 32 times in 8 files as a label, never defined, and it pulled in the criminal-law meaning (a low bar for acting against someone, which suggests "probably guilty") for a standard that is stricter and is a measure short of a finding. The real standard is the "Ground" in Chapter Thirteen §5 *Interim hold on a named sentient's pathway*: specific facts disclosed to the sentient and verified to a preliminary tier; more likely than not that the risk is real; a risk of serious harm to others, or of defeating a check on a body, a forum, or a certification, that cannot wait for the merits; and a narrower safeguard would not be enough. (1) **Body text** now says "the grounds for an Interim Hold" or points to Chapter Thirteen §5, or says "specific verified facts" where a short phrase is needed (Chapter Five Interim Hold entry and the Trust Lock note; Article XI-E, whose bullet is retitled *Grounds for an Interim Hold*, with "the only test of this kind"; Chapter Five Participation; Chapter Nine Part D §11 and §13.5; Chapter Eleven §5.1, §5.5 and §5.6, where the bullet "No lock from probable cause" is now "No lock without a verified finding"; Chapter Thirteen §5 index line; CF-13.410 §15 plain-terms and Ground lines; the regression-scenario files). (2) **Headings** in Chapter Seven: §2.3 is now "Second limit: a short hold on specific verified facts" (anchor `#23-second-limit-a-short-hold-on-specific-verified-facts`) and §6.3 is now "When the facts may support an Interim Hold" (anchor `#63-when-the-facts-may-support-an-interim-hold`); the gate list names the second gate "An Interim Hold". No standard was changed; only the label. Chapter Thirteen §5 keeps its heading *Ground*. Translations still carry the old term until they are resynchronized.

**Seventy-sixth move (Chapter Seven §8 "What System and Institutional Charters Cannot Do" removed; old §9 is now §8; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; no rule changed): all six bullets repeated text that is already stated with more force elsewhere. Classification, boundary, and "not a substitute for certification" are in the Chapter Five *System Charter* and *Institutional Charter* entries (assessment items, primary and tertiary failures), in Chapter Nine Part A (classification "not from System Charter text alone"; chartered scope against functional boundaries), and in Chapter Nine Part B and Chapter Four (vendor attestation and self-attested proof do not substitute). "Carry the decision rules" is §3.1 item 8 and Chapter Fourteen §4.3. "Grant authority" is the §1.6 reader guidance, the Chapter Fourteen bullet in the relationships section, and the Chapter Five primary failure ("offered as current authorization"). The creation-right half of "narrow a right" is §2.1 and the Chapter Six bullet in the relationships section. Two pieces were not stated elsewhere and are kept, reworded: (1) the exclusion clause and Rights Floor are now the *Not an opt-out* sub-bullet under §3.1 item 5 (a group left off the list is not out of scope, keeps every Rights Floor protection, and a list narrower than the real reliance is read against it), and the Chapter Six relationships bullet now says adopting a System Charter or Institutional Charter must not narrow the right "or any Rights Floor protection"; (2) "writing a narrow one does not shrink what the Chartered Party really does" is now the *A narrow document is no shelter* bullet in §7.2 (class, boundary, and duties follow real activity and dependency, and a document that claims less is read against them), with the §7 reader-guidance line for §7.2 extended. Renumbering: old §9 *Relationship to Other Chapters* is now §8 (anchor `#8-relationship-to-other-chapters`); its Trace now reads "§1 through §7". The owner line, the reading-order line, the subsection list, and the body link in the chapter opening follow. The two Chapter Five Downstream descriptors no longer say Chapter Seven covers "what it cannot do". No other file linked the old §8 or §9 anchors. Dated records (earlier moves, TODO and MEMLOG history, evidence, archive) keep the numbers they were written with. Translations are paused and still use the old numbering.

**Seventy-seventh move (Chapter Seven relationships section removed; its content distributed; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; no rule changed): the section that was §9 and, after the previous move, §8 (*Relationship to Other Chapters*) restated the Trace block and the chapter-opening chain, one bullet per later chapter. The chapter now ends at §7. Where each bullet went: **Chapter Six** is one sentence at the end of the first paragraph of §2.1 (adopting a System Charter or Institutional Charter must not narrow the right to form, "or any Rights Floor protection"). **Chapter Eight** is dropped; §4 already opens with Chapter Eight §5 requiring the map. **Chapter Nine** is dropped except for two sentences added to the §7.5 *Certification review* bullet (certification reads the stated scope against the real boundaries and does not rewrite the document; an institutional certification cites each included System Certification Record without redoing it); the formation-check-statement test is already in the §5 Trace and the reopening triggers are already in §7.5. **Chapter Ten** recognition of contribution is a short paragraph at the end of §1.4 (Class P), with the quilting-circle example and the Chapter Ten §3.7 pointer; the custody sentence is the last sentence of the §4.6 opening. **Chapter Eleven** is dropped; §5.2 already ends "creates no lock, no hold, no bar, and no standing record". **Chapter Thirteen** is the new *Where a dispute goes* bullet in §7.5, and the §7 reader-guidance line for §7.5 follows. **Chapter Fourteen** is the *A claim, not a grant* sub-bullet under §3.1 item 3 (the document states authority the Chartered Party already holds and grants none; legitimacy, including by Constitutional Charter, is decided under Chapter Fourteen §1); the binding stakeholder-choice rules were already §3.1 item 8. **Chapter Eighteen** is added to the *Implementation owner* line in the chapter opening (incorporated text binds only within valid adoption scope; the floor binds as core text). The Trace "Read with: Preamble owner register" line is dropped with the section. The Subsections list, the reading-order line, and the sentence in the chapter opening that pointed at the section are updated. Nothing else linked the anchor `#8-relationship-to-other-chapters`. Dated records keep the numbers they were written with. Translations are paused and still use the old numbering.

**Seventy-eighth move (Chapter Seven §1.7 chart matched to §6; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; no rule changed; chart labels only, no node or arrow added or removed): the chart was checked against the chapter after the two section removals. Nothing in it pointed at the removed sections, and the section labels still match the headings. Three labels no longer matched §6, and are fixed. (1) The failed-holder box and the "holder replaced" arrow said the only fix is replacing the holder; §6.4 says replacing the holder is one choice and never required, and the cause can also be fixed by ending the undisclosed control, changing the reward, or disclosing the interest. The box now says "replace the holder, or fix the cause", the arrow says "resolved, check again", and the reader-guidance line says "replaced, or the cause is fixed". (2) "Consequence review" appeared only in the chart; the term in §6.7, CI-11.210 and CJS-5.125 §15 is structural review, which only a forum opens. The box is now "Structural review", says only a forum decides, and the arrow from the resolution window carries "§6.7". (3) The reader guidance said a dissolved Chartered Party may not come back while the lock stands, but the chart did not show it; the dissolution box now says so, with "§6.5". The two box titles are also put in the same order (CI-11.210 and CI-11.215). Left as is: the chart does not show the §1.6 request route for a missing System Charter or Institutional Charter or the §6.7 "finding in the next certification" step; both are in the text and the chart is a roadmap, not a full flow.

**Seventy-ninth move (Chapter Eight §1 "does not relocate" list removed; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; no rule changed): the sentence "This chapter states the cross-process segregation-of-duties floor. It does not relocate:" and its five bullets (Chapter Four burden and verification method; Chapter Five canonical meanings; Chapter Six Rights Floors; Chapters Nine through Thirteen process-specific record content; seat catalogs, staffing methods, and lane-map mechanics from designated implementation text) were cut as boilerplate. Each was already stated elsewhere: the general non-relocation rule is in the Chapter Five Authority Stack entry (*Owner register and non-relocation*) and the Preamble owner register; §2 says Chapter Five supplies the seat meanings; the file-top note says process-specific application remains downstream and that implementation text may be stricter but may not narrow the chapter; §9 says what later processes may add and may not omit; the Trace widgets route to Chapter Four and Article XVI. Unlike the Chapter Seven exclusion clause, no scope exclusion or opt-out is guarded here, so nothing needed to be kept. The §1 heading *Purpose, Scope, and Owner Boundary* is unchanged (its anchor is linked from several places); the owner boundary now lives in the chapter opening and the file-top note. Derived files regenerated: `plain_terms_edition.json`, `boundary_chunks.json`, and the `ai_corpus` indexes. `owner-line-audit`, `owner-discipline-audit`, and the three derived-file checks pass. `_pages_site` (ignored, generated) still holds the old text until the site is rebuilt. Same sitting: the class-scaling paragraph at the end of §2 is set out as three bullets (Class C, Class B, Class A) under a lead-in, with the wording unchanged except that "it" became "full four-seat separation" in the Class B and Class A bullets; the mixed-or-uncertain-scope sentence stays as its own paragraph.

**Eightieth move (Chapter Eight §4: wrong-seat routing built into the process; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; **one new "should" added, no new "must"**): §4 told only the individual steward what to do when asked to take a step outside the steward's seat, and said nothing about designing the process so the wrong-seat request does not arise. §4 now opens with a paragraph saying wrong-seat routing should be built into the process wherever practicable (an intake, queue, workflow, or interface sends each step to its published seat from the start), and that automated routing (1) follows the §5 published placement and routes on seat, not on the merits; (2) logs each route as the existing item 3 requires; (3) holds the matter and logs the gap when no seat is placed, rather than sending it to the nearest, fastest, or most senior seat (this mirrors the §5 rule that an unplaced seat does not pass to the initiating seat or a superior); and (4) is not itself a seat-holder and cannot change who holds a seat, since only the published placement does. The five-step steward duty is unchanged and now opens "Where routing is not automated, or fails, the duty below applies to the steward." The *In plain terms* gloss gained one sentence. Not made a "must": automation is not always practicable, and how routing is built belongs to CJS-5.125 §11, CI-8.205 §2, and CI-14.205 §6, none of which mentions automated routing today (open item: say so in CI-14.205 §6 item 3, the wrong-seat rule). Derived files regenerated; `owner-line-audit`, `owner-discipline-audit`, and the three derived-file checks pass. `plain-language-audit` still fails only on the existing `doc_architecture.md` line 930 finding. Translations are paused.

**Eighty-first move (Chapter Eight §5 reorganized into four labeled blocks; §4 router bullet matched to §5; 2026-10-21).** Decision (custodian, asked in session after the evaluation in `claude/ch08-section5-organization-evaluation.md`; uncommitted; no duty changed). §5 keeps its heading and anchor and now has four bold lead-ins: *What the map must say* (list unchanged); *Where the map is kept*; *A seat the map leaves unplaced, or a holder who cannot serve*; *Delegation* (text unchanged). (1) The Class P material is cut to one sentence (outside this chapter under §1, need not publish a map, may do so under Chapter Seven §4); the private-unit wording, the CS-9.305 / CS-9.310 reason, and the "floor does not apply" sentence stay only in §1. (2) The shared-map-by-reference sentence moved into *Where the map is kept* and now says the member still names its own substitute and independent route (already required by the §5 list, §6, and Chapter Seven §4.4). (3) The gap rule is three bullets; "unplaced seat" and "absent, excluded, captured, or conflicted holder" are now named together, matching the map list. (4) "You can't bypass the independence requirement just because you're in a hurry" became "however urgent the act". (5) The certification-seat clock now links to Chapter Seven §4.7.4 *Certification seats*, not §4.7. (6) The §5 reading-order line now reads "published placement, substitutes, and delegation". The §4 automated-routing bullet now says a router follows the §5 substitute or independent path when a holder cannot serve, and holds and logs only when no seat, substitute, or independent path is placed. Derived files regenerated; `owner-line-audit`, `owner-discipline-audit`, the three derived-file checks pass; `plain-language-audit` shows no `core_08` finding. Translations are paused.

**Eighty-second move (Chapter Eight §5 broken into four subsections; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed, no sentence added or dropped apart from the four bold lead-ins that became headings): the four bold lead-ins from the Eighty-first move are now `####` subsections with `<a id>` anchors: **§5.1 What the map must say** (`#51-what-the-map-must-say`), **§5.2 Where the map is kept** (`#52-where-the-map-is-kept`), **§5.3 A seat the map leaves unplaced, or a holder who cannot serve** (`#53-a-seat-the-map-leaves-unplaced-or-a-holder-who-cannot-serve`), and **§5.4 Delegation** (`#54-delegation`). §5 gained a *Reader guidance (non-operative): how this section is laid out* widget ahead of its Trace, in the Chapter Seven §4 pattern. The `#5-published-placement-vacancy-and-substitution` anchor is unchanged, so every existing link into §5 still resolves; none was retargeted. The §4 router bullet now cites §5.3 by number and name. Each subsection opens with prose, so MD-SECTION-LEAD-01 holds. Derived files regenerated. `reference-audit`, `anchor-heading-drift-audit`, `section-bullet-lead-audit`, `section-cite-name-audit`, `owner-line-audit`, `owner-discipline-audit`, `reader-accessibility-check`, and the three derived-file checks pass. `corpus-markdown-audit` still fails on one finding in `translations/bn/core_07_charters.md`, none in `core_08`. Translations are paused. Same sitting: §5.2 is now three paragraphs (where the map is kept; the shared map by reference; Class P), with no wording change.

**Eighty-third move (Chapter Eight §5.4 Delegation: a seat may be delegated only to a deputy or substitute the Charter Role Map names; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; **narrows a rule: delegation outside the map is no longer left open**). The old §5.4 said delegation keeps the seat boundary, evidence duties, record, and clock, but did not say who a seat may be delegated to, so it read as allowing a delegate the map never named. Chapter Seven §4.5 already makes "which offices may hold a seat, act as a substitute, or share hosting" a change to the map's rules (amendment), and a change of current holder a new dated map version with notice and a chance to contest, so a delegation could otherwise bypass both. §5.4 now says: (1) a seat holder may delegate the seat only to a deputy or substitute the map names; (2) a delegation to anyone else leaves the seat unplaced and §5.3 applies; (3) naming a new deputy or substitute changes the map under Chapter Seven §4.5, which a delegation cannot do; (4) a delegation keeps the same seat boundary, evidence duties, record, and clock, the delegate stays under the Material Control Line, and it does not create a seat, merge seats, or let a delegate do what the delegating seat could not; (5) a delegate who holds a second seat on the same act is a merger, allowed only through the merged-hosting arrangements the map lists under §6. The old closing clause ("including by converting a class-scaled recommendation or requirement ... into permission to merge") is replaced by (5). To match, the §5.1 map bullet "the substitute for an absent, excluded, captured, or conflicted holder" now reads "each deputy or substitute who may act in a seat, including for an absent, excluded, captured, or conflicted holder", and the same bullet in the Chapter Five *Charter Role Map* entry was changed the same way. The §5.4 line in the §5 reader guidance follows. **Same sitting, three follow-ups:** (1) CI-14.205 §6 item 5 now opens "A seat is delegated only to a deputy or substitute that the Charter Role Map or lane map names (Chapter Eight §5.4 Delegation); a delegation to anyone else leaves the seat empty and rule 6 applies." (2) CI-8.205 §3 gained a paragraph before its consistency paragraph: a delegation of a seat goes only to a deputy or substitute the Charter Role Map or lane map names, and naming a new one is a change to the map, not a delegation. (3) §5.4 gained a closing paragraph: a holder may use staff or tools to help do the seat's work; that is not delegation and the holder stays answerable for the result and for the seat's independence; help that decides for the seat, or takes a step the seat could not take, is a delegation and must meet §5.4. Also from the Eightieth move's open item: CI-14.205 §6 item 3 (the wrong-seat rule) now says that where the process can route automatically, the router follows the Charter Role Map or lane map and logs each route, and the rule is the fallback when routing is not automated or fails (Chapter Eight §4 Wrong-Seat Routing). Derived files regenerated; the lexical-vocabulary, section-cite-name, reference, owner-line, owner-discipline, reader-accessibility, and derived-file checks pass. Translations are paused. The assistance paragraph in §5.4 was reworded for clarity on 2026-10-22: staff or tools may help with the seat's work (gathering evidence, drafting, routine checks) as assistance, not delegation; the holder still makes the seat's decisions and stays answerable; if someone else makes a decision or takes a step in the seat's name, that is a delegation under §5.4; assistance cannot be used to do what the seat itself could not do, including pressuring the holders of other seats to determine the outcome of an act.

**Eighty-fourth move (Chapter Eight §6 Proportional Scaling and Merged Hosting broken into six subsections; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed, no sentence added or dropped apart from the bold labels that became headings or were dropped): §6 was a lead paragraph, a six-item merger list, a one-paragraph informal-work rule, and a long *Seats held in common* bullet list that also held the joint-professional-practice rule and the stake limit. It now has `####` subsections with `<a id>` anchors: **§6.1 How separation scales** (`#61-how-separation-scales`), **§6.2 Merging two seats in one office** (`#62-merging-two-seats-in-one-office`), **§6.3 Informal and community work** (`#63-informal-and-community-work`), **§6.4 Seats held in common** (`#64-seats-held-in-common`; the five independence bullets stay together), **§6.5 Joint professional practice** (`#65-joint-professional-practice`; was a bullet of §6.4), and **§6.6 Stake, class, and what does not justify a merger** (`#66-stake-class-and-what-does-not-justify-a-merger`; the *Stake still counts* bullet plus the closing paragraph). A *Reader guidance (non-operative): how this section is laid out* widget sits ahead of the §6 Trace. The `#6-proportional-scaling-and-merged-hosting` anchor is unchanged, so every existing link into §6 still resolves, including the cites that name *Seats held in common* in Chapter Seven §4.4 and CI-14.205 §6 item 8. One link was retargeted: §5.2's shared-map cite now points to §6.4. The *Stake still counts* bullet and the joint-practice bullet are now paragraphs, so "The rules above apply to it" in §6.5 refers to §§6.2 to 6.4. A word-count comparison against `HEAD` shows only the labels removed. Derived files regenerated; the lexical-vocabulary, section-cite-name, reference, anchor-heading-drift, section-bullet-lead, heading-echo, heading-runin-echo, owner-line, owner-discipline, reader-accessibility, and derived-file checks pass. Translations are paused. The §6.2 bullet "verify-and-record and verify-and-contest remain prohibited" was reworded on 2026-10-22 to "the verify-or-authorize seat is never merged with the record seat or the contest seat" (same rule, seat names as in §2), and CI-14.205 §6 item 8 ("never the verify-and-record or verify-and-contest pairing") now says "never the verify-or-authorize seat with the record seat or the contest seat."

**Eighty-fifth move ("segregation of duties" renamed "separation of duties" across the corpus; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, chose "separation of duties" and the whole corpus; uncommitted; **a rename, no rule changed**): the corpus named the seat rule "segregation of duties" (chapter title, Preamble §5, Chapter One §18.3, Chapter Ten §3.7; 44 uses in core, 15 in implementation text) but described it as "separation" ("full four-seat separation", "functional separation", Preamble §5.1 *Four-seat separation map*, CJS-5.125 §11, CI-8.205). It is now "separation of duties" everywhere. **Renamed:** Chapter Eight is *Functional Independence and Separation of Duties*, file `core_08.000_functional_independence_separation_of_duties.md` (was `..._segregation_of_duties.md`); Chapter One §18.3 is *Separation of Duties*; Chapter Ten §3.7 is *Separation of duties*. **Anchors changed:** `#chapter-eight-functional-independence-and-separation-of-duties`, `#183-separation-of-duties`, `#37-separation-of-duties`, and the Preamble §5 anchor. No redirect from the old file name or old anchors. **Scope:** 49 live files, 206 lines (core chapters, CJS, CI, CF, README, TODO nav line, guides, docs, evaluation, implementation, and `tools/` file-name lists, registries, and tests). Two sentences that used bare "segregation" for this rule (Chapter One §19.3 and the Chapter Five *Integrative* band, "segregation keeps it from being performed by the actor under review") now say "separation of duties keeps it". **Left as is, different meaning:** "escrow or segregation of the contested weight, funds, or keys" (Chapter Five *Accountability*, Chapter Thirteen, CI-11.215), the "segregation" bullets in Chapter Six Part C and Chapter Twelve, and data-handling "segregation" in CS-6.305, CF-13.430, and CF-13.435. "Separation of powers" (CI-8.205) and "functional separation" were already the right word and are unchanged. **Not touched (dated records):** earlier entries in this log, the `**Fails:**` example paragraph in the rules section, the `original_file` field in `tools/architecture/chapter_numbers.json` (the pre-numbering file name), `archive/`, `evidence/`, `project/`, and `translations/`. **Translations:** about 37 translation files link to the English Chapter Eight file name or old anchors, and the translation files carry their own old names (`core_07_functional_independence_segregation_of_duties.md`); `translation-link-audit` was already failing before this change on older numbering. A translation run was active while this was done, so none was edited. After it finishes, retarget links to the English file and anchors. Derived files regenerated (ai_corpus, plain-terms edition, boundary chunks, print pack, reader accessibility, pages corpus index). `reference-audit`, `anchor-heading-drift-audit`, `lexical-vocabulary-audit`, `section-cite-name-audit`, `section-bullet-lead-audit`, `heading-echo-topic-audit`, `heading-runin-echo-audit`, `owner-line-audit`, `owner-discipline-audit`, `footer-audit`, and the derived-file checks pass. `_pages_site` (git-ignored) still has the old name until rebuilt.

**Eighty-sixth move ("segregation" added to the do-not-use list; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed): after the Eighty-fifth move the word *segregation* still stood for other ideas and could pull the seat rule back to the old name, so it is banned like *instrument* and *verifier*. **Audit:** `make lexical-vocabulary-audit` rule `avoid-segregation` rejects segregation, segregate, segregated, and segregating in prose (code spans, code fences, link targets, and `id="..."` are skipped). It runs on the same files as `avoid-instrument` (numbered `core_*` and `corpus_*` files, `implementation/`, README, START_HERE, `guides/`, `docs/`, `evaluation/`, `project/`), except dated records and `project/translation_drafts/` and `project/MEMLOG.md`; `doc_architecture.md` and `tools/` are not scanned. It has an internal regression case and a line in the audit report. `tools/architecture/lexical_guardrails.json` gained the advisory entry (avoid *segregation*; prefer *separation of duties* for the seat rule, and *separate holding*, *isolation*, or *separation* otherwise). **Rewrites to reach zero, meaning kept:** "escrow or segregation of the contested weight, funds, or keys" is now "escrow or separate holding of ..." (Chapter Five *Accountability*, Chapter Thirteen, CI-11.215); the Chapter Twelve disputed-rewards list says "separate holding"; the Chapter Six Part C remedy list (tainted information) says "isolation"; data handling in CS-6.305, CF-13.430, and CF-13.435 says "isolation". **Also done:** the Eighty-fifth move skipped `project/`; the live files there (`PSI_DIAGRAMS.md`, `PROCESS_SYSTEM_INSTITUTION_CROSSWALK.md`, `CONSTITUTIONAL_REGRESSION_SCENARIOS.md`, `plans/stewardship_pillars_overview_plan.md`) now say "separation of duties". `project/trials/agent_cooperative_2026-10-20/` is a dated record and keeps the old wording. `lexical-vocabulary-audit` and the reference, anchor, section-cite, owner, footer, and derived-file checks pass. Translations still carry the old word (paused).
**Eighty-seventh move (*independent* defined in Chapter Five; "disinterested" added to the do-not-use list; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed): the corpus used *disinterested* for the body that relies on a record and *independent* for the seat rule, and Chapter Five defined neither. The corpus keeps *independent*. **Definition:** Chapter Five gains `#### Independence` (anchors `independence`, `independent`; Governance architecture cluster, second after *Governance*; primary-only measurement tier): a party is independent of an act when it is outside the [Material Control Line](core_05.035_band_accountability.md#material-control-line) of the party whose act is at issue and of every [Interested Party](core_05.035_band_accountability.md#interested-party), and is not itself an Interested Party; it is judged act by act on actual power and stake, not on title; independence also needs authority, access to the evidence, and the ability to decline, condition, or route. Expertise or association is not enough, independence is not indifference, and seat placement stays in Chapter Eight. The A to Z directory in `core_05.000`, `tools/architecture/measurement_tier_seeds.json`, and the expected order in `tools/ch5_cluster_order_audit.py` follow. The Charter Role Map entry names "the independent route" for an unplaced seat. **Audit:** `make lexical-vocabulary-audit` rule `avoid-disinterested` rejects the word in prose (code spans, fences, link targets, and `id="..."` skipped) on the same files as `avoid-instrument`, with an internal regression case, a report line, and an advisory entry in `tools/architecture/lexical_guardrails.json` (prefer *independent*, usually *independent of* the party or act; *impartial* only for the sense of unbiased). `_DATED_RECORD` now also matches dated folders (`..._2026-10-20/`), so `project/trials/agent_cooperative_2026-10-20/` keeps its old wording. **Rewrites to reach zero, meaning kept:** 15 live uses in Chapter Eight §6.3 and §6.4 (the §6.3 first use links to the new definition), Chapter Ten §3.7 and the watershed vignette ("independent of the repair"), the Chapter Ten vignette table, Chapter Eleven, CI-6.225, CI-6.255, and the *Authority is also power* bullet above. Archive and move-log history keep the old word. The Chapter Five audits, `lexical-vocabulary-audit`, and the derived-file checks pass. `definition-appropriateness-audit` fails on a missing function (`audit_cjs3_letter_headings`) in the committed tools, not on this change. Translations still carry the old word.
**Eighty-eighth move (Chapter Eight §6.3 Informal and community work moved after the joint professional practice subsection; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, proposed in session and agreed; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed, no sentence added or dropped): §6.3 *Informal and community work* is the short general case of placing a check with an outside independent body, and the two subsections that spell out the independence rules for such placements (*Seats held in common*, *Joint professional practice*) read better before it, with §6.6 *Stake, class, and what does not justify a merger* still last. New order: §6.1 *How separation scales*, §6.2 *Merging two seats in one office*, §6.3 *Seats held in common* (was §6.4), §6.4 *Joint professional practice* (was §6.5), §6.5 *Informal and community work* (was §6.3), §6.6 unchanged. The anchors (`63-seats-held-in-common`, `64-joint-professional-practice`, `65-informal-and-community-work`), the reader-guidance bullets, and the one in-chapter cite (§5.2, now §6.3 *Seats held in common*) follow. No other file cites these subsections by number; cites to Chapter Eight §6 as a whole stand. Translations still carry the old layout.
**Eighty-ninth move (translation links repaired in all 19 locales; two broken checks fixed; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session, scope "link repair only"; uncommitted; no translated text changed): `make translation-link-audit` now reports 0 unresolved targets, down from about 103,000 (87,400 missing files, 15,600 missing fragments). The translations had been written against three older numbering schemes and the pre-migration subfolder names. **Tool:** `tools/translation_link_repair.py` (dry run by default; `--write` applies; listed in `tools/README.md`). It maps a historical English file name to the current file from the git rename history, the chapter-number registry, and a layer-and-slug match; it points the link at the locale's own translation when there is one and at the English file (`../../...`) otherwise; it replaces a stale fragment only by the one current anchor with the same heading text once the old number is ignored, and otherwise drops the fragment; and for a link into a translation it adds `<a id="...">` above the translated heading aligned by section number (or by position when both files have the same outline), falling back to the English file when no heading aligns. **Result:** 561 files changed; 35,586 link targets rewritten and 513 anchor lines added; a line-by-line comparison shows no change outside link targets and anchor lines. **For review:** `evidence/2026-10-22/translation_link_repair_report.md` lists 4,517 links whose fragment was dropped (the English heading is gone or was retitled, for example renumbered Article XXIV to XXVII headings in Chapter Six) and 5,188 links sent to English because no translated heading could be aligned. **Also fixed:** `definition-appropriateness-audit` called two functions that had been renamed (`audit_cjs3_letter_headings`, `audit_cjs3_compass_and_frames`) and now calls `audit_operational_letter_headings` and `audit_compass_and_frames`; `copy-suffix-check` crashed on unreadable " 2" sync copies, so `tools/copy_suffix_clean.py` falls back to a size comparison, and the 60 copies (all of files that exist, none with unique content) were removed. **Not done (outside "link repair only"):** the translations still describe the old Chapter Eight layout and carry the old words (*segregation*, *disinterested*); `translation-structure-audit` and `translation-freshness-audit` still fail on content drift, which needs a re-translation run.
**Ninetieth move (Chapter Eight §6.3 first bullet clarified; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed): the first independence bullet under *Seats held in common* said the holder "is outside the initiating business's Material Control Line and has no interest that the act determines", and that a dependent party "is not outside that line for an act that concerns the dependence". The bullet now leads with *Independent of the business*, links the Chapter Five *Independence* definition, keeps the control-line test and the no-stake test, and gives an example (a dispute over the supplier's own contract) of an act that gives a dependent supplier, lender, landlord, or customer a stake of their own, so it is not independent for that act.
**Ninety-first move (Chapter Eight §6.3 "The group's own acts" given sub-bullets; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed, no sentence dropped): the bullet now ends its first sentence with "For these acts the group:" and lists three sub-bullets: takes the four seats; publishes its own System Charter or Institutional Charter; keeps an independent route outside itself (another group, a forum, or a public office), so it never judges itself or a member it controls.
**Ninety-second move (Chapter Eight §6.3: groups may set admission limits; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the old last bullet, *No permission and no trap*, said a group's terms "may not be used to shut a business out", which would have forced a cooperative to admit any applicant, including one that would use up its capacity. It is replaced by four bullets. **No compulsion:** no business is required to join, and no group is the only route (unchanged). **Admission limits:** a group may refuse or defer an applicant for a reason tied to what it can serve, with four named reasons as sub-bullets: *Capacity* (expected volume of material acts would use up capacity existing members rely on; the group may cap the volume it takes from one member or keep a waiting list), *Fit* (the applicant's kind of acts or class is outside what the group's holders are qualified and staffed to serve, tied to §6.6), *Independence* (the applicant is inside the Material Control Line of one of the group's holders), and *Conduct* (the applicant broke the group's published terms before or will not accept them). **Limits on the limits:** the limit is published before it is applied, applies the same way to like applicants, is not used to shield members from competition or make the group the only route in its area, the refused applicant is told the reason in writing, and refusing or admitting is an admitting act under *The group's own acts*, so it is contestable through the independent route outside the group. **No trap:** terms may not make the seat a cost the business cannot bear (Avoidable Burden), and the group may not keep a member's Act Records when the member leaves (both unchanged). The list intro now reads "The same independence rules apply as to any placement, together with these rules for the group:". The regression scenario that cited the old wording (RS shared-seat scenario in `project/CONSTITUTIONAL_REGRESSION_SCENARIOS.md` and `tools/architecture/regression_scenarios.json`) now expects published, evenly applied, contestable admission limits. The Twenty-fourth move entry above keeps its original wording as history. Other cases a group might name (for example a minimum record-keeping standard) are for the group's own published terms and were not added.
**Ninety-third move (Chapter Eight §6.3: "Limits on the limits" renamed; admission limits stated in the group's System Charter or Institutional Charter; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session and agreed; uncommitted): the bullet *Limits on the limits* is now *How the limits are applied*, because it is a fair-process rule on how a group uses its admission power and not an authority claim (an authority claim is what a System Charter says the party holds, Chapter Seven §3.1 item 3, "a claim, not a grant"). The bullet now begins "The group states its admission limits in its System Charter or Institutional Charter ([Chapter Seven §3.1 What a System Charter states]), so each limit is published before it is applied", which places the limits among the in-scope and out-of-scope items of its System Charter or Institutional Charter and meets the publish-first rule that way. The rest of the bullet (applied the same way to like applicants; not used to shield members from competition or to make the group the only route in its area; reason given in writing; contestable through the independent route outside the group) is unchanged.
**Ninety-fourth move (Chapter Eight §6.3 "How the limits are applied" given sub-bullets; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed): the bullet now opens "Every admission limit:" and lists five sub-bullets: stated in the group's System Charter or Institutional Charter so it is published before it is applied; applies the same way to like applicants; not used to shield members from competition or to make the group the only route to a seat in its area; explained in writing to a refused applicant; and contestable through the independent route outside the group, because refusing or admitting an applicant is an admitting act under *The group's own acts*. The sentence about the written reason became "is explained in writing to a refused applicant"; the other wording is as before.
**Ninety-fifth move (Chapter Eight §6.3: "admitting act" clarified; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed): the last sub-bullet of *How the limits are applied* said that refusing or admitting an applicant "is an admitting act under *The group's own acts*", but *The group's own acts* does not use that phrase; it lists "admitting or removing a member" as a materially binding act of the group. The sub-bullet now says the limit "can be contested through the independent route outside the group, because deciding whether to admit an applicant is a materially binding act of the group (see *The group's own acts*, above)". Deciding whether to admit covers admitting, refusing, and deferring. The Ninety-second and Ninety-fourth entries keep the earlier phrase as history.
**Ninety-sixth move (Chapter Eight §6.3: leaving a group tied to the data types; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): the old closing clause "a group may not keep a member's Act Records when the member leaves" was too blunt. It ignored the data types in CS-6.310 §9, and it conflicted with Chapter Eight §8, which does not let an Act Record be erased while it is needed for challenge, correction, or remedy. *No trap* now holds only the cost rule (Avoidable Burden). Two new bullets follow. **Leaving:** a member that leaves takes its records with it, and the group may not hold them back or make leaving depend on leaving them behind. The group gives a full export of the member's Act Records and the data in them in a usable form (CS-6.305 §1.2, with any lawful limit on export disclosed up front); hands the record seat for those acts to the holder the member names and stays the record seat holder until the member names one (§8); and keeps only what its own duties still require (for example an act it hosted for another member, or one under challenge) for the retention period it disclosed (CS-6.305 §1.3). **Data kept by the group:** handled by type under CS-6.310 §9, with five sub-bullets: Type I used only to verify who held each seat and who acted; Type H used only for the record, audit, and challenge duties of the act; Type G open to audit and not public by default; Type S kept only for its time-bound review; Types Y and W returned to the member and deleted from the group's copies when the member directs. The shared-seat regression scenario (`project/CONSTITUTIONAL_REGRESSION_SCENARIOS.md`, `tools/architecture/regression_scenarios.json`) follows. No CS-6.305 or CS-6.310 text was changed; the sub-bullets restate the type rules only. The Twenty-fourth and Ninety-second entries keep the old clause as history.
**Ninety-seventh move (Chapter Eight §6.4 Joint professional practice bulleted; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed, no sentence dropped): the paragraph is now a lead sentence ("... may place its seats for those acts with a host:") and six bullets: *Who the host may be*; *What the practice adopts*; *Only for listed acts*; *The same rules*; *The host's added duties* (CI-14.205 §7); *What it settles, and what it does not* (meets the four-seat floor for those acts; does not change the practice's class). The stray comma before "may place" is gone. **Open:** CI-14.205 §7 still says "The host's Act Records for a member are the member's to take when they leave", which is shorter than the Chapter Eight §6.3 *Leaving* rule (full export, record seat handoff, only what the host's duties still require is kept, by data type) that §6.4 applies through "the rules above". Not changed.
**Ninety-eighth move (Chapter Eight §6.4 *Only for listed acts* clarified; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed): "This is not open for work with no listed licence or clearance" left "this" and "listed" open. The bullet now says the route covers only the acts that Article XI-E lists for a practice licence or practice clearance, that for any other work a practitioner may still place seats under §6.3 *Seats held in common*, and that a host's published documents then have no standing as a joint professional practice. The last two points come from CI-14.205 §7 *Only for listed acts*, which also mentions "the direct route"; that phrase is not used in Chapter Eight and was left out here.
**Ninety-ninth move (Chapter Eight §6.4: "no standing" and "the rules above" clarified; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed): (1) *Only for listed acts* said a host's documents "have no standing as a joint professional practice" for other work. It now says that adopting a host's published documents "does not then meet the practice's own class duties for that work, as a joint professional practice would", which is what the standing amounted to under CI-14.205 §7 (a joint professional practice meets the class duties for listed acts by adopting the host's Institutional Charter and Charter Role Map). (2) *The same rules* said "The rules above apply to it". It now names §6.3 *Seats held in common* and lists what carries over: the host's independence from the practice, the four seats and an independent route for the host's own acts, published admission limits, and the rule for a member that leaves. §6.2 (merging two seats in one office) does not apply, because placing seats with a host is not a merger. CI-14.205 §7 *Only for listed acts* was reworded to match ("adopting a host's published documents does not meet the practitioner's class duties, because it is not a joint professional practice").
**Hundredth move (joint professional practice opened to any work; listed acts keep their added safeguards; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session after the Ninety-ninth move: the listed-acts-only rule would split a solo practitioner's business across organizations, and the custodian agreed to widen it; uncommitted). Reverses the Revision 7 limit recorded in `claude/xi-e-practice-clearance-and-practice-host.md` ("Joint professional practice and the host of last resort apply only to acts with a listed licence or clearance"). **What changed:** a sentient or small practice of Class L or higher may meet the duties of that class for its acts by adopting a host's Institutional Charter and Charter Role Map, whether or not the acts are listed for a practice licence or practice clearance, so a practice with both kinds of work can use one host for all of it. **What stays tied to listed acts:** the technical-forum standards that bind the host, the host check that each practitioner holds the licence or clearance, and the public host of last resort. **For other work:** the host admits and refuses only on the Chapter Eight §6.3 admission-limit grounds (capacity, fit, independence, conduct), may not write competence criteria that keep a sentient out of work the Constitution does not gate, and the direct route stays open. The capture safeguards that do not depend on a listed act (no closed shop, direct route, no fixing, not one operation, host replacement window, sampling, the host's own acts taking four seats) already applied to every host. **Files:** Chapter Eight §6.4 (lead sentence; *Any work* replaces *Only for listed acts*; new *Listed acts* bullet; reader-guidance bullet), Chapter Seven §1.1 (joint professional practice bullet), CI-14.205 §7 (summary line, plain-terms line, definition paragraph, *Any work, with more for listed acts*, *Standards are not the host's to set*, the technical-forum and institutional-forum bullets qualified "for a listed act" / "where one applies"). **Also aligned:** the CI-14.205 §7 *What the host carries* sentence on leaving members now points to the Chapter Eight §6.3 *Leaving* rule (full export, record seat handoff) in place of "The host's Act Records for a member are the member's to take when they leave". Article XI-E, Chapter Nine Part D §12.4, and the Domain-Specific Materiality aggregation sentence needed no change. Translations still carry the old layout.
**Hundred and first move (Article XI-E aligned with the Hundredth move; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed). In the *Class of the Operating Party* bullet of the practice licence and practice clearance entry, the sentence "A sentient who performs licensed acts alone, or in a small practice, can meet the duties of Class L through a joint professional practice" now continues: "That route is open for all of the practice's work, listed or not. The host of last resort, the technical-forum standards, and the licence or clearance check apply to the listed acts (CI-14.205 §7)." The *Implementation* bullet now names "the joint professional practice route" among the matters that route to CI-14.205 §7. Nothing else in XI-E mentioned the route. The right to form is unaffected: the requirement still attaches to the sentient who performs the act and never to forming, running, or joining an Operating Party.
**Hundred and second move (Chapter Eight §6.3 and §6.4: "meets the separation-of-duties requirement"; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed). The §6.4 bullet *What it settles, and what it does not* said placing seats "meets the four-seat floor", which was unclear, and added "It does not change the practice's class", which Chapter Seven §1.1 (*Class*) and CI-14.205 §7 (*Class does not move*) already say. It is now **Meets the requirement:** "Joining a host this way meets the separation-of-duties requirement in [§2 Four-Seat Constitutional Floor] for the acts the host's documents cover." The same phrase now closes the §6.3 lead paragraph ("It is how a business too small to staff four seats still meets the separation-of-duties requirement"), which used to say "still has them". **Language to keep using:** separation of duties is the requirement; the four seats are how it is met; joining a host (or placing seats with a group) meets it for the acts the host covers; the section name stays *Four-Seat Constitutional Floor* so cites keep working. Chapter Seven §1.1 and CI-14.205 §7 talk about meeting the duties of the class and the duty to publish a System Charter or Institutional Charter, which is wider than this requirement, so they were not changed.

**Hundred and third move (Chapter Eight: "separation-of-duties requirement" carried through; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, "Please", accepting the offer to carry the phrase into other Chapter Eight passages; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed). Chapter Eight now calls separation of duties the requirement and the four seats the way it is met, in the file header, the owner block ("the separation-of-duties requirement for materially binding acts, met through distinct seats"; "Chapter Nine applies this requirement"), §1 (owner sentence and the Class P paragraph, "The requirement applies from the day…"), the §2 plain-terms line, the §2 "This requirement applies both to…" line, the class paragraph at the end of §2 ("The separation-of-duties requirement applies at every class, and the four seats are how it is met"), the §9 Chapter Ten bullet ("meet the separation-of-duties requirement through the four seats"), and the §9 closing rule ("It may not narrow this requirement"). **Left as they were:** the section name *Four-Seat Constitutional Floor* and every cite to it (the section-cite-name audit depends on them); the reading-order nickname "§2 four-seat floor"; "Rights Floors"; and the Chapter Seven §1.1 and CI-14.205 §7 wording, which speaks of the duties of the class and the duty to publish a System Charter or Institutional Charter, wider than this requirement. Other chapters that still say "four-seat floor" (about 17 live lines outside Chapter Eight, for example Chapters Five, Nine and Ten and the CI files) were not touched; they can follow in a later sweep if wanted.

**Hundred and fourth move (Chapter Eight §2 renamed Four-Seat Constitutional Requirement; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; naming only, no rule changed). "Floor" was too easy to confuse with the Chapter Six Rights Floor, so Chapter Eight §2 is now **Four-Seat Constitutional Requirement**, and the short form is **Four-Seat Requirement**. The anchor is now `#2-four-seat-constitutional-requirement` (the `<a id>` in Chapter Eight and every link and cite changed with it: about 57 live links and cites, 12 lower-case title cites now spelled as the heading, 8 running-text uses of "four-seat floor", and 261 link fragments in the translations). Also changed: the Preamble §5 and §8 lines now say "Chapter Eight's separation-of-duties requirement" (they said "functional-independence floor" and "separation-of-duties floor"), the CJS/CI/guide cites, and Part D's anti-relocation line ("Chapter Eight seat floor" became "Chapter Eight Four-Seat Requirement"). The Chapter Eight §2 plain-terms line now names it: "separation of duties is the requirement for every materially binding act (the Four-Seat Requirement), and four jobs are how it is met". **New ban rule `avoid-four-seat-floor`** in `tools/lexical_vocabulary_audit.py` (with a regression case) and `tools/architecture/lexical_guardrails.json`: it rejects "four-seat floor", "four-seat constitutional floor" and "Chapter Eight seat floor" in the same files as the instrument ban. **Not changed:** earlier entries in this file and the dated evidence folders (history keeps the old name); the translated text of the heading, which stays in the old wording until the translations are rerun (only the English anchor and link fragments were updated, so links still resolve); Chapter Seven's "System Charter or Institutional Charter floor" wording and "Forum appointment independence floor" in Chapter Fourteen, which are different uses of "floor" and were not asked for.

**Hundred and fifth move ("floor" retired for Chapter Seven, Chapter Eight and the forum appointment rule; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, "Yes please"; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed). Following the Hundred and fourth move, "floor" is kept for the Chapter Six Rights Floor, so these uses now say "requirement": **Chapter Seven** (header "the requirement to publish scope documents for a system and for an institution"; the Class P paragraph "The requirement applies from the day…"; the owner line "the requirements in this chapter bind as core text"); **Chapter Fourteen §1.2** (the bold heading is now *Forum appointment independence requirement*, with "This requirement is mechanism-neutral" and "must not narrow this requirement"); **Preamble §5 and §8** ("Chapter Seven's System Charter or Institutional Charter requirement", "the separation-of-duties requirement that makes every act in this chain validly checkable"); **Chapter Nine Part A** upstream line and the **Conceptual Overview** ("separation-of-duties requirement", were "functional-independence floor"); **CI-8.205 §1** ("Chapter Seven holds the requirement… the constitutional requirement… the template and procedure for that requirement"). **Not changed:** the many other named "floor" terms that belong to Chapter Six or to process chapters (Anti-Delay Floor, Adjudication Floor, Restriction Floors, owner floors, timing floors in CF-13.445, the Chapter Five definition *Publication Truthfulness and Recklessness Floor*, the Article titles *Labor and Economic Floor* and *Sentience-Status Adjudication Floor*, the other Chapter Fourteen floors such as political-equality, integrity and verifiability, and the portfolio floor in Chapter Nine Part D). Retiring those needs a separate decision because several are Article or definition titles.

**Hundred and sixth move ("floor" inventory and Chapter Seven/Eight leftovers; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian, "yes please", asked for an inventory of every "floor" that is not the Rights Floor; uncommitted; wording only). **Inventory:** `evidence/2026-10-22/floor_term_inventory.md` lists 2,161 mentions in 277 phrases outside the Rights Floor itself, in three groups: (A) 759 mentions in 142 phrases (named floors plus phrases found only in Chapters Zero, One, Five and Six) that belong to the Rights Floor family (owner, restriction, sentience-status adjudication, economic, survival, anti-displacement, welfare, dignity, anti-delay and the other Chapter Six Article floors and their Chapter Five definitions), recommended to keep; (B) the Chapter Seven, Chapter Eight and Chapter Fourteen uses already renamed; (C) 286 mentions in 134 phrases that need a decision (for example CF-13.445 "timing floors", the Chapter Eleven §9.2 "remedy-parity funding floor", the CJS-5.105 to 5.135 "X floor" headings, the Chapter Fourteen political-equality and competency floors, the Chapter Nine "portfolio floor" and "clean-recertification floor"), plus 1,116 bare "the floor / this floor" uses that refer back to a floor named in the sentence before. **Leftovers renamed:** the Preamble "What it owns" line for Chapter Eight ("The cross-process requirement that every Materially Binding Act assign four functions separately"); "principle-layer floor" became "principle-layer requirement" in Chapter Ten §3.7, CI-14.205, CI-8.205 and the Steward Entry Doors page; the Conceptual Overview ("owns the cross-process requirement"); "amendment and review floor" became "amendment and review rules" in the Chapter Five definitions of System Charter and Institutional Charter; and the two process-diagram notes in `project/` now say Chapter Eight is a requirement. **Not changed:** dated review files and evaluation runs, which keep their wording as history.

**Hundred and seventh move (Group C "floor" phrases renamed; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian: "The changes that you did are good… please just go ahead with your suggestions"; uncommitted; wording and anchors only, no rule changed). "Clock" was considered and not used: TIME-NAME-01 already retires it as a name and the registered names fit better. **Time terms:** CF-13.445 §3.1 is now *Stage targets and outer bounds* (was "…timing floors"; anchor `#cf-13445-31-stage-targets-and-outer-bounds`, about 30 cites); the tier-default targets say "adopters may only shorten them" (was "adopter floor"); "Tier B floor" is "Tier B intake target"; "Tier A/B urgency floors" is "urgency levels"; "Numeric tier floors" is "Numeric tier targets"; "cadence floors" is "certification intervals"; "remedy-organ and intake timing floors" is "intake targets"; the CJS-5.110 §6 and CI-8.215 "Incident and assurance notification urgency floor" is "…urgency levels". **Operating minimums:** about 35 joint-structure headings and their cross-cites in the CI, CF and CS files changed from "X floor" to "X minimum" (cluster, compliance, auditability, class-scaled template, structured review and incentive-integrity, external assurance trigger, dissolution-review trigger, emergency support-bundle, challenge-pathway accessibility, access-burden, anti-abuse, graceful degradation and the rest), with their `<a id>` anchors and every link fragment updated, translations included. **Process titles:** *Composition minimum* (CJS-18.110 §1.1), *Lived-condition minimums* (CS-6.320 §11), *Remedy-parity funding minimum* (Chapter Eleven §9.2), *Funding minimum* and shared capacity minimum (CI-11.205), clean-recertification minimum, classification minimum, *Binding minimums that drive cost* (Minimum Viable Adopter), Class C minimum (Forum Foundation Kit). **Requirements:** Chapter Fourteen's integrity, verifiability, incentive-integrity and no-single-point-of-failure floors, the authorized-role and competency floor, and CI-14.205 §1 *Shared staffing and competency requirement* (anchor `#ci-14205-1-shared-staffing-and-competency-requirement`). **Rule:** Chapter Nine Part D "Portfolio floor" is *Portfolio rule*. **Kept as floors on purpose** (Rights Floor family or Chapter Fifteen): political-equality, durable political-voice, constitutional choice equality, Stakeholder System Participation, public floor, Type O floor, the Article XX-B floors (imprisonment, anti-discrimination, destructive disposition), the Chapter Fifteen non-regression floors, the Article XIII-A and XIII-F floors, essential-access and creation floors, and the reader-facing "thin public floor", "organize floor" and "business-creation floor" lines. **Undecided:** Chapter One §11 "Market Structure floor" and the CJS-5.130 "governing floor". The inventory file was regenerated and now reflects this state. **Audit tooling touched by this move:** the section-abbreviation descriptor audit now accepts a descriptor with nested parentheses or a dash or colon lead-in; the corpus reference-name audit accepts a link fragment that is a real heading or `<a id>` anchor in the owner file (for example an operational-definition `-a` or `-c` anchor); the translation-link repair report is split by locale under `evidence/2026-10-22/translation_link_repair/` to stay under the file-size limit. The CS-6.310 descriptor was corrected to *Data classifications* on the lines this move touched, and Chapter Fourteen now cites CI-14.205 §1 through §5 by section anchor. The full audit report passes 105 of 105 targets.

**Hundred and eighth move (Market Structure "floor" renamed to "requirements"; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian: "Market Structure Requirements would be good"; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed). The Chapter One §11 non-concentration discipline is now the **Market Structure requirements** (was "Market Structure floor"; the bold lead "Constitutional floor:" is "Constitutional requirements:"; "above the floor" is "that breaches the requirements"; "floor preservation" is "requirement preservation"). The same wording is carried through Chapter One §11.1 and its plain-terms lines, CJS-5.125 §11.1 and §12 ("Requirement preservation" bullet, "nullify the constitutional requirements", "breach the … Market Structure requirements"), Chapter Five accountability (the market-structure rows), Chapter Twelve §5.1 and the steward owner index. The CJS-5.130 §17 "governing floor" (personal-service pathway integrity) is *governing rule*, since it names the rule in force and not a constitutional floor. Survival-floor, essential-environment and Rights Floor uses are unchanged. Translations still carry the earlier wording. **CS-6.310 descriptor:** the 59 cites that named CS-6.310 as *Information types and handling* (the Part A title) now read *Data classifications*, the title of CS-6.310 Part B; the pair-level phrase "CS-6.305 and CS-6.310 — Information types and handling" is unchanged. The custodian considered "Information Type Handling Requirements" and kept the existing title. The same pass corrected the CS-9.310 descriptor (*System impact classifications*, was "System classification machinery" or "System classification and handling") in about 90 cites, replaced two enumerated CJS-5.110 to 5.135 lists (CJS-18.105, CS-6.315) with a pointer to CJS-5.105, named CJS-5.115 §3 *Audit process home* in CS-6.305, and pointed the CS-9.305 lifecycle cite at CS-6.345 §7.

**Hundred and ninth move (Chapter Nine reading index folded into Part A; 2026-10-22).** Decision (custodian: an index like this is not appropriate in the core; fold the useful content into the next Chapter Nine file and consolidate; uncommitted; reader aids only, no rule changed). `core_09.000_system_alignment_certification.md` was the 25-line pointer left when the single Chapter Seven file was split into Parts A and B (2026-07-09). It had grown to 127 lines of reader aids that repeated Parts A, B and D. It is removed. (The Part files were renumbered in the hundred and tenth move; file names in this entry are those of the time.)
