**Hundred and twelfth move (Chapter Nine Part A tidied; run sheet moved to a new systems family CS-9.315; 2026-10-23).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; reader aids and structure only, no rule changed). Links, labels and the D13 numbers (`(9.000 §2.3)`) were retargeted in Part A, Part B, Part C, Part D, Chapter Five (`core_05.040`), CS-6.345 and CS-9.315. Earlier dated entries keep the old numbers. **Hundred and fourteenth move ("level" removed from the forum urgency wording; 2026-10-23).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no clock or duty changed). First step toward limiting "tier" to the forum urgency tiers and freeing "level" for other uses. (1) **Time-scale levels** (Chapter Thirteen §6) are now **time-scale settings** (anchor `#time-scale-settings`; the section already said "settings" and "slower setting"). In CF-13.445 §3.1 the table header reads **Setting**, and the columns read Setting 1 Standard, Setting 2 Coordinated and Setting 3 Heavy. The **Emergency level** is now the **Emergency setting** everywhere it is named (Chapter Thirteen, Chapter Seven, CF-13.415, CF-13.430, CF-13.445, CI-8.205, CJS-5.110, CJS-5.125, CJS-0.105, the Conceptual Overview). (2) **Minimum urgency levels** (CF-13.445 §2.3) are now **minimum urgency categories**, with urgency subcategories and local categories (anchor `#cf-13445-23-minimum-urgency-categories`; the three `new_anchor` rows in `chapter_crosswalk.json` follow it). (3) Small fixes: "Tier A/B urgency levels" in Chapter Thirteen is "urgency windows"; "Forum urgency levels and timeframes" in the Conceptual Overview is "Forum urgency tiers and timeframes"; the vignette column "Tier B (urgency level)" is "(urgency tier)". (4) **Kept on purpose, to decide:** the Notification Urgency Levels (NUL-1 to NUL-4, CJS-5.110 §6) and Correction Urgency Levels (CUL-1 to CUL-4, CJS-5.125 §15). They are named terms with codes, anchors and about forty mentions, and they borrow the tier columns. Renaming them changes their names, codes and anchors. (5) Three time-reference baseline entries were rewritten because their sentences changed (`tools/time_reference_baseline.json`, 79 entries, same sentences). The ch1-cjs3 alignment audit pruned its oldest evidence set under `evidence/2026-10-22/` after delete permission was granted, as its prune step does by design. Regenerated: the indexes, plain-terms edition, boundary chunks and corpus index. Not touched: translations, `_pages_site/` and the print pack. Resolved in the Hundred and fifteenth move.

**Hundred and fifteenth move ("tier" limited to the forum urgency tiers; "level" added to the must-be-qualified list; 2026-10-23).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule, clock or duty changed). "Tier" now names only the forum urgency tiers (Tier A/B/C/L/P). (1) **Notification Urgency Levels and Correction Urgency Levels** became **Notification Urgency (NU)** and **Correction Urgency (CU)**, codes **NU-1** to **NU-4** and **CU-1** to **CU-4**; the label "level" is dropped. Anchors: `#incident-and-assurance-notification-urgency-nu` (CJS-5.110 §6; heading *Incident and assurance notification urgency (NU)*) and `#correction-urgency-cu-discipline` (CJS-5.125 §15, with `-a` and `-c`). The time-reference registry patterns follow the codes. In CJS-0.105 the two links to these sections had pointed at the wrong sections and now point at them. (2) **Definition layers (layer, not tier):** measurement layers, per-term layers, primary, secondary and tertiary layer, Layer 1 and Layer 2 in the **Two-layer definition contract** (CJS-18.105 §8; anchor `#cjs-18105-8-two-layer-definition-contract-binding-abstraction--owner-detail`, retargeted in about twenty files). (3) **Level, with the scale named:** *Treatment of Life by Status* is Level 1 to Level 4 (**protection level**); stewardship is **stewardship level** (CSS-A to CSS-C); CJS-5.110 §4 is **Oversight: transparency levels and audit-access terms** (anchor `#transparency-levels-and-audit-access-terms`; CS-6.305 §5.3 anchor `#53-transparency-levels-and-audit-access`), with **access level** and **disclosure level**; two more CJS-5.110 headings were renamed to *Access-level structure and baseline accessibility* and *Audit reconstruction and access-level continuity* (anchors `#access-level-structure-and-baseline-accessibility` and `#audit-reconstruction-and-access-level-continuity`); the severity labels are **severity level** (codes **T1** to **T4** kept); also **evidence level**, **verification level**, **strictness levels**, **practice level** (licence and clearance), and **class or stewardship level** where "class or tier" stood. (4) **Other words:** transition ordering in CS-6.350 §7 and CI-6.275 §4 uses **exposure groups** (not tiers); CS-6.355 uses **Priority 1 to Priority 3**; billing "paid tiers" are **paid plans**; CF-13.415 §1's urgency sorting says **categories**. (5) **New rule `avoid-bare-level`** (`tools/lexical_vocabulary_audit.py`; guardrail entry in `lexical_guardrails.json`): in the files that own a leveled scale (Chapter Five *Participation* band, CS-14.305, CJS-5.110, CJS-5.115, CS-6.305, CS-6.310) "level" must name its scale; numbered forms (Level 1) and "level of …" pass. Expand the scope as other files are swept. (6) Corpus-reference fixes on lines this pass touched: stale names for CS-6.310, CS-9.310 and CJS-5.115 §3, and links that lacked section anchors. Kept on purpose: the legacy anchors `chapter-ten-tiered-anti-constitutional-misconduct` and `3-tier-labels-criterion-counts` (Chapter Twelve), the machine keys `sub_tier` and `sub_tier_display`, the tool name `ch5_measurement_tier_audit`, and dated records (the Chapter One Tier-1 relocation and Chapter XXVII cut lists). Not touched: translations, `_pages_site/`, the print pack.

**Hundred and sixteenth move (bare "the floor" outside the Rights Floor chapters; 2026-10-23).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only). Sweep of every bare "the / this / that / a floor" in the live corpus outside Chapters Zero, One, Five and Six (69 uses). Most refer to a named Rights Floor and stay (Chapter Nine, CS-6.335, CS-6.345, CI-6.275, CI-9.210, the easy-entry guides, Chapter Fourteen's political-equality floor, Chapter Seventeen's Validity-Floor Rule). These were not Rights Floor uses and changed: "a floor set of indicators" (Chapter Thirteen §3) is "a minimum set"; "sets the floor" (emergency measures, Chapter Thirteen §6.1; ballot rules, Chapter Fourteen §4) is "sets the requirements"; "This floor has four limits" (CS-6.305 key custody) is "These requirements have four limits"; "holds the floor / narrow the floor" (CI-8.205 §6, the Chapter Seven formation check) is "requirements"; CF-13.450 "The floor that survives any degraded mode" and CF-13.420 "The floor for review" are "The minimum"; CF-13.420 "meet that floor" is "meet that requirement"; CF-13.435 "Disclosure is a floor" is "the minimum"; the LEQU default is "the minimum credit"; "a floor of 1%" (CJS-5.130, CI-6.265) is "a lower limit of 1%". Also corrected the CS-6.310 label in one CJS-5.110 line. The two earlier open items were already settled in the Hundred and eighth move (Market Structure requirements; "governing rule"). Left alone: the bare "the floor" uses in Chapters One, Five and Six, which refer to a Rights Floor.

**Hundred and seventeenth move (Chapter Nine Part A §4 opening: "does not add to, narrow, or reorder" sentence removed; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian, asked in session whether any of it was needed, then "Please"; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed). The §4 opening (*Evaluations Where Implicated*) ended "It does not add to, narrow, or reorder what any subsection requires." It guarded nothing: the opening states no duty, each of §4.1 to §4.3.1 carries its own trigger ("more than one Chapter Six privacy locus"; "Where Chapter One §7.x applies"), nothing in the opening sets an order, and the §5 opening has no such sentence and works. Same reasoning as the Seventy-ninth move (Chapter Eight "does not relocate" list). The sentence before it said the evaluations apply "where the certification matter materially implicates the locus or principle a subsection names", which could be read as a higher threshold than the "applies" wording in §4.2 and §4.3. The opening now ends: "It groups the whole-system evaluations that apply only where a subsection's own trigger is met." **Left as is:** the sentence under the §3 opening table ("The table is just a guide; it doesn't change what any section requires"), because the table restates triggers and could drift from the subsections. The `_pages_site` copy is a build output and was not edited.

**Hundred and eighteenth move (Chapter Nine Part A §4.1 *Cluster loci* given sub-bullets; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed, no sentence dropped): the bullet now ends its lead sentence with "lives across:" and lists the five Chapter Six loci as sub-bullets (Article VII-A, VII-B, IX, X-A, XIV-A), each with its name unchanged.

**Hundred and nineteenth move (Chapter Nine Part A §4.1 *Type minimums preserved* given sub-bullets; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed, no sentence dropped): the bullet's first sentence now ends with a colon and is followed by three sub-bullets: each applicable type's access posture and handling duties apply in full, with the privacy homes the type names; the most protective applicable handling governs where more than one type fits, and meeting one type's handling does not discharge another's; and "The loci above reach these types:", which now holds the four locus-to-type sub-bullets one level down. The paragraph "The floors run in both directions for open and audit-accessible types:" is a fourth sub-bullet of the same bullet, with its three bullets nested under it.

**Hundred and twentieth move (Chapter Nine Part A §4.1: locus-to-type list merged into *Cluster loci*; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian, asked in session whether the two lists could be combined; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed). *Cluster loci* named the five Chapter Six articles, and *Type minimums preserved* then repeated them with the data type each reaches ("The loci above reach these types"). The type for each article now sits in *Cluster loci* (lead sentence: "lives across the following, each with the data types it reaches:"), and the repeat is deleted from *Type minimums preserved*, which keeps its first sentence and three sub-bullets (types apply in full; the most protective handling governs; the floors run in both directions). **Details:** Article VII-B and Article X-A, which shared one line, are now separate sub-bullets that each carry the Type N text; Article IX has three nested sub-bullets (Type I under IX-A, Type H under IX-B, Type Y under IX-D with the carry-over to Types W, U and T and the reserved rights). Links and wording are otherwise as before. The Hundred and nineteenth move's third sub-bullet ("The loci above reach these types:") no longer exists.

**Hundred and twenty-first move ("No relaxation by read-across" made a principle: Chapter One §15.1.1; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian: "this may be a principle; if not, it should be"; uncommitted). **Finding:** the rule was stated only in Chapter Nine Part A §4.1 (*No relaxation by read-across*), and the Def.C3 *Anti-bypass* line said "Anti-read-across for standard-setting remains as stated under **Def.C3**", which pointed at text that does not exist (it points to its own entry). Existing principles cover the neighboring cases but not this one. The Anti-Segmentation Principle (§15.1.1) stops a matter being split so each part passes; Fullest Protective Effect chooses between two rules that both apply to the same obligation; Chapter Five §2.1 requires all cluster members to be met. None says that assessing members together may not lower one member's standard by borrowing a laxer one from another. **Change (operative, one new sentence of duty):** Chapter One §15.1.1 gains a paragraph, **No relaxation by read-across**: "Assessing a matter as one adds duties; it never lowers one. Each part's own standard controls within its own scope and may not be loosened by importing a laxer standard from another part." The *In plain terms* line gains one sentence ("Putting the pieces back together does not lower any of them: each keeps its own standard"). **Pointers fixed:** the Chapter Nine §4.1 bullet keeps its wording and now cites §15.1.1; the Def.C3 *Anti-bypass* sentence now says "No member's standard is loosened by importing a laxer standard from another member (same section, *No relaxation by read-across*)". Placed in §15.1.1, not a new §15.1.3, so no anchor, directory or Trace changes. The existing "This principle does not" limits (no pulling in unrelated definitions; no change to any Rights Floor provision; cluster statements kept) apply to it. **Incidental fix:** the Chapter Nine §4.1 cite "CS-6.305 Part A §7" (Type O baseline) became "CS-6.305 §7", the form of the heading, because `corpus-ref-name-audit` (changed-lines mode) read "Part A §7" as the file title once the line was touched; the same older form remains at Chapter Five (Public Oversight Baseline Disclosure), unflagged because that line is unchanged. `make audit-report`: the only failures left, section-cite-name-audit (two unnamed cites in CS-6.305 and CS-6.310) and local-markdown-fragment-audit (`plain_terms_edition.md`), also fail on the committed HEAD. **Open:** a Chapter One principle change falls under Chapter Seventeen §1 heightened review; the record and the translations were not updated.

**Hundred and twenty-second move (Chapter Nine Part A §4.1 *Joint-invocation rule* folded into the opening; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian asked whether the rule needed a principle; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed). **Finding:** the principle exists. Chapter One §15.1.1 (Anti-Segmentation) already bars dividing a matter into separate framings, categories, tests or procedures so that one part is met while another materially implicated part is defeated, says meeting one part is not compliance with the whole, and requires the assessment to test the parts together; Chapter Five §2.1 applies it to dependent clusters; §4.3 here already cites it. The §4.1 *Joint-invocation rule* bullet was a third statement of it: the §4.1 opening already says certification must address each locus and that closing under one locus is insufficient. **Change:** the bullet is deleted. Its one added clause (no routing through one locus in a way that lets another be evaded) now ends the opening sentence of §4.1, with a cite to §15.1.1. No Chapter One text changed. No other file referred to the bullet by name.

**Hundred and twenty-third move (Chapter Nine Part A §4.1 reorganized: principles first; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian, asked in session whether to state the related principles first, in a separate list; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed, no sentence dropped). §4.1 was one flat list mixing governing rules, a lookup of loci and types, and what certification must show. It now has the opening sentence (trigger; address each locus; one locus alone is insufficient) and three labeled blocks. **Principles that apply:** *Joint assessment* (no routing through one locus to evade another; the clause that ended the opening sentence in the Hundred and twenty-second move, with its §15.1.1 cite), *No relaxation by read-across*, *Type minimums preserved* (now including "apply in full, together with the privacy homes that type names" as its second sentence), and *Most protective type governs*. **Cluster loci:** the five articles and their types, no longer nested one level down. **What certification must show:** *Participation enablement*; *Open and audit-accessible types* (the three floors, which were a sub-bullet of *Type minimums preserved*); *Data-types evidence*. New wording is limited to the labels and the lead-ins ("These govern how the loci are read together:"; "The floors run in both directions:" under its own label).

**Hundred and twenty-fourth move (Chapter Nine §5 principles and defects moved up to Chapter One; §5.2 consolidated; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian, asked in session). **Chapter One and Chapter Five:** the general rules behind the Chapter Nine §5 domain defects now live at principle level. §5 Truth names selective reporting ("reporting only favorable results while leaving out unfavorable ones"); §6 Trust also fails when reliance is endorsed or continued while documented problems materially threaten a system's alignment or cause misalignment; §15.1 No-Bypass (and the Chapter Five No-Bypass definition, kept in step) widens "owner" to include a supplier, partner, or other system that performs or bears the act, adds "efficiency, cost, innovation, or scale justification" to the allowed-only-if framings, and states that whether a requirement was met is judged on checkable evidence of what the act does, not on the actor's own label, self-report, or paperwork. **Chapter Nine Part A §5:** the intro now carries **Principles that apply** (pointers to Chapter One §15.1 and §15.1.1, System Boundary Integrity, Chapter One §5 and §6) and **Defects that apply** (a breach of those principles; hiding, misstating, breaking up, or handing off information the named Article requires disclosed), extended to every domain evaluation (5.5 gains the disclosure defect; 5.1 gains continued reliance; 5.1 and 5.3 gain the framing defect). Each 5.x defects subsection keeps only its domain-specific items and points to §5 and Chapter One §6; 5.1.4 is a stub. The §5 intro limits are bulleted; §5.1.2 merges its boundaries sentence into the §5 pointer; the 5.2.2 clinical bullet is generalized and its routing-logic bullet says what the record must show. **§5.2:** the trigger and the evaluate-under paragraph moved into the 5.2 lead-in and 5.2.1 was removed, so 5.2.2 to 5.2.4 are now 5.2.1 to 5.2.3 and the Part C illustration is §5.2.4 (anchors and cross-references updated; translations and one-off tools keep the old numbers). Needs the steward's sign-off on the duty extensions.

**Hundred and twenty-fifth move (Chapter Nine Part A §5.3.1 Materiality Trigger made the lead text of §5.3; §5.3.2–§5.3.8 renumbered §5.3.1–§5.3.7; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout and numbering only, no rule changed): the trigger paragraph and its four-item list now open §5.3 directly after the plain-terms widget, as the trigger already does in §5.2, and the separate "5.3.1 Materiality Trigger" heading is gone. Old to new: 5.3.1 → lead text of §5.3, 5.3.2 → 5.3.1 (Meaning of Extraction), 5.3.3 → 5.3.2 (Governing Rules), 5.3.4 → 5.3.3 (Evaluation Requirement), 5.3.5 → 5.3.4 (Map and Record Integrity), 5.3.6 → 5.3.5 (System Class Scaling and Escalation), 5.3.7 → 5.3.6 (Defects and Misalignment), 5.3.8 → 5.3.7 (Part C illustration). Anchors changed to match (`#531-...` to `#537-...`); the §5.3 Trace subsection list, the Part A and Part C corpus-placement lines, the Part C table, and the Part A cross-links to the Part C illustration follow the new numbers. §5.4 to §5.6 keep their "Materiality Trigger and Governing Rules" first subsections. Translations were not touched (they still carry the old numbering; translations are paused). **Wording (same day, custodian):** §5.3 now names the flows as "resources, goods, services, information, or money" in its plain-terms note, the trigger lead-in and bullets, and the two questions in §5.3.3 (what the system takes, what it puts back); a one-line list of typical flows follows the trigger; §5.3.1 says information counts as a flow only when it is itself a shared foundation others rely on (a shared registry, directory, or reference dataset), and data handling stays under §3.4. The examples are illustrative: Chapter Five's Cross-System Extraction list (money, fees, public funding; compute and connectivity; maintenance labor and source capacity; other shared resource flows) is unchanged. **Definition aligned (same day, custodian):** Cross-System Extraction in Chapter Five now lists goods and services supplied through the shared substrate, labor (maintenance, upkeep, and support work) and source capacity, and information that is itself a shared foundation (registry, directory, reference dataset; counted for upkeep and cost, not data handling), beside money, compute and connectivity; its plain-terms line and the Chapter Five cluster summary follow, and §5.3 names labor with the other flows. Article V-A's plain-terms line uses the same words; its duties are unchanged. The out-of-scope data-handling line is unchanged. **Citation fix and audit (same day, custodian):** the "details live elsewhere" bullet in §5.3.2 no longer cites `corpus_systems.md`; it cites CS-6.335 (§7 required allocation categories, §10 evolution of funding models, §12 reference allocation guidance) and CS-6.325 (§3 adaptive allocation requirement, §5 ecosystem risk response), as sub-bullets. New audit `tools/wrapper_cite_audit.py` (REF-WRAPPER-01; `make wrapper-cite-audit`, test `make wrapper-cite-audit-test`) flags every link to or file-name mention of the wrapper outside navigation and incorporation-inventory lines; all 140 existing citations were then corrected (90 by script to the subfile owning the named CS id; the rest by hand, with the section chosen from the sentence's topic), so the audit is blocking and in `make regression`. The links carry heading anchors and titles so `make corpus-ref-name-audit` passes. Also fixed: two unnamed section cites in CS-6.305 and CS-6.310 that failed `make section-cite-name-audit`. Derived files regenerated.

**Hundred and twenty-sixth move (wrapper citations removed for the institutions, joint-structure and forum layers; REF-WRAPPER-01 covers all four wrappers; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian, asked in session: "Cite the specific CI/CF/CJS section"; uncommitted; citation targets only, no rule changed): the 105 body, Trace and Read with citations of `corpus_institutions.md`, `corpus_joint_structure.md` and `corpus_forum.md` in the core chapters and in the CS, CJS and CF subfiles now name and link the subfile that owns the rule, chosen from the sentence's topic (e.g. forum operation and routing → CF-13.405, CF-13.415, CF-13.420; conflict and procedure integrity → CI-8.210, CI-13.205; remedy → CI-11.205; dissolution → CI-11.210; appointment and seat catalog → CI-14.205; System and Institutional Charter adoption → CI-8.205; fiscal treatment and stewardship → CI-6.210, CI-6.215; cross-institution handoffs → CI-13.210). Where a sentence named the layer as a whole ("the joint-structure family") and no single section carries the rule, the wrapper is replaced by the family name in words. Bare-wrapper list items that preceded a list of ids were deleted, and the ids now carry the links. A mis-resolving link helper (it sent several CF and CI ids to the wrong file) was replaced by a file-by-id lookup and the affected links were relinked. `tools/wrapper_cite_audit.py` now checks all four wrappers by default (`--wrapper NAME` narrows it); the on-demand `wrapper-cite-audit-all-wrappers` target and registry row were removed as redundant. Translations were not touched. Derived files regenerated.

**Hundred and twenty-seventh move (principle links for the six Chapter Nine Part A §5 scaling sections; Chapter One §11.4 levels tied to the §5.3.5 step-up; reclassification home settled on Part B §9; 2026-10-24).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted). Evaluation: `claude/ch09-section-5-3-5-principle-links-evaluation.md`. **Links:** §5.1.3, §5.2.2, §5.3.5, §5.4.3, §5.5.4 and §5.6.3 now cite the Chapter One principles behind their bullets, inline at the phrase that does the work, and each §5.1 to §5.6 Trace gained an *Upstream: Principles: Chapter One* entry. Principles used: §5.1 Chapter One §5, §9.1.1, §11.4; §5.2 §3.1, §3.1.1, §7, §11.2, §11.4; §5.3 §9, §11.4, §15.1.1, §18.5; §5.4 §3.1, §3.1.2, §11.4; §5.5 §5.2, §7.1, §11.4; §5.6 §4, §6, §10, §11.4. Choices follow each domain's Rights Floor article's own Upstream list where it has one. **Operative change (custodian confirmed the reading; still needs the steward's sign-off):** the step-up when a system becomes a choke point does not wait for a Chapter One §11.4 concentration level. A crossed level is further evidence; a level that is unset or set too high does not delay the step-up; crossing a level is not by itself misconduct. Stated in Chapter Nine §2.2 (which all six sections now point to), §5.3.5 (its own closing paragraph), Chapter One §11.4 (end of *When a level is crossed*, and its Downstream), and Part B §9 (the dependency-growth reopening trigger). §5.3.5 also now cites the §5.3.3 replaceability factors for "cannot realistically switch". **Inconsistency settled:** the six scaling sections sent reclassification to Part B §7 (Certification Outcomes), §2.2 sent it to Part B §9, and CS-9.305 §3.5 said "reopened under Part B §7". Part B §9 already lists dependency growth and system-class misalignment (CS-9.305 §3.5) as reopening triggers and §7 only records outcomes, so the home is §9. The six sections point to §2.2, which names CS-9.305 §3 and Part B §9; CS-9.305 §3.5 and its Trace now say Part B §9. Part B §7 keeps the outcomes and defects. Translations were not touched. Derived files regenerated. `make audit-report`: 106 of 107; `ch1-cjs3-alignment-audit` passes when run directly and fails in the gate only because its evidence-prune step cannot delete files in this sandbox.

**Hundred and twenty-eighth move (Chapter Nine Part A §5.5.1 Materiality Trigger made the lead text of §5.5; §5.5.2–§5.5.6 renumbered §5.5.1–§5.5.5; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout and numbering only, no rule changed, no sentence dropped): the trigger paragraphs and lists now open §5.5 directly after the plain-terms widget, as §5.2, §5.3 and §5.4 already do, and the separate "5.5.1 Materiality Trigger and Governing Rules" heading is gone. Old to new: 5.5.1 → lead text of §5.5, 5.5.2 → 5.5.1 (Evaluation Requirement), 5.5.3 → 5.5.2 (Profiles in Scope), 5.5.4 → 5.5.3 (System Class Scaling and Escalation), 5.5.5 → 5.5.4 (Defects and Misalignment), 5.5.6 → 5.5.5 (Part C illustration). Anchors (`#552-...` to `#556-...`) changed to match; the §5.5 Trace subsection list and step-up pointer, the Part A and Part C corpus-placement lines, the Part C table and cross-links, and the one cite in CS-9.305 follow the new numbers. §5.6 keeps its "Materiality Trigger and Governing Rules" first subsection. Earlier move entries that name the old §5.5.x numbers are left as written. Translations were not touched (they still carry the old numbering; translations are paused). **Wording (same day, custodian):** §5.5.2 *Profiles in Scope* no longer says "every sentient form and ability profile" and then "every relevant profile category"; the lead names the scope once (with a link to *Accessibility*), and the three bullets are labeled *Kinds of profile*, *How a need shows up* and *What does not satisfy this section*. The list is the one *Accessibility* already uses. **Second pass (same day, custodian, plain language):** bullets relabeled *Profiles that count*, *How needs change over time* and *What does not satisfy this section*; plurals made to agree; short plain glosses for constant, episodic and developmental (the corpus does not define them; they use the ordinary meanings); substrate-interface and compute-interface described in a few words; the two failing patterns made sub-bullets, with "specific affected profile" now "a specific group of affected sentients", "selective-materiality scaling" explained as arguing the system is only partly material to shrink the accommodations owed (the wording §5.5.1 already uses), and the bare "participation floor" replaced by "the participation that Article VI-D (Accessibility) protects". No duty changed. **New requirement (same day, custodian; needs steward sign-off):** a second bullet in §5.5.3 for systems whose billing, collection or cutoff process could cut sentients off from survival essentials when they stop paying or answering. The record must show how it protects sentients who cannot respond because of a cognitive or mental health condition such as severe depression: silence is not consent; a notice a sentient cannot act on is not the meaningful notice Article III-A requires before an essential relationship is ended; and four showings (more than one way to reach the sentient, a chosen trusted contact or advocate reached before any cutoff, review by a sentient reviewer before cutoff for non-response, and a quick low-barrier way back). This adds a duty to the certification record; it adds none to the operator beyond what the record must show. **§5.5.4 (same day, custodian):** a matching third defect: treating silence or non-payment as consent, refusal or a choice to give up the service, and cutting off survival essentials, without the safeguards the record must show under §5.5.3. **Part C and Trace (same day, custodian):** the Class A accessibility illustration (Part C §5.5.5) now lists late-notice, collection and cutoff steps among the pathways in scope, adds a test (silence alone must not cost a sentient with a cognitive or mental health condition their safe water, under the §5.5.3 safeguards) and a record item (non-response safeguard findings, including whether they worked in practice), and its read-across sentence names the safeguards; the §5.5 Trace "Read with" line now lists Article III-A. Class B and Class C illustrations were not changed. **Revision (same day, custodian: systems should be proactive about getting unresponsive sentients help):** the §5.5.3 non-response bullet now says the protection is owed "whatever the reason" and that the system must not need to know why a sentient is silent (so it does not infer a protected internal state from behavior; Protected Internal-State Boundary, Article VII-B); a new showing requires active outreach before any cutoff that tries to connect the sentient with help; and the pre-cutoff review is based on what the system can observe, not on a guess about health or state of mind. The Part C Class A test lists the outreach. Evaluation of a possible Chapter One principle on present capability and proactive help: `claude/present-capability-and-proactive-help-evaluation.md` (project); not adopted.

**Hundred and twenty-ninth move (Chapter One §3.1.5 Present Capability and Proactive Help added; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked to implement the recommendations in `claude/present-capability-and-proactive-help-evaluation.md`; uncommitted; new principle, steward sign-off still advisable). **New text:** Chapter One §3.1 *Fairness* gains a fifth working part, §3.1.5, with five rules: judge present capability for the task at hand (not past conduct, a diagnosis or label, or an average); survival essentials must not depend on a sentient being able to reply; when a sentient who relies on a system for something material stops responding, the system must try to reach them and connect them with help by the least intrusive means, without needing to know why; support before substitution (a sentient's Rights Floor does not shrink with capability; limits meet §7.1); and no guessing at inner states (Protected Internal-State Boundary). Two limits: connecting is not treating (hand-off to a chosen contact, a support or care service, or the Article III-B route; non-consensual action stays under Article VII-C), and trusted contacts and data are consent-based and minimal. The §3.1 intro and closing now say "five" parts, and the §3.1 Trace lists the new subsection and Chapter Nine §5.5.3 downstream. **Links added:** Chapter Nine §5.5 Trace (Upstream: Principles), §5.5.2 (needs that change are judged by present capability) and §5.5.3 (the non-response bullet applies §3.1.5); Chapter Five *Accessibility* (Upstream: Principles) and *Graduated Capability* (Read with); Article VI-D (Read with); and the three role-capacity sentences (Chapter Eleven §6.2 *Material capacity change*, Article XIX-C *Capacity reassessments*, and the work-capacity safeguard in Article XI-E) now say capacity is judged as it is now and cite §3.1.5. **Not changed:** CI-6.220, Part C Class B and C, translations (paused). **Open:** the outreach duty's reach is "something material" (steward may narrow it to survival essentials and healthcare first); the catalogue of acceptable "help" is left to adopted implementation text; what a system may do when a sentient cannot be reached at all (for example, asking for a welfare check) is not designed and needs Article VII-C and privacy analysis.

**Hundred and thirtieth move (Chapter Nine Part A §5.6 applies Chapter One §3.1.5; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; evaluation: `claude/ch09-section-5-6-present-capability-evaluation.md`). Four small additions, no new bullet and no mental-health wording (§3.1.5 is cause-agnostic). **Trace:** §3.1.5 is the first entry under *Upstream: Principles*. **§5.6.2:** the challenge test now reads "as they are now, including when they cannot easily answer or follow a long process" with a §3.1.5 link; the misled test is "judged against the sentients who actually rely on it and not the average sentient". **§5.6.3, first bullet:** the evidence list gains "survival-critical alerts and advisories reach sentients who do not acknowledge them, with follow-up when they go unanswered" (§3.1.5). **Not done (optional, left for the steward):** a §5.6.4 defect on treating silence as proof of being informed (it partly repeats the §5.5.4 defect); a matching Part C Class A test and record item (Class B and C unchanged); links from Article XIII-C and the Chapter Five Trust entries to §3.1.5. Translations paused.

**Hundred and thirty-first move (Chapter Nine Part A §5.6.1 Materiality Trigger made the lead text of §5.6; §5.6.2–§5.6.5 renumbered §5.6.1–§5.6.4; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout and numbering only, no rule changed, no sentence dropped): the trigger paragraph, its list and the "must evaluate under" list now open §5.6 directly after the plain-terms widget, as §5.2 to §5.5 already do, and the separate "5.6.1 Materiality Trigger and Governing Rules" heading is gone. Old to new: 5.6.1 → lead text of §5.6, 5.6.2 → 5.6.1 (Evaluation Requirement), 5.6.3 → 5.6.2 (System Class Scaling and Escalation), 5.6.4 → 5.6.3 (Defects and Misalignment), 5.6.5 → 5.6.4 (Part C trustworthiness illustration). Anchors (`#562-...` to `#565-...`) changed to match; the §5.6 Trace subsection list and its "step-up in §5.6.2" pointer, the Part A and Part C corpus-placement lines, the Part C table, class rows and read-across links follow the new numbers. With this, §5.2 to §5.6 all open with their trigger text; §5.1 (*Scope and Governing Rules*) and §5.3 (*Governing Rules*) keep their governing-rules subsections. The Hundred and thirtieth move above names the section numbers as they were at the time (its "§5.6.2" is now §5.6.1 and its "§5.6.3" is now §5.6.2). Translations were not touched (paused). **Wording (same day, custodian, plain language):** the four tests in §5.6.1 were reworded for ordinary readers ("challenge the system, get a decision reviewed, and get a problem fixed"; "misled ... more than it deserves"; "the rewards built into the system favor deception or cutting corners"; "quietly taking away anyone's rights"). Meaning, cites and the §3.1.5 link are unchanged.

**Hundred and thirty-second move (Chapter Nine Part A §5.6.1 product-information tests; operating terms in CJS-5.120 §10 and CJS-5.125 §15; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): add accurate product safety, health, nutrition, and allergy information to the §5.6.1 tests; keep the accessibility test in Part A and move the other working rules to the operational corpus. §5.6.1 gained three tests: accurate health and safety information on products and services (safety warnings, health claims, nutrition, ingredients, allergens, with unknown or untested stated plainly); usable and timely delivery of that information to sentients with different sensory, cognitive, and language needs; and prompt, reach-the-sentient corrective notice when information is wrong or a product is unsafe. The §5.6 Trace gained a Read-with for Article VI-D and an Operating terms line. Three Participation and Accountability operating entries were added: *Product and service health and safety information* and *Ratings, rankings, and paid promotion* (CJS-5.120 §10), and *Harm reports and corrective notices* (CJS-5.125 §15). Placement rule applied: tests of whether the system works stay in Part A; the working rules (claim substantiation, supplier checks, review and ranking integrity, harm-report intake, notice mechanics) live in the operational corpus. The second §5.6.1 test was also reworded to "more than it deserves" to satisfy the vocabulary audit (see the 131st move note).

**Hundred and thirty-third move (Article III-F Safe Products and Health Information added as a Rights Floor; Article III-A "safe to consume"; Chapter Five Commercial Compliance added; Article XI-B compelled speech and Commercial Compliance; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): make accurate product safety, health, nutrition, ingredient, and allergen information a Rights Floor; add "safe to consume" to the survival floor; and draw a line between compelled speech and Commercial Compliance. **Article III-F** (new, after III-E): safe-to-offer rule (a hazard recipients cannot judge or avoid; a known, disclosed, chosen hazard stays the sentient's decision under Article VII-A); accurate and usable information with unknowns stated; harm reports and corrective notice inside the notice window for the urgency of the harm (CU-1 to CU-4); honest promotion; and the rule that these are conditions of offering, not compelled speech. **Article III-A**: food and water that are safe to consume, and for synthetic and digital entities energy and technical-environment integrity that are safe to use; the duty falls on suppliers and is not a condition on recipients, and a safety doubt may not cut off survival-critical access. The three "III-A through III-E" phrases in Article III now read "through III-F". **Chapter Five, Commercial Compliance** (new, Assembly, collective organization, and institutional formation cluster, after Business Creation): a condition on offering others something that can injure them, sorted by four questions (Offer, Fact, Risk, Least) and two guards (no silencing of warnings or protected reports; no enforcement by cutting off survival-critical access). Practice licence and clearance, certification before go-live, lifecycle records, and product information are named as cases. The Expression entry's out-of-scope note on compelled speech now points to it. **Article XI-B** gained a bullet: no sentient may be made to state, endorse, or carry a message of opinion, belief, identity, or affiliation, and a rule that does so faces highest scrutiny; a condition of Commercial Compliance is not compelled speech. This states a protection that Chapter Five previously implied only by calling compelled speech "not protected expression", so it is a strengthening. **Article XI-E** names practice licence and clearance as cases of Commercial Compliance (no change to their conditions). Read-withs were added on Articles III-D and XIII-A and on Chapter Five *Safe Conditions*; Chapter One §4, §5, and §6 list III-F in their downstream Especially lists; the Chapter Nine §5 table has a row for III-F; the §5.6 tests and the three CJS entries (CJS-5.120 §10 and CJS-5.125 §15) now cite III-F as the owner and Commercial Compliance as the test. `tools/ch5_cluster_order_audit.py` lists Commercial Compliance in the expected members of the Assembly cluster, and the cluster's scope now names three questions. Test 1 self-check: `evidence/2026-10-25/product-safety-test-1-self-check.md`. Not done: Part C illustrations for III-F; a sector file for notice-window figures by product class; a data type for health and allergy profiles; translations (paused).

**Hundred and thirty-fourth move (Chapter Nine §5.6.3 defect and Part C §5.6.4 walkthroughs for Article III-F; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): do the first two items left open after the 133rd move. Part A §5.6.3 gained one certification defect: treating the absence of a warning, or a blank field, as showing that a product or service is safe, instead of saying plainly that it is unknown or untested. Part C §5.6.4 gained Article III-F items for all three example systems. Class A (water): water-quality and contaminant disclosures as reliance pathways, a test that a missing result or clean dashboard does not read as safe water, corrective notice that reaches non-responders inside the CU-1 notice window without cutting off water, and matching record findings. Class B (clinical records exchange): medication, allergy, and adverse-reaction data and recall notices as pathways, a test that a blank allergy field shows as unknown rather than "none", reach of recall and correction notices, adverse-reaction intake, and correct use of a patient's own allergy profile. Class C (scheduling platform): vendor listings with allergen information, ratings and sponsored placement, a test of whether Article III-F applies at all, and a record item stating why it does or does not. The "Reading across system classes" paragraph names the Article and gives one sentence per class. Not done: further defects for burying safety information or notifying only responders (the §5.6.1 tests and Article III-F already cover them); translations (paused).

**Hundred and thirty-fifth move (notice and correction wording for Article III-F; allergy and dietary profiles named in Type I; easy-entry brief E19; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): three follow-ups to the 133rd and 134th moves. (1) The corrective-notice sentence in Article III-F, Chapter Nine §5.6.1, CJS-5.125 §15, and Part C §5.6.4 used "notice window" with the CU categories, but the notice window belongs to the NU bands (CJS-5.110) and the CU categories use a containment window and a correction window (CJS-5.125 §15). It now cites both: everyone who relied on the information is told inside the notice window for the urgency of the harm (NU-1 to NU-3), and the unsafe product or wrong information is contained and corrected everywhere it appears inside the containment and correction windows for that urgency (CU-1 to CU-4). The Part C water walkthrough cites the notice window of NU-1 and the containment window of CU-1. The 133rd and 134th entries above keep their original wording as history. (2) CS-6.310 §9.5 (Type I): the health bullet now says "including allergy, dietary, and medication profiles", and the CJS-5.120 entry on product and service health and safety information says those profiles are Type I. No new type was added; Type N still applies where health data enables inference of internal states, and the more protective type applies. (3) New easy-entry brief E19 (*you rely on a label for what you eat, take, or give to someone*), with roster and how-common rows in the easy-entry README, a row in SCALE_NOTES (WHO 2026 foodborne-disease estimates; Gupta et al. 2019 for US adult food allergy), and a Pending row in the announcement review inventory. No Rights Floor text changed in this move other than the time-reference citation in Article III-F; the change names the same duty with the correct band terms and shortens no time limit. The sector file (notice times by product class) was not added.

**Hundred and thirty-sixth move (Article XI-B compelled-speech sentence settled; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session): resolve the open review of the compelled-speech bullet that the 133rd move added to Article XI-B. The first draft opened with an absolute bar ("No sentient may be made to state, endorse, or carry a message") and then put the same rule under highest scrutiny, which is not an absolute bar. XI-B already puts content-based restriction of expression under highest scrutiny (Highest Scrutiny, Chapter Five), so the bullet now uses that standard and says so plainly: a rule that makes a sentient state, endorse, or carry a message of opinion, belief, identity, or affiliation is a limit based on what is said, is presumed non-compliant, and faces highest scrutiny. One sentence was added so the line is not read too widely: verifiable facts, such as what a product contains or who is accountable for an act, are not messages of opinion, belief, identity, or affiliation. The Commercial Compliance wording is unchanged. Test 1 non-regression: the baseline said only that compelled speech is not protected expression under the Expression home, so the bullet still adds protection and removes none; see the updated `evidence/2026-10-25/product-safety-test-1-self-check.md`.

**Hundred and thirty-seventh move (forum family names capitalized; new audit rule; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; casing only, no rule changed): the six forum families are names, so the corpus now writes **Sentient Forums**, **Technical Forums**, **Institutional Forums**, **Environment Forums**, **Integrity Forums**, and **Constitutional Forums** (singular **Forum**) when the family is meant. Generic uses stay lowercase: forum family, lead forum, the forum, forum process. `tools/lexical_vocabulary_audit.py` gained `load-bearing-forum-family-casing`, beside the Rights Floor and Foundational Rights casing rules: it flags a family name followed by lowercase **forum** or **forums** outside link targets, inline code, and HTML ids, and has an internal self-test. One sweep of the binding corpus fixed 188 occurrences in 30 files, including headings and link text, so cite names still match their headings; anchors are unchanged because they are lowercase. This move log was swept too. Translations were not touched and will lag on casing until their next run. Derived files regenerated (`plain-terms-edition`, `boundary-chunks`, `print-pack`, `reader-accessibility`, `ai-corpus-sync`).

**Hundred and thirty-eighth move (Chapter Nine §6 wording and layout; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording and layout only, no rule changed; logged after the 137th): (1) the chart box "review challenge paths remain available" now reads "challenge paths stay open" in both the Part A and Part B charts. (2) The §6 plain-terms line and the Phase III paragraph say the evaluation work can run side by side but the record moves in order from review to decision to record, and that the contestability chain's routes are matched to where a dispute arises; "at each step" overstated §6.3, whose routes follow the level of the dispute, not the step. (3) In §6.1, the "What this section does" and "does not do" bullet lists were removed because the sentence above them, Chapter Thirteen, and §6.1.7 already say the same; the closing sentence now adds that no family absorbs work that belongs to another. (4) In §6.1.1, the "Where the seats are placed", "Outside the operator", and "Lead forum and record holder" paragraphs became bullets with the wording unchanged.

**Hundred and thirty-ninth move (Chapter Nine §6.1.2 health and safety information; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; logged after the 137th): **Rule effect:** Technical Forums supply component findings on health and safety information under Part A §5.6 and Article III-F (accuracy, evidence behind claims, kept current, and unknown or untested stated plainly). Technical adequacy only: accessibility, corrective notice, and harm-report handling stay with their existing owners. Before this move, Part B gave no forum family the Article III-F component and Chapter Thirteen has no routing for it. **Open:** Chapter Thirteen routing for Article III-F is still to be written. Layout only: the Sentience Status Adjudication bullet in §6.1.2 became a lead-in with three sub-bullets, wording unchanged.

**Hundred and fortieth move (Chapter Nine §6.1.5 Sentient Forums reworked; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; logged after the 137th): the old bullets gave Sentient Forums "stakeholder-impact and community-obligation" roles that no section defines, tied to "no institution is a necessary party", which is a dispute-routing test and rarely fits a certification pass. **Rule effects:** (1) Sentient Forums supply community-governance findings wherever a system materially affects how a community governs itself, whoever operates it. (2) Sentient Forums hold the Rights Floor component for community-level stakes; Institutional Forums hold the broader Rights Floor questions (§6.1.6); where no family holds it, the Integrity lead applies the Rights Floors. (3) If the two Rights Floor findings conflict, the Institutional Forum has the final say in that pass; both findings stay on the record and the conflict stays open to challenge under §6.3. (4) Sentient Forums hear challenges confined to the community-governance finding (the component forum path), receive community-level evidence as part of the participation duty of the assigned forums, and take up remedy and restoration among sentients after the pass, with no second merits track. Matching edits: the Sentient Forums line in §6.2 step 2, and the Other components bullet in Part D §12 (Sentient Forums supply the community-governance finding where the Institution's own conduct materially affects how a community governs itself; affected members and communities submit evidence through the assigned forums). The two "do not hold" bullets were dropped because §6.1.2 and §6.1.6 already say them. Part A line 383 needed no change.

**Hundred and forty-first move (Chapter Nine §6.1.6 and §6.1.7 plain wording; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording only, no rule changed): the §6.1.6 lead-in "Institutional mandate and Rights Floor questions when institutions are involved" now reads "Where an institution is involved, Institutional Forums:", matching the §6.1.5 lead-in. The custodian's draft ("Questions about Institutional mandates and Rights Floors are handled by Institutional Forums") was not used as written because, after the 140th move, Sentient Forums hold the community-level Rights Floor questions and the Integrity lead applies the Rights Floors where no family holds them; the condition and the full list of triggers stay in the second bullet. The first bullet, "Hold component authority for institutional mandate, supervised scope, classification, and institutional-duty compliance", now says the same thing in plain words: Institutional Forums "supply the component findings on what the institution is allowed and charged to do, the scope it is supervised under, how it is classified, and whether it is meeting its duties, when those questions arise in a certification". The two §6.1.7 bullets ("Keep assigned component authority for constitutional validity, meaning, class-wide structural remedy, and any other primary-stakes question…" and "The certification process must not let technical expertise or Integrity coordination displace those assigned merits roles") now say: Constitutional Forums and the other assigned families keep the questions Chapter Thirteen gives them (whether constitutional text is valid, what it means, whether a class-wide fix must restructure governance, and any other question whose main stake Chapter Thirteen gives to a family not named above), and technical expertise and Integrity coordination must not take those questions over. Same duties, plainer words.

**Hundred and forty-second move (Chapter Nine §6.1.7 and §6.1.2 certified questions named; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; no rule changed): §6.1.7 described the Constitutional family's role without saying how its questions reach it. It gained a bullet: Constitutional Forums answer the constitutional questions the lead forum certifies to them under Chapter Thirteen §5 (a question of validity, a structural question about restructuring governance, or a conflict among lower panels on a constitutional point), and until they answer the lead forum cannot treat that point as finally resolved. This restates Chapter Thirteen §5 (family-to-family escalation) and §6.2 step 5. §6.1.2 gained the matching bullet for Technical Forums: they answer the technical questions the lead forum certifies to them for the certification record, as Chapter Thirteen §4.2 allows.

**Hundred and forty-third move (Chapter Nine Part B §6.2 broken into subsections; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; layout only, no rule changed, no sentence added or dropped): §6.2 *Supervisory sequence* was already four blocks set off by bold lead-ins, so the bold lead-ins became third-level headings with anchors, as in §6.1: §6.2.1 *Challenge path first* (`621-challenge-path-first`), §6.2.2 *Default sequence* (`622-default-sequence`; the six steps), §6.2.3 *Urgent protection* (`623-urgent-protection`), §6.2.4 *Participation during review* (`624-participation-during-review`). The `62-supervisory-sequence` anchor is unchanged, so every existing link and every "§6.2 step N" cite still resolves. The §6 Trace subsection list names the four new subsections. Translations still carry the old layout.

**Hundred and forty-fourth move (Chapter Nine Part B §6.2.2 step 3 plain wording; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted; wording and layout only, no rule changed): step 3 of the default sequence was one dense paragraph. It now states the two limits on the lead forum (no authorizing the affected scope, and no removing or significantly loosening its conditions, until required component work is finished; "lift" became "remove or significantly loosen", and tightening a condition stays allowed) in plain words ahead of the existing list of required component work, then sets out two sub-bullets: limited operation needs a completed component finding that says why the permitted scope still protects the applicable Rights Floors and what remains to be verified; silence, missing findings, and unanswered objections never count as authorization. The four items of required component work were reworded in plain terms (a "disposition" is a decision; a "material" objection is one that could affect the outcome; a "certified" question is one the lead forum has sent to another forum for an answer, and the term is kept in parentheses). "Each component authority" in step 4 and in §6.1.1 was spelled out as the forum or authority that gives a component finding; step 4 now lists what the record states as separate items. The four governing cites in §6.2.3 became a bulleted list, with "Chapter Thirteen" repeated on both of its items. Every duty in the old text is kept.

**Hundred and forty-fifth move (Chapter Nine Part B review submissions log; 2026-10-25).** Decision (custodian, asked in session; uncommitted): §6.2.4 told the assigned forums to give affected sentients ways to submit evidence and concerns during review, but named no place where those submissions are logged, and §8.1 counted affected parties only "through a challenge path". Options weighed: widen the forum intake triage body (rejected: it is a per-family desk at filing, it must not decide merits, and six desks would mean six logs for one pass) or create a new office (rejected: it would need a seat placement and independence rules under Chapter Eight for a job the Record Seat already does). **Rule effects:** (1) New duty: the record seat holder keeps one review submissions log as part of the System Certification Record, logs every submission without judging relevance or weight, acknowledges it, sends it to the forum it bears on, and later enters what the forum did with it. (2) New duty: a forum that receives a submission passes it to the record seat holder inside the intake target of the Tier that matches the system's class. (3) New protection: a submitter may ask to be anonymous or confidential; identity is withheld from everyone, the operator included, until the lead forum decides; where confidentiality is warranted (as Protected Reporting (Whistleblowing) protects a reporter) it stays withheld; if not warranted, the submitter is told before any disclosure and may withdraw. Retaliation or interference is Protected Reporting Retaliation and Access Interference. (4) The log is public with the record; only a safety or security limit under §8.2 may keep an entry from view. (5) §8.1 "Concerns raised" now covers review submissions and records that a submitter is anonymous or confidential; a new §8.1 bullet lists the log. The custodian added that an operator with no interest in fixing problems already has an authenticity problem, so no operator-specific access limit was added; the willingness finding in §7.1 and the concealment test in §7.2 are unchanged. Open: the withdraw-if-not-warranted step was proposed in drafting, not stated by the custodian, and is for review. Translations still carry the old layout.

**Later addition (one new obligation, 2026-10-07).** With the record now written after the decision, §6.1 gained one required item, **Concerns raised in review and decision**: every objection, dissent, concern, and challenge raised in review or in reaching the outcome, with who raised it, how it was resolved or why it stays open, and any condition, reliance limit, or reopening trigger it produced. The Completeness paragraph and §5 say the same in one sentence each. This is the only operative change since the reorder; it is the one place where the conserved-obligations statement above no longer holds. Note: [evidence/2026-10-07/ch08_record_decision_concerns.md](evidence/2026-10-07/ch08_record_decision_concerns.md).

**One record throughout (2026-10-07).** The certification record is a single record opened at the start of Phase III (§4.2 step 1), kept through review and decision, and completed in Phase V (§6). It is not a working record that is later replaced. §4.2 step 4 is "Integration for decision" and step 6 hands off to §5 and §6; §6 says no separate record is made for the decision. This keeps the anti-bypass rule in §4.4 (no hidden sub-records) intact. The one-record rule is per pass: each recertification, and each reopened or fresh review, opens a new record linked to the completed one (§7, "New record for each pass"; §6.1 "Prior record"). A completed record is preserved and not edited in place.

**Why the challenge path moved to Operate (2026-10-08; clarified by the process-alignment pass below).** The ordinary published record challenge path opens when the outcome is published, before go-live; a system already operating has an open path, or opens it with its record. That opening rule does not imply that a proposed system has no affected parties before operation. Evaluation identifies actual and reasonably foreseeable effects and dependency; stakeholder evidence and the component, lead-integrity, and escalation paths inform review before publication. The contestability chain (Part B §4.3) stays with the forum process because it mirrors the supervisory sequence; its first step opens with the outcome for a system not yet operating, and steps 2 to 4 cover review.

**Process alignment (editor instruction, 2026-10-08).** The editor authorized implementation of the chart-and-text review. The six phases and section numbers remain. The chart separates permitted operation from protection and correction, shows independent verification in the return path, and uses one §8-gated standing connection covering current certification or facts arising during certification. Part B §5 distinguishes conditions before go-live, continuing operating conditions, and corrective work under bounded authorization; requires condition owners, clocks, independent verification, and consequences; and adds a forum-set notice interval and earliest permitted go-live for a first deployment or material expansion. Part B §6.1 gathers the operating plan on the same record, and §7 requires monitoring and timely response. These are substantive obligations, not only reader-aid changes. Part B §4 preserves required component findings while permitting a completed finding to support bounded conditional operation, and makes participation during review explicit. The transition exception and survival-essential protection remain applicable. Reader aids in Part A and the hub match. Evidence: [evidence/2026-10-08/ch08_process_alignment.md](evidence/2026-10-08/ch08_process_alignment.md).

**Rules going forward.**

- A new Chapter Nine section goes in the phase where the work it describes is done. Do not append it to the end of a file.
- The sub-phase labels in §3 (always, when implicated, when triggered) orient the reader. They do not narrow what a subsection requires, and the subsection's own trigger wording controls. Do not call them "tiers"; "tier" names the forum urgency tiers (Tier A/B/C/L/P). Name a scale "level" only with its kind (protection level, stewardship level, access level, severity level) and a definition scale "layer"; see `avoid-bare-level`.
- Reader aids (phase table, run sheet, record checklist, certification-status table) are non-operative widgets under NAV-READER-06. Where an aid and a section differ, the section controls.
- The §1.1 chart, the phase tables in `core_09.000_system_alignment_certification_evaluation.md` (§1.2), the run sheet in `corpus_systems/cs_09.315_system_alignment_certification_run_sheet.md`, and the §3 sub-phase table move together (VIS-CHART-SYNC-03 applies by analogy).
- Publication, the go-live gate, and the operation/correction branches must match Part B §5. Conditions and the operating plan remain on the one certification record under §6.1; each later review uses a new linked record under §7. A failed or refused certification does not imply permission to operate.

**Evidence.** [evidence/2026-10-07/ch08_process_order_reorder.md](evidence/2026-10-07/ch08_process_order_reorder.md) holds the full map, verification, and open items. The rewrite tools are `tools/ch8_process_order_reorder.py` (first move) and `tools/ch8_record_after_decide.py` (second move); the second move's note is [evidence/2026-10-07/ch08_record_after_decide.md](evidence/2026-10-07/ch08_record_after_decide.md).

Gate: manual for placement; links, anchors, and section-cite names are held by the audits in the registry row above.

### Adopted implementation subfile anatomy (NAV-IMPL-SUBFILE-01)

Every substantive `CJS` / `CS` / `CI` / `CF` subfile opens the way a numbered `core_*` file does, so a reader arriving from a citation lands on a title and a sentence rather than on collapsed routing widgets.

**Visible stack (in this order):**

1. `<a id="xx-n"></a>` and an `#` H1 carrying the stable family ID and a title — `# CF-12: Forum continuity`.
2. Collapsed **Corpus placement** widget: layer, binding status, and where to start reading.
3. One-line **owner sentence** in operative position — `This file is the forum implementation home for **CF-12**.`
4. One file-level collapsed **Trace**, then **Definitions · Assessment · Compliance** where the file invokes Chapter Five terms.
5. One-line `*In plain terms:*` gloss for the file.
6. `##` sections, each with an `<a id="...">` anchor, then its own `*In plain terms:*` gloss before the operative prose.

**Section-level widgets are the exception, not the default.** A section-level **Trace** survives only where it carries routing that cannot be derived from the file-level block — chiefly a `Topic routing (primary owner):` bullet that `make router-bidirectional-audit` requires inside the owning section slice. A section-level **DAC** survives only where that section invokes Chapter Five terms beyond the file-level set. Three line shapes are prohibited outright, because they state nothing a reader could not already see:

- `- Downstream: this section's local operational requirements for **X**.` where `X` is the enclosing section;
- `- Read with: **X**.` naming only the enclosing section;
- an `- Upstream:` line byte-identical to the file-level Trace.

**Owner sentence rules (2026-10-20).**

- Registry annexes (`cf_00`, `ci_00`, `cs_00`, `cjs_00`) carry the same sentence for their `-0` family. It sits after the file-top widgets, because the widget-top audit does not allow operative prose before them.
- Part files use the same opener: `This file is the systems implementation home for **CS-2** (*…*), **Part A**. It owns **…**.`
- When one family number spans several files, each file says which part it holds and points to the others: CJS-1 across `cjs_01`, `cjs_04`, `cjs_05`, and `cjs_06`; CJS-3 between the hub and the operations files; CJS-3.3 between `cjs_03o` and `cjs_03u`.
- The names in the sentence are the heading names. A sentence that names an ID the file does not hold, or gives it another section's name, is a defect. CJS-1.7 and CJS-1.9 carried another section's name in 11 places and were corrected.
- A cited ID must carry its name (**CORPUS-REF-NAME-01**), so the sentence points at files, or names each section in full, and does not use bare ID ranges.
- A file that is reader guidance only says so in its opening sentence (`cjs_reader_guide.md` does).

**Gate (2026-10-20):** `make owner-line-audit` also reads every CJS, CS, CI, and CF subfile: the visible sentence comes before the first `##` heading, its layer word and ID prefix match the folder, every ID in its first sentence is a heading ID in the same file, and no two files claim the same ID and Part letter. `cjs_reader_guide.md` and `cf_13.475_sentience_status_record.md` are the two listed exceptions (`IMPL_EXEMPT`), each with the sentence it must keep.

**Heading grammar is one language.** CI, CF, CJS, and CS family files all use `## XXI-n.m Title` (example: `## CS-7.3 Mandatory validation record`). Cite that ID as **CS-7.3**, not **CS-7 §3**. CS files keep the older `<a id="1-purpose-and-role">` slugs as compatibility aliases beside the `cs-n-m-…` ids.

**Heading depth carries no meaning.** Markdown slugs derive from heading text, not depth, so promoting `### CF-12.1 Rights-Floor continuity rule` to `##` leaves `#cf-121-rights-floor-continuity-rule` unchanged and breaks no inbound reference. Prefer an explicit `<a id="...">` above the heading so the link survives a later rewording; `make architecture-index` regenerates the stable-ID index from these.

**Domain-interface local-duty pattern (CI-15–CI-25).** Domain CI files do not restate CJS-3, Chapter Five, or Chapter Six floors as if they originated here. After the file gloss they use:

1. **Quick orientation** — short bullets for what this file is for.
2. *Shared rules live elsewhere.* — pointers only (CJS-3 clusters, Chapter Five terms, Chapter Six articles, sibling CI machinery).
3. **Apply** / a **local owner-duty map** — what this institution must name, file, and maintain.

CS-5–CS-13 and CF specialist / continuity files use a lighter version of the same split: one purpose sentence, then what this file adds versus what it cites.

**Reference shapes:** [CI-15](corpus_institutions/ci_06.220_neurodiversity_disability_justice_trauma_informed_participation.md) for the domain local-duty pattern; [CS-2 Part A](corpus_systems/cs_06.305_information_types_and_handling.md) for numbered sections with anchors; [CS-4](corpus_systems/cs_14.305_critical_system_stewardship.md) and [CS-9](corpus_systems/cs_06.335_resource_allocation_funding_stewardship.md) for files rebuilt from unstructured prose.

**Supporting tools** (one-shot migration helpers, not gates): `tools/prune_adopted_implementation_scaffolding.py`, `tools/promote_cs_topic_labels.py`, `tools/add_section_anchors.py`, `tools/collapse_pronoun_chains.py`, `tools/debold_shouting_prose.py`, `tools/insert_section_glosses.py`. Wording changes to operative text are gated on `tools/obligation_inventory_diff.py` (`make obligation-snapshot` before, `make obligation-diff` after).

<a id="plain-language-vocabulary-guardrails"></a>
### Plain-Language Vocabulary Guardrails

Capitalize **Constitutional Tetrad**, **Two Constitutional Aims**, **Flourishing**, **Continuity** (constitutional aim sense), **material stake**, **Wellbeing**, **Safety**, **Truth**, **Rights Floor**, **Foundational Rights**, **Constitutional Contract Layer**, and **Stakeholder System Participation** when they name constitutional layers. Do not use `cure` (or `cured`, `uncured`, `curing`, `curable`) anywhere in the binding corpus: prefer **resolve**, **resolved**, or **resolution** for the clock and deadline sense, and **fix**, **correct**, or **settle** for the remedy sense (`make lexical-vocabulary-audit`, rule `avoid-cure-family`). Avoid bare **drift** for stewardship, governance, incentive, or alignment divergence — prefer **misalignment** or **constitutional misalignment**; retain **anti-drift**, **classification drift**, **version drift**, and other established custody or certification compounds. Forum/standing boundary gloss: a **filed case** is not standing by itself — prefer **case** over **claim** in that contrast. Names for the scope instruments: (1) Chapter Five **System Charter** — published scope instrument of one system ([core_05.040_band_continuity.md#system-charter](core_05.040_band_continuity.md#system-charter)); (2) Chapter Five **Institutional Charter** — published scope instrument of an institution that runs more than one system, holding their System Charters and the reason it runs them ([core_05.040_band_continuity.md#institutional-charter](core_05.040_band_continuity.md#institutional-charter)); (3) Chapter Five **Constitutional Charter** — polity-authorization instrument, as in the Chapter Fourteen **treaty, compact, or Constitutional Charter** family ([core_05.040_band_continuity.md#constitutional-charter](core_05.040_band_continuity.md#constitutional-charter)); (4) verb forms such as **hold a charter over** / legacy **supervise, charter, or …** — authorizing control. Do not use a bare Charter for any of the three instruments: name which one. Do not use a bare charter for the Constitution or Corpus itself. Prefer **Preamble** over **Chapter Zero** / **Chapter 00** when naming [`core_00.000_preamble.md`](core_00.000_preamble.md). Avoid bare **pathway** / **pathways** in every sense — each use must say which kind of pathway it is. In the standing/privilege sense prefer **named pathway**, **named privilege pathway**, or a type prefix from Chapter Eleven §4.2 (`trust`, `role`, `authority`, `credit`, `oversight`, `recognition`, `stewardship`, `governance-voting`, `stakeholder-participation`); for process routes prefer **challenge**, **review**, **appeal**, **remedy**, **redress**, **contest**, **dispute-resolution**, **participation**, or **self-service** pathways; for causal analysis prefer **causal**, **harm**, **risk**, or **interaction** pathways. Generic adjectives (*accessible*, *independent*, *credible*, *paper*) and determiners do not qualify; `pathway-…` prefix compounds (`pathway-specific`, `pathway-scoped`) take the qualifier too (`named-pathway-specific`). Enforced by `avoid-bare-pathway` in `tools/lexical_vocabulary_audit.py`. Do **not** invent **clearance pathway** or **lock pathway** as house terms (clearance and locks open or close a named pathway). Avoid **named-pathway result**: state whether a **specific role, authority, or access** is open, limited, or blocked, and name the **verified reason**. Leave already-qualified families alone: **Capture of Resolution Pathways**; challenge / review / appeal / remedy / redress / contest pathways; restorative / restoration pathways; causal or system compounds (`harm pathway`, `reward pathway`, and similar); and established titles such as **Role-Depth and Material-Responsibility Pathways**. **Governance layers:** name **Constitutional Contract Layer** (who may govern, by what **legitimacy mechanism**, on what durable terms) vs **Stakeholder System Participation** (voice inside already-authorized systems). Canonical split: [Preamble §3.3](core_00.000_preamble.md#33-governance-layers). Avoid **stakeholder governance**, **constitutional governance** as layer labels, **governance layer mechanism**, and **stakeholder-layer**. Keep finding-profile codes **CCL** / **SSP** / **INT** as audit shorthand; keep **governance-layer discipline** as the Preamble §3.3 nickname. Full tables: [tools/architecture/lexical_guardrails.json](tools/architecture/lexical_guardrails.json). Forum vocabulary: [.cursor/rules/sentient-constitution.mdc](.cursor/rules/sentient-constitution.mdc). Reader briefs in [`implementation/adoption/easy_entry/`](implementation/adoption/easy_entry/) have a **scoped** jargon list (`easy_entry_jargon` in that JSON; `make easy-entry-jargon-audit`); do not import those terms into the global vocabulary table.

### Time-window names (TIME-NAME-01)

A time window is named by its kind of time. The last word of the name says which kind.

| Last word | Kind of time | Current names |
|---|---|---|
| **window** | Time allowed to act, counted from a trigger | response window, resolution window, restore-challenge window, prompt-review window, containment window, correction window, notice window, declaration window, transition window, tier window, host replacement window |
| **target** | A non-binding stage goal inside a window | stage target, intake target |
| **bound** | The longest a measure may last; it lapses on its own | tier outer bound, hold lapse date |
| **term** | The fixed life of an order, office, or grant | order term |
| **interval** | The length between recurring checks | certification interval, provisional interval |
| **period** | Time during which a rule applies or something is watched | cooling-off period, watch period, retention period |
| **span** | The time a record covers | record span |

Rules:

- **Clock is not a name.** Use it only in plain idiom ("the applicable clock", "published clocks") and for how a window runs (starts, keeps running, stalls). The retired names are tier clock, response clock, restore-challenge clock, transition clock, recertification clock, and resolution clock.
- **Cadence is not a length.** *Review cadence* stays as the name of the Chapter Thirteen rule that sets the intervals. The lengths are the certification interval, the provisional interval, the audit midpoint, and the audit advance allowance.
- **"Response window" means the Chartered Party's window to answer only** (Chapter Seven §1.6 and §6; CI-13.3). It takes the length of the matching tier's intake target, and the **resolution window** takes the matching tier's outer bound. Neither is the time to begin review of a hold or an order (the **prompt-review window**) or the time to finish a matter (the **tier window**: a tier's stage targets and its outer bound together).
- **Promptly is not a length.** Where owner text says promptly and names no window, it states a duty of diligence. It implies no tier, level, or window, and it does not excuse delay. It is the sibling of "Cadence is not a length". The three uses that stay open on purpose are the Article V Timeliness aim, the Chapter Ten Diligence credit, and the Chapter Twelve Part A good-faith drafting carve-out; the TIME-REF-01 registry lists the first and the last in `qualitative_on_purpose`, each with its reason.
- **Prompt review is a window.** Where text requires a measure, order, hold, stay, or interim decision to be reviewed promptly, the time to begin review is the **prompt-review window**: the intake-target length of the matching tier, counted as a compliance line (Chapter Thirteen §6 *Prompt review*). Prompt intake and preservation mean the intake target. Prompt notice means notice with the measure or inside the restore-challenge window.
- **"Reasonable" periods are the Tier B outer bound.** Where owner text asks for a "reasonable" period or timeframe (Articles II-E and XVIII-C, CS-3, CS-4, CS-5, CS-9), it means 14 days, the Tier B outer bound (the forum response time for a Tier B matter), whatever class or tier applies. It is a ceiling, and no new window name is created.
- **Never use the word `cure`.** The 30-day window is the resolution window (see Plain-Language Vocabulary Guardrails).
- **Stage targets are not compliance lines.** The tier outer bound is. A stage that runs past its target is not itself non-compliant (Chapter Thirteen §6).
- **Units.** Calendar days unless the text says otherwise. A week is seven days. No business days.
- **Dated move logs keep the wording they used.** The audit skips this file.

The list of names and their owners is [CJS-0.4](corpus_joint_structure/cjs_00.105_registry_and_reading_rules.md#cjs-0105-4-time-window-names). It is non-operative and carries no lengths, so the owner files stay the only clock table. This rule governs wording only.

### Names for bodies that verify, hold, and file (TERM-BODY-01)

A body is named by the job it does on an act, and the last word of the name says which job.

| Last word | Job | Current names |
|---|---|---|
| **authority** | Verifies, authorizes, or decides a question for others, and gives reasons | record-opening authority, Backup Record Authority, restricted information authority, verifying authority, records-recognition authority |
| **custodian** | Enters, holds, and versions a record; never verifies it | record custodian |
| **registrar** | Takes in a filing or result, checks that it is whole, gives a receipt, and keeps the register; decides nothing and grants no permission | Institutional registrar, local registrar, Integrity registrar |

Rules:

- **Verifier is not a name.** A body or sentient in the verify-or-authorize seat is a *verifying authority*. The word is banned in the binding text, the implementation files, the guides, and the front doors (`make lexical-vocabulary-audit`, rule `avoid-verifier`). The contributor role *Register verifier* in CONTRIBUTING.md is a project role and keeps its name.
- **Office is a unit or a post, not a job.** Use *office* for a unit inside a body or the holder of a post (office-holder, an office in the Material Control Line, the offices a Charter Role Map places). Do not use it as the name of a body that verifies or decides; say *authority*. This is not machine-checked, because the word has many legitimate uses.
- **Authority is also power.** "Published authority to rely on the record" and "governing authority" use the word for power. When one sentence needs both, call the body a *body* and keep *authority* for the role or the power: an independent body with published authority to rely on the record acts as the verifying authority.
- **A registrar stays a registrar, and a custodian stays a custodian.** A registrar is a clerk by design (CF-5.5, CF-5.6) and is not renamed an authority.

### Audit registry and report (AUDIT-REGISTRY-01)

`make regression` is the gate. It is one flat list, and each target prints its own command and directory lines. `make audit-report` runs the same targets quietly and prints one row per category, then expands only the targets that failed (the failing lines, the lines under them, and the command to re-run). On a green run the whole report is a dozen lines.

Before running audits, the report refreshes the five AI corpus indexes whenever `ai-manifest-validate` is selected. All candidate outputs are generated and validated first; only missing, invalid, or stale files are replaced, ignoring generation timestamps.
